# Galaxy native development foundation

This is **not a playable PC port**. Native disc access, texture decoding, partial
model reading, input-provider adapters and a Direct3D texture preview now work.
There is no gameplay renderer, audio backend, host game-window launcher,
functioning title screen, settings menu, or co-op yet.

## Implemented

- A real Windows x64 C++ executable, built without emulation.
- Five original Petari translation units compiled directly from the sibling
  checkout: Nerve, Spine, NerveExecutor, ActorStateKeeper, ActorStateBase.
- A fixed-width host type shim; original source files are unchanged.
- State-machine behavioral tests for transition timing, callbacks and state lifecycle.
- Read-only single-disc, single-file WBFS block mapping and partition inspection.
- WBFS tests for cross-block reads, sparse data, invalid ranges, malformed headers,
  bad mappings, unsupported multi-disc files and truncation.
- Native retail Wii partition decryption using Windows CNG AES-CBC, with H0
  SHA-1 payload checks on every cluster read. No emulator process is used.
- Checked filesystem table parsing and lookup by the game's asset paths.
- Yaz0 decompression and RARC/U8 archive indexing and resource lookup.
- Individual resource extraction to new files; existing files are never overwritten.
- Crypto known-answer tests, corrupt cluster rejection, malformed filesystem,
  compressed-stream, archive-cycle and extent tests.
- Base-level GX texture decoding: I4, I8, IA4, IA8, RGB565, RGB5A3, RGBA8,
  C4, C8, C14X2 and CMPR. Palette lookup, tile clipping and alpha are checked.
- TPL/BTI readers and embedded TEX1/BTI textures in J3D2 BMD3/BDL4 models.
- J3D section indexing, position array decoding, and joint/shape/material header
  counts. Mesh topology, joint transforms and material interpretation are pending.
- Native Direct3D 11 texture upload, shaders, readback, and a resizable Win32
  preview window with aspect-preserving scaling. This does not execute game logic.

## Verified graphics milestone (2026-09-28)

A full archive scan decoded **1,758 model containers**, **2,865,919 positions**,
**8,035 base texture occurrences** and **167,463,744 pixels**. These counts include
resources wherever they occur; they are not a deduplicated asset count.
Mario's model contains 2,587 positions and 22 base textures; its headers report
30 joints, nine shapes and nine materials.

The original 440x256 title logo texture was decoded and visually inspected.
Direct3D hardware and WARP render readback each matched all **112,640 pixels**
against a CPU compositing reference, with one channel value of rounding tolerance.
A hidden native preview window passed swap-chain creation, paint and resize tests.
This verifies a static textured draw, not Wii GX material emulation, gameplay,
8K performance, frame pacing or 120/240 FPS support.

Run `../Preview title texture.cmd` for the interactive native preview. Close the
window or press Escape to exit. The checkerboard represents texture transparency;
this single texture is not the game's complete title-screen composition.

## Verified asset milestone (2026-09-28)

The supplied RMGE01 image was successfully indexed: **2,371 files**.
All **2,232 .arc archives** were decrypted, decompressed where applicable, and
parsed: **25,108 resources**, **215,305,696 stored archive bytes**. The run
performed **7,234 cluster reads with successful H0 checks** (this is a count of
reads, not necessarily unique clusters).

Native resource lookup also extracted the real Mario and Luigi J3D2/BDL4 models
(220,416 and 227,136 bytes) and title logo TPL texture (225,344 bytes) into
`build/sample-assets`. These are data for future renderer work, not rendered
characters or a functioning title screen.

This validates the archive containers and accessed payloads. It does not validate
all model/animation/texture internals, non-archive disc files, the complete hash
hierarchy, ticket signatures, or full-game behavior. H0 checks detect payload
corruption; they are not disc authenticity verification.

## Build and run

Open PowerShell in this directory and run `./build.ps1`.
The portable Zig 0.16.0 compiler is in `../tools`; its downloaded archive was
checked against the SHA-256 from the official Zig download index.

```
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --self-test
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --inspect "D:/Desktop/Super Mario Galaxy (USA, Canada) (En,Fr,Es).wbfs"
./verify-assets.ps1
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --archive "D:/Desktop/Super Mario Galaxy (USA, Canada) (En,Fr,Es).wbfs" /ObjectData/Mario.arc
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --extract-resource "D:/Desktop/Super Mario Galaxy (USA, Canada) (En,Fr,Es).wbfs" /ObjectData/Mario.arc /mario.bdl new-mario.bdl
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --model ./build/sample-assets/mario.bdl
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --decode-texture ./build/sample-assets/titlelogo.tpl new-preview.bmp
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --preview-texture ./build/sample-assets/titlelogo.tpl
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --render-test ./build/sample-assets/titlelogo.tpl new-readback.bmp
./build/galaxy-probe.exe --preview-smoke ./build/sample-assets/titlelogo.tpl
./verify-graphics.ps1
```

`--inspect` remains a metadata-only command. `--list-assets`, `--archive`,
`--verify-archives` and `--extract-resource` use the native decryption path.
The loader rejects split WBFS files, containers with multiple occupied slots,
non-retail common-key indexes and unsupported disc encryption layouts. Asset
reads and Yaz0 output are capped at 256 MiB per resource/archive.
CMakeLists.txt is also provided, but the verified build path is build.ps1.

`verify-assets.ps1` writes `build/asset-index.tsv`, `build/archive-verification.tsv`
and their corresponding logs. `build/build-log.txt` records the latest build/tests.
`verify-graphics.ps1` writes a graphics resource manifest and summary log.
`build/renderer-test-log.txt` and `build/preview-smoke-log.txt` record rendering checks.

## Native loader interfaces

`WiiAssets::readFile("/ObjectData/Mario.arc")` returns decrypted archive bytes.
`Rarc` accepts Yaz0-wrapped or uncompressed RARC and U8 data;
`Rarc::readFile("/mario.bdl")` returns the resource bytes. These readers are
single-threaded and use explicit big-endian parsing, never native struct overlays.
They are not yet wired into Petari's Wii DVD/JKR runtime.

`decodeModel` returns positions, embedded textures and section metadata; it does
not yet return a drawable skinned mesh. `decodeTpl` and `decodeBti` return RGBA
pixels. Only the base mip is decoded. `TextureRenderer` consumes those pixels
without an emulator and can render offscreen or into a native window.

Format references: Petari's `JKRArchive.hpp`/`JKRDecomp.cpp`, plus Dolphin's
[Wii partition reader](https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Source/Core/DiscIO/VolumeWii.cpp),
[ticket definitions](https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Source/Core/Core/IOS/ES/Formats.h)
and [retail interoperability constants](https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Source/Core/Core/IOS/IOSC.cpp).
The native loader uses Windows cryptography rather than compiling Dolphin code.
Texture layout references also include Petari's `ResTIMG` definition and Dolphin's
[texture decoder](https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Source/Core/VideoCommon/TextureDecoder_Generic.cpp).
The renderer uses the [Windows Direct3D 11 API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/d3d11/nf-d3d11-d3d11createdevice).

## Source baseline

Petari: https://github.com/SMGCommunity/Petari

Commit: `0d9b80c569b954562c80206113e6936378c90c27`

Upstream code retains its license in `../Petari/LICENSE`. No changes have been
submitted upstream.

## Boot blockers and implementation order

1. Asset pipeline: disc decryption, filesystem, archives, compression and base
   textures are implemented. J3D positions and texture sections are decoded.
   Next decode shape display lists, joint hierarchy, skinning, materials and
   animation, then connect the host asset service to game startup. The supplied image is
   RMGE01; upstream's matching build targets RMGK01. Runtime asset compatibility
   and region-dependent behavior still need investigation.
2. Host runtime: replace Wii OS threads, synchronization, timers, heaps, DVD I/O,
   ARAM and save storage with host implementations. GameSystem.cpp's main()
   immediately calls these APIs.
3. Graphics: translate GX state, textures, materials and display lists into a PC
   graphics backend. MainLoopFramework and GameSystem GX initialization cannot
   be linked against the original hardware implementation on x64.
4. Audio and input: the KPAD/WPAD adapter and keyboard/mouse frame pump exist,
   but the host launcher must bind the game window and a rebind UI/controller
   validation remain. Replace Wii DSP/audio services. See
   [`INPUT-ROADMAP.md`](INPUT-ROADMAP.md) for mouse-to-Wii-pointer mapping,
   keyboard/controller shake rebinding, settings UI, and acceptance checks.
5. ABI/portability: replace PowerPC assembly and intrinsics, inspect pointer casts,
   packed layouts and byte order; compile increasingly broad groups of actual
   game sources. Wii layout comments do not describe native x64 structure sizes.
6. Boot logo/title/save selection, then validate a complete first level at original
   simulation timing before extending coverage to the whole game.
7. Resolution menu through 7680x4320. Separate presentation timing from simulation;
   audit frame-dependent gameplay and animation before promising 120/240 FPS.
   Faster simulation is not a valid implementation of high frame rate support.
8. Only after stable single-player: add a second actor (Luigi), separate cameras,
   shared world progression, network authority/synchronization, loading transitions,
   saves, cutscenes, deaths and reconnect handling. The co-op menu should offer
   Host Game and Join Game for P2P and LAN sessions. Preserve independent camera
   views and exploration; when player 1 starts a cutscene or talks to an NPC,
   bring player 2 to player 1 before the shared sequence.
9. Treat Download Play as a separate browser-hosted mode: relaunch/open a browser
   session, let the host create a room and share its URL/address, and let a client
   join without an installed game image. Transfer only the required assets with
   integrity checks and a visible loading/progress screen. This mode, its web
   transport, asset packaging and security boundaries are design requirements,
   not implemented features.

No delivery date or full-game compatibility is established by this foundation.

## Current campaign-provider checkpoint (2026-10-11)

### Mouse pointer and Wii Remote shake input

The Windows keyboard/mouse sampler now runs once at the beginning of
`GameSystem::frameLoop()`. `nativeBindGameInputWindow(HWND)` supplies the game
window handle; while that window is foreground, client-area mouse motion is
mapped to the Wii pointer and left-click is mapped to the pointer trigger/Wii B.
The generated GameSystem source is reproducible from
`generate-native-game-system.ps1`. The host must still create/bind a real game
window before entering `nativeOriginalGameMain`; this project does not yet have
that launcher or a bootable game runtime.

Validation: `test-kpad-input.ps1` passes coordinate/viewport, motion/reacquisition,
button-edge, KPAD and virtual-WPAD checks. Q and XInput X now default to the
shake bind; `nativeSetGameInputShakeBindings` can change the virtual key and
XInput button mask. A new press creates one synthetic acceleration impulse in
the original KPAD acceleration stream, with held-input debounce. The settings
menu for changing these binds is not implemented, and controller hardware has
not been tested. `test-original-wpad-button.ps1` also sends the mapped impulse
through Petari's WPadAcceleration and WPadHVSwing and verifies a single swing
trigger, held-input suppression and release/rearm. No full-game link, boot or
play session is verified; end-to-end completion remains 0%.

The focused original MapObj compile sweep passes 316/317 units. The one failing
unit, `SpinDriverPathDrawer`, writes directly to the Wii-only `GXWGFifo` register.
The duplicate-free link audit includes 1,248 original providers (632 primary,
64 NPC, 21 AreaObj, 110 Enemy, 169 Boss and 252 additional MapObj providers) and
reports 722 unresolved symbol occurrences / 719 unique symbols. The audit still
fails to link an executable; graphics/platform, UI and DSP/audio services remain
among the missing dependencies. `native/build.ps1` and its diagnostic self-tests
pass, but they are not a game launch. No title, level or campaign has been
verified, so end-to-end project completion remains 0%.

## Host input and audio plan (partially implemented)

- Map mouse coordinates to the game's Wii IR/pointer coordinates in the active
  viewport. Account for aspect-preserving letterboxing, window/fullscreen size,
  DPI scaling and pointer clamping; define off-screen behavior and a calibration
  path so aiming remains consistent across display modes. Preserve pointer
  movement, visibility and off-screen semantics expected by the game's cursor
  interactions, including menus and star-pointer gameplay.
- Bind mouse buttons to pointer-trigger actions used by star-pointer interactions
  and menus. The Windows KPAD path now maps left-click to the Wii B/pointer trigger,
  distinct from pointer motion and Mario movement; additional button bindings and
  input-menu rebinding remain to be done.
- Expose Wii Remote shake as a configurable action that can be bound to keyboard
  keys and game-controller buttons/axes. Route a deliberate press/gesture through
  the same gameplay shake event the game expects, with configurable threshold and
  debounce so a held key/button does not generate accidental repeated shaking;
  allow rebinding from the input options menu.
- Add an input-settings UI for pointer sensitivity/calibration and key/controller
  rebinding; retain actual Wii Remote input as an optional device path if supported.
- Add an audio output mode for Stereo and 5.1 Surround. Offer 5.1 only when the
  selected host output supports it; otherwise fall back to stereo and preserve
  sensible channel downmixing.
- Verify coordinate mapping at multiple aspect ratios and DPI scales, off-screen
  handling, mouse-trigger behavior, key/button shake routing and repeat behavior,
  plus stereo/5.1 channel mapping and unsupported-device fallback. Keep hardware
  DSP replacement and audio-device routing as separate tasks.

Current input implementation: Native KPAD accepts four channels, emits button
edges/repeat, retains Wii calibration setter values, and includes a Windows
keyboard/mouse sampler. A virtual WPAD channel-0 freestyle remote reports mouse
pointer support, queues controller-info callbacks, and tracks host-window attach
state. Host-generated pointer coordinates are already normalized, so Wii DPD
filtering/calibration values are retained for API compatibility but are not
applied to these samples. Left click maps to Wii B. Q and XInput X create a
debounced shake impulse consumed by the original WPadAcceleration/WPadHVSwing
code; code-level rebinding exists, but no input-settings menu or physical
controller test is available. The sampler is called from the GameSystem frame
loop, but a host launcher must still create and bind the actual game window.
This is not yet a verified playable-game control path.

## Current campaign-provider checkpoint (2026-10-11 addendum)

The latest link-only original-provider audit includes 1,341 compiled providers,
including the native KPAD/WPAD/input adapters, host NAND/banner services, GX
draw-sync callback and native GX texture-mode commands, plus the existing
language/GX depth-peek providers and generated host-endian BCSV data. It reports
595 unresolved occurrences / 593 unique symbols, zero duplicate definitions,
and still fails to produce an executable. KPAD, WPAD, NAND save, draw-sync and
the GX texture scale/sync commands now resolve;
deferred WPAD info requests return a no-controller callback
if the virtual device disconnects before delivery. The Windows sampler is called
once per GameSystem frame, but no host launcher currently binds a real game
window. These checks do not establish game boot or campaign execution. Input
mapping and Petari's original shake detector pass focused tests; viewport/DPI
calibration, off-screen gameplay validation, a settings UI for rebinds, physical
controller validation and Stereo/5.1 remain unfinished. Focused
`test-gx-texture-mode.ps1` verifies the exact BP register IDs, scale values,
disable semantics and texture-mode sync encoding. Core GX startup/state services,
including GXInit, remain unimplemented. End-to-end completion remains 0%.

### Gravity-provider link update (2026-10-11)

Added the unchanged original `GravityCreator.cpp`, seven gravity-shape providers
(`ConeGravity`, `CubeGravity`, `DiskGravity`, `DiskTorusGravity`,
`ParallelGravity`, `SegmentGravity`, and `WireGravity`), plus
`GraviryFollower.cpp` and `GravityDust.cpp` to the repeatable source/link audit.
All ten focused source compiles pass. The latest audit includes 1,356 providers,
reports 564 undefined-symbol occurrences / 562 unique symbols, and has zero
duplicate definitions. No gravity-named undefined symbols remain in the link
report; the probe still fails to produce an executable on unrelated audio/DSP
and other runtime dependencies. This is link evidence only, not gravity gameplay
or normal game boot. End-to-end completion remains 0%.

### VI dimming and idle activity follow-up (2026-10-11)

The native VI provider now implements Petari's `VIEnableDimming` and
`VIGetDimmingCount` APIs. The countdown advances on host retraces, uses the
original 18,000-frame default or 15,000-frame PAL threshold, pauses while
dimming is disabled, and resets when the Windows input sampler detects
keyboard/controller activity or pointer motion. This gives the original audio
screen-saver process a meaningful idle countdown; it does not implement Wii
display dimming or establish audio playback. `test-vi-port.ps1`,
`test-kpad-input.ps1`, and `test-original-wpad-button.ps1` pass. The fresh
link-only audit still exits 1, but the provider set resolves the VI dimming
symbols: 1,341 providers, 593 unresolved occurrences / 591 unique symbols,
zero duplicates. No executable or game session is produced; end-to-end
completion remains 0%.

### Immediate GX vertex stream and SpinDriverPathDrawer follow-up (2026-10-11)

The original `SpinDriverPathDrawer` provider now compiles through a generated
host copy that replaces its Wii MMIO FIFO writes with the same big-endian
position/texture-coordinate bytes in the active GD display list. The native
writer checks for an active list and reserves the full 20-byte vertex before
writing. `test-gx-immediate-vertex.ps1` verifies exact XYZ/ST bytes, missing-list
rejection and overflow atomicity; the focused original actor compile passes.
The authoritative link-only audit now includes the actor and serializer: 1,343
providers, 585 unresolved occurrences / 583 unique symbols, zero duplicates.
The audit still fails and creates no executable; this is graphics-command
serialization and symbol integration evidence, not a rendered scene or boot.
End-to-end completion remains 0%.

The same audit also now includes the host-compatible original fog-color helper,
with `MR::calcFogStartEnd` remaining owned by Petari's original `ModelUtil.cpp`
to avoid duplicate definitions. `TDDraw::mixFogColor` resolves. Latest status:
1,344 providers, 584 unresolved occurrences / 582 unique symbols, zero
duplicates. The unresolved audio/DSP, GX startup/state, layout and platform
dependencies still prevent a game executable; end-to-end completion remains 0%.

Two more original single-player helpers now compile and link: `ActiveActorList`
for actor-list lifecycle and `AnimationRandomPlayer` for original animation
selection. Their focused provider compile passes. Latest link-only status is
1,346 providers, 578 unresolved occurrences / 576 unique symbols, zero
duplicates. This verifies compilation and symbol integration only; neither
provider has been exercised in a game session, and the link still produces no
executable. End-to-end completion remains 0%.

Two more original single-player helpers now compile and link: `ActiveActorList`
for actor-list lifecycle and `AnimationRandomPlayer` for original animation
selection. Their focused provider compile passes. Latest link-only status is
1,346 providers, 578 unresolved occurrences / 576 unique symbols, zero
duplicates. This verifies compilation and symbol integration only; neither

## Verified skeletal-data milestone (2026-10-03)

The native rig reader now decodes JNT1 local scales, rotations and translations,
INF1 joint parentage, EVP1 skinning envelopes and referenced inverse-bind matrices,
and DRW1 draw-matrix references. All 1,758 model containers in the graphics scan
passed this additional validation. Mario loads with 30 joints, 13 envelopes and
48 draw matrices.

Hierarchy tests cover parentage, transforms, duplicate joints, invalid remaps,
unbalanced nesting and non-finite transforms. Actual-disc validation also found
unused inverse-bind slots containing non-finite exporter padding. Only matrices
referenced by skinning envelopes are decoded; inverseBindPresent records usable
slots and prevents treating unused slots as meaningful matrices.

This does not yet calculate animated joint/world matrices, transform mesh
vertices, interpret material shaders or draw character geometry. Next decode
SHP1 packets and indexed GX primitives, then apply the rig to their vertices.
A one-minute continuation was enabled for this chat at the user's request.

## Verified mesh-packet milestone (2026-10-03)

SHP1 shape remaps, vertex descriptors, packet matrix palettes and display lists
now decode into triangle connectivity. The reader checks position-array indices,
draw-matrix references, packet bounds and command layout. Matrix palette entries
marked 0xffff inherit the previous packet's slot. Triangle lists, strips, fans
and quads are triangulated with preserved winding; point/line primitives consume
vertices but do not produce triangles. Direct position data remains unsupported;
all tested disc models use supported position references.

Verification passed across all 1,758 models: 8,357 mesh packets and 5,053,844
triangles. Mario: 12 packets and 4,877 triangles. Luigi: 12 packets and 5,256
triangles. Cutscene DemoMario: 13 packets and 5,360 triangles. The current mesh
output retains position and draw-matrix references only; normal/color/UV indices
are consumed to parse the stream but are not yet imported into render vertices.
Joint transforms/skinning are not yet applied and meshes are not yet rendered.

Commands:
- galaxy-probe.exe --mesh build/sample-assets/mario.bdl
- galaxy-probe.exe --verify-mesh "D:/Desktop/Super Mario Galaxy (USA, Canada) (En,Fr,Es).wbfs"

Results: build/mesh-verification.tsv and build/mesh-verification-log.txt.
Next: construct joint/world and skinning matrices, retain full vertex attributes,
and upload transformed mesh geometry to the native renderer.

## Verified default-pose skinning milestone (2026-10-03)

Native joint rotation/translation, parent transforms and Basic/Softimage/Maya
matrix modes now produce joint/world matrices. Maya parent-scale compensation
follows Petari J3DJoint.cpp; the 0xff compensation sentinel is correctly disabled.
Draw matrices use rigid joint transforms or weighted world-times-inverse-bind
matrices. These transform the decoded packet positions into default-pose geometry.
Tests cover quarter-turn rotation, parent scaling, scale compensation, weighted
blending and cycle rejection. All 1,758 models pass packet skinning and finite
vertex checks (5,053,844 triangles). This is structural/numeric validation, not
full comparison against Wii rendering or animation.

Mario and Luigi default-pose OBJ exports are in build/sample-assets:
- mario-bind.obj: 4,877 triangles
- luigi-bind.obj: 5,256 triangles
Mario's exported geometry was also visually inspected using a separate diagnostic
rasterization of the native export. The model forms a coherent default T-pose.
This diagnostic image does not use the native Direct3D mesh renderer; that renderer
still only handles texture previews. No character animation or gameplay is running.

Command: galaxy-probe.exe --export-bind-obj input.bdl new-output.obj
The existing --verify-mesh command now additionally transforms all packet vertices
using the native rig. Current results: build/skinning-verification.tsv and log.
Next: retain normal/color/UV attributes and implement Direct3D mesh drawing,
then materials, animation and game-runtime integration.

## Verified native mesh-rendering milestone (2026-10-03)

Skinned default-pose triangles now upload to Direct3D 11 vertex buffers and draw
with native vertex/pixel shaders, a depth buffer, orthographic diagnostic camera
and flat face-normal shading. Mario renders 4,877 triangles covering 137,994
pixels; Luigi renders 5,256 triangles covering 110,352 pixels at 720x720.
Hardware and WARP output matched with zero significant pixel differences on both
models. Overlapping-triangle tests verify order-independent depth occlusion.
The GPU Mario readback was visually inspected and forms the expected T-pose.

The reader now retains indexed normal/color/UV attribute references, triple NBT
indices and direct matrix selectors. These are not yet resolved into textured
render vertices; arbitrary direct color/normal/UV payloads remain consumed rather
than retained. The renderer deliberately uses calculated face normals and a gray
diagnostic material. Game materials, animation, cameras and gameplay are pending.
All 1,758 models still pass packet/rig/skinning validation after the reader change.

Open Preview Mario mesh.cmd or Preview Luigi mesh.cmd in the parent directory.
These show a static native-rendered mesh snapshot in the resizable preview window;
they are not animated model viewers or gameplay. The model-preview window passed
its hidden paint/resize lifecycle test. Close or press Escape to exit.

Commands:
- galaxy-probe.exe --render-mesh input.bdl new-preview.bmp
- galaxy-probe.exe --preview-model input.bdl
- galaxy-probe.exe --preview-model-smoke input.bdl

GPU readbacks: build/sample-assets/mario-d3d11.bmp and luigi-d3d11.bmp.
Next: decode referenced normal/color/UV values and map J3D materials/textures,
then skeletal animation and the host game runtime.

## Verified initial textured-material milestone (2026-10-03)

The native reader now resolves indexed normals, two vertex-color arrays and eight
UV arrays, with scalar formats, fixed-point conversion and per-array bounds.
MAT3 material IDs, primary colors and eight texture-slot references decode, and
INF1 hierarchy entries associate shapes with their materials. All 1,758 models
pass appearance and material-reference validation; this includes the existing
packet, rig and skinning checks. Direct attribute payloads remain unsupported in
this appearance interface; current tested models use indexed appearance data.

The Direct3D diagnostic material renderer uses primary material times vertex color,
UV0, texture slot zero, inverse-transpose skinned normals, diffuse lighting and
simple alpha rejection. Mario and Luigi render in their recognizable colors and
textures with smooth normals. Hardware/WARP comparisons reported zero significant
pixel differences for both 720x720 previews. Tests cover numeric appearance,
invalid UV/texture references, matrix-normal scaling, texture sampling, alpha
rejection and depth ordering. The textured preview window passes its lifecycle test.

This initial draw path does not implement the Wii TEV stages, texture generation,
texture transforms, original sampler states, blending, fog or game lighting.
It is an approximation for asset validation, not the final game material pipeline.
No animation, game loop, controls or gameplay is running.

Preview Mario mesh.cmd and Preview Luigi mesh.cmd now show textured static views.
Commands:
- galaxy-probe.exe --render-materials input.bdl new-preview.bmp
- galaxy-probe.exe --preview-materials input.bdl
- galaxy-probe.exe --verify-materials game.wbfs

Results: build/material-verification.tsv and its log; sample-assets contains
mario-materials-smooth.bmp and luigi-materials-smooth.bmp. Next: skeletal animation
and broader game-runtime integration, while completing the material pipeline.

### BCK joint animation milestone (2026-10-03)
Implemented bounded J3D1/bck1 ANK1 reading, constant/default tracks, both Hermite tangent layouts, and signed rotation conversion. Animation pose sampling now feeds the native joint hierarchy, skinning, normals, materials and D3D11 renderer.

Usage: `galaxy-probe --render-animation <model.bdl> <animation.bck> <frame> <new-output.bmp>`.
Extracted MarioAnime.arc:/run.bck from the supplied disc. Its 30 joints and 60-frame duration match Mario. Frames 0 and 15 render 4,877 triangles and produce distinct images; hardware/WARP mismatches were 1 and 0 respectively within the existing tolerance. Numeric interpolation, clamp, separate tangent, rotation and default-channel tests pass. Output images are in build/sample-assets/mario-run-0.bmp and mario-run-15.bmp.

This samples explicit clamped frames. Loop metadata is read but automatic loop scheduling and continuous playback are not implemented. No gameplay or game boot is verified. Next: validate the remaining BCK resources and integrate a persistent animated rendering loop before game actor/runtime integration.

### Complete disc BCK validation (2026-10-03)
Added `--verify-animations <game.wbfs>`. All 3,231 BCK resources across 2,232 archives decode successfully: 54,819 joint tracks, 2,214,706 channel keys, and 1,480,113 finite channel samples at start, midpoint and duration. Retail BillBoarder attack animations have equal consecutive timestamps. The reader now accepts nondecreasing times and selects the final coincident key at a transition, matching Petari J3DGetKeyFrameInterpolation (including equal timestamps at frame zero). Regression checks cover both cases and truncated input. Validation checks every key's finite data, data-array bounds and timestamp order; it does not establish correctness of every intermediate animated pose or animation-to-model pairing.

Detailed inventory and scan results: build/animation-verification.tsv and build/animation-verification.log. Build tests pass. Continuous playback, scene runtime and gameplay remain pending.

### Animation clock milestone (2026-10-03)
Added animation_clock.hpp with the five J3DFrameCtrl update modes, stopped/wrapped state flags, positive/negative rates and loop-point behavior. Boundary behavior follows Petari/src/JSystem/J3DGraphAnimator/J3DAnimation.cpp (including reverse-loop end minus one and one-shot end minus 0.001). Large loop jumps use arithmetic wrapping to bound work rather than iterative subtraction. An elapsed-time accumulator supplies fixed 60 Hz updates for the forthcoming preview; this is a preview clock choice, not verified game simulation timing or unlocked FPS support.

Tests cover hold/reset/loop/reverse/reverse-loop boundaries, reverse wrapping, multi-cycle steps, invalid elapsed time and identical tick results under 30 Hz and 120 Hz render cadence. Build/self-tests pass. `galaxy-probe --animation-timeline <animation.bck> <tick-count>` records a timeline. Actual Mario run.bck wraps at ticks 60 and 120; saved in build/mario-run-timeline.tsv.

This clock is not yet connected to a continuous animated window. That integration is the next rendering milestone; gameplay remains unverified.

### Continuous animation preview milestone (2026-10-03)
Added `--preview-animation <model.bdl> <animation.bck>` and a root Preview Mario animation.cmd launcher. A Win32 timer drives the fixed-step animation clock, recomputes joint transforms/skinned positions/normals, and displays each newly rendered pose. Space toggles pause and Escape closes. This is a live model preview, not gameplay.

The D3D11 mesh device, context, compiled shader bytecode and model texture resources persist across frames. Display texture uploads are updated in place. Render targets, vertex buffers and several pipeline objects still allocate per frame; frames are read back and uploaded to a separate display device. This correctness-first path is not a performance target or a promise of 120/240 FPS. Diagnostic camera framing follows each pose's bounds and is not the game's camera. Timer stalls are capped at 0.25 seconds to avoid unbounded catch-up.

Build/self-tests pass. Hidden Mario and Luigi animation window checks render eight distinct poses, assert GPU-device retention, compare every display-upload/readback pixel with the rendered frame, and verify pause, resize and close. Luigi is tested with Mario's run animation as a compatible rig diagnostic; this does not implement Luigi gameplay. Logs: build/mario-animation-preview.log and build/luigi-animation-preview.log. Visible playback and performance have not been manually evaluated. Next: direct swap-chain mesh rendering, stable camera framing, and game scene/actor integration. No playable game is verified.

### Stable animation preview camera (2026-10-03)
The animated preview now computes fixed orthographic camera bounds from the entire clip before playback. The bounds are the union of rotated skinned geometry at sampled poses, so motion no longer causes automatic camera zoom and centering on every update. Clips up to 240 frames sample every frame; longer clips sample 241 uniformly spaced poses to bound startup work. This is diagnostic camera framing; sampled bounds cannot guarantee containment of every interpolated extreme and do not reproduce the game's camera system.

Build/self-tests and Mario/Luigi hidden animated rendering checks pass, including display pixel readback, pause, resize and retained-device checks with fixed camera bounds. Logs: build/stable-camera-build.log, build/mario-stable-camera.log and build/luigi-stable-camera.log. Launch with Preview Mario animation.cmd. Next: direct rendering to the window swap chain and scene/actor integration. Gameplay remains unverified.

### Stage placement table loading (2026-10-03)
Added bounded native JMap/BCSV StageTable reading, based on Petari JMapInfo: 31-based field hashes, field lookup, masked/shifted 32/16/8-bit integers, finite floats, inline 32-byte strings and string-table offsets. Strings are preserved as source bytes (no Japanese encoding conversion yet); integer reads currently expose unsigned masked values rather than typed signed game arguments. Bounds, descriptor overlap, shifts, string pointers and terminators are checked.

`galaxy-probe --stage-tables <game.wbfs> </StageData/archive.arc>` validates every cell in every /jmp/ resource and writes row/field counts. HeavenlyBeachGalaxy passes 119 tables / 113 records. EggStarGalaxy and AstroGalaxy scans also pass; exact counts are in build/egg-stage-tables.log and build/astro-stage-tables.log. Hash lookup, masked integer extraction, string-table lookup and invalid string pointers have regression tests. Build/self-tests pass.

This supplies structured placement data only: actor creation, zone transforms, scenario layer activation, collision and stage rendering remain pending. The game is not playable. Next: expose placement names/transforms and connect referenced stage models to a scene.

### Object placement inventory and transforms (2026-10-03)
Added `--stage-placements <game.wbfs> </StageData/archive.arc>` to read object, planet, zone and player-start names plus positions, degree rotations and scales. Produces a TSV inventory and checks whether a direct /ObjectData/<name>.arc or /StageData/<zone>.arc exists. Missing transform fields use identity defaults. Explicit float SRT matrices and nested matrix composition are implemented and tested; zone hierarchy and scenario filtering are not connected yet.

EggStarGalaxy: 59 placements, 15 zone records, 51 direct-name archive matches. HeavenlyBeachGalaxy and AstroGalaxy inventories also pass (counts in build/beach-placements.log and build/astro-placements.log). These inventories include all stored layers; they do not represent a selected playable scenario. Direct-name matches are candidates only: actor factories can redirect model names and resource dependencies. Actor creation is not implemented. Placement string encoding remains source bytes and TSV numbers are diagnostic display precision.

Build and SRT/composition tests pass. Inventories: build/egg-placements.tsv, build/beach-placements.tsv, build/astro-placements.tsv. Next: load a directly referenced planet model and apply its placement for a native stage geometry preview. Collision, gravity, scene actors and gameplay remain pending.

### Native placed planet rendering (2026-10-03)
Added `--render-placement <game.wbfs> </StageData/archive.arc> </jmp/table> <row> <new-preview.bmp>`. The command reads a placement, resolves its direct-name ObjectData archive, decodes its single model, skins default-pose geometry, applies local-zone placement SRT and inverse-transpose normals, and renders materials through D3D11. Multi-model archives fail with an explicit selection requirement instead of guessing.

SoramameZone common objinfo row 0 renders BroadBeanPlanetB: 1,484 triangles, 109,093 geometry pixels, zero hardware/WARP mismatches. Row 1 BroadBeanPlanetC also passes; details in build/broadbean-c-placed.log. Images: build/sample-assets/broadbean-placed.bmp and broadbean-c-placed.bmp. Build/self-tests pass and the BroadBeanPlanetB image was visually inspected.

This previews one locally placed model at a time using a diagnostic camera which centers the transformed bounds. Parent-zone transforms, simultaneous scene geometry, actor factory redirects, scenario layers, TEV material reproduction, collision, gravity and gameplay remain pending. It does not establish visual parity with the original game. Next: combine common-layer models into a zone preview and load collision geometry.

### Combined native zone geometry (2026-10-03)
Added stage_scene.hpp and `--render-zone <game.wbfs> </StageData/archive.arc> <new-preview.bmp>` / `--preview-zone <game.wbfs> </StageData/archive.arc>`. The common objinfo table now produces one scene containing all supported direct-name, single-model instances. Each instance receives its local SRT and normal transform; texture indices are remapped into a scene texture list and geometry renders with shared depth. Unsupported archive names/model counts are explicitly logged as skips; malformed supported data fails.

SoramameZone: three models, no skips, 10,621 triangles, 15 textures, 34,387 geometry pixels and zero hardware/WARP mismatches. DiskGardenZone: 30 instances, 10 unresolved placements, 25,859 triangles, 47 textures, 177,839 geometry pixels and zero mismatches. Build/self-tests pass. Soramame's combined image was visually inspected. Images and detailed loaded/skipped reports: build/sample-assets/soramame-zone.bmp, diskgarden-zone.bmp, build/soramame-zone.log and build/diskgarden-zone.log.

Root launcher Preview Soramame zone.cmd opens a static scene snapshot in the existing preview window. This loads only common objinfo, not scenario layers, parent zones, sky handling, actor factories or dynamic game behavior. Textures are duplicated per instance and camera/materials remain diagnostic. Collision, gravity, player control and gameplay are still pending. Next: collision resource loading and scenario/zone assembly.

### Requested HD texture and HUD support
User supplied https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1SwN5iBekCtjLaltkFZ_vzQApzSU3Rltb and requested optional HD textures and HUD elements targeting 4K, with 1080p fallback. Integrate after more single-player runtime progress. Inspect pack contents, replacement naming/format, HUD coverage and compatibility before promising asset coverage. Keep original extracted textures available as fallback. This is a future requirement, not implemented support.

### Initial collision geometry milestone (2026-10-03)
Added bounded KCL vector/prism decoding and triangle reconstruction based on Petari KCollisionServer::getPos. Vector array alignment padding is supported. `--render-collision <mesh.kcl> <new-preview.bmp>` compares native hardware and WARP renders. BroadBeanPlanetB collision results are in build/broadbean-collision.log; reconstruction image in build/sample-assets/broadbean-collision.bmp. Octree traversal, collision attributes and player hit response remain unimplemented. No gameplay is verified.

Collision follow-up: accounted for the stored prism pointer's reserved-record bias, which can overlap the end of the normal array. The pack link was saved but the web reader could not inspect its Drive folder; pack contents and coverage are unverified.

### Native segment collision queries (2026-10-03)
Added collision_query.hpp with a double-precision, two-sided segment/triangle nearest-hit query. Results carry fraction, contact point, source face normal, KCL attribute index and prism index. Zero-length segments and parallel triangles return no hit; non-finite endpoints are rejected. This is a linear scan of reconstructed geometry, not a port of KCollisionServer prism thickness/octree checks. It cannot yet reproduce gameplay collision behavior.

Regression checks cover nearest surface selection, reversed direction, edge hits, misses, parallel segments and zero length. `--verify-collision <mesh.kcl>` checks normal-aligned segments at sampled real triangle centroids, both in isolation and in the complete collision mesh. BroadBeanPlanetB: 1,216 triangles and 32 real-geometry checks pass. BroadBeanPlanetC also passes; details in build/collision-c-query.log. Build/self-tests pass. Build and query logs: build/collision-query-build.log, collision-query.log and collision-c-query.log.

Next: apply placement/zone transforms to collision, add spatial acceleration, and implement sphere contact/movement foundations. Gravity, Mario control, game actor logic and gameplay remain pending. One-minute continuation resumed at the user's request.

### Placed scene collision coordinates (2026-10-03)
Added stage_collision.hpp and `--verify-zone-collision <game.wbfs> </StageData/archive.arc>`. Common-layer objinfo placements resolve direct-name archives with exactly one KCL, reconstruct triangles, apply the same local placement SRT as visual models and transform face normals with the inverse transpose. Segment hits now retain an instance ID alongside prism and attribute IDs, preventing ambiguity between repeated objects.

Build/self-tests pass, including a known rotated/nonuniformly scaled collision plane and hit metadata test. SoramameZone: two collision instances, 5,097 triangles, 33 sampled isolated/combined segment checks; BroadBeanPlanetC is logged as skipped because its archive has two KCL resources. DiskGardenZone: three collision instances, 4,260 triangles, 33 checks, 37 logged skips (missing direct archive, no KCL or multiple KCLs). These results do not establish complete zone collision coverage. Reports: build/soramame-collision.log, build/diskgarden-collision.log, build/zone-collision-build.log.

Only common objinfo and local zone coordinates are supported. Parent-zone transforms, scenario layers, actor-specific collision selection, collision PA attributes, octree/spatial acceleration, sphere contacts, gravity and player response remain pending. Geometry thickness is not used by segment tracing. No gameplay is verified. Next: spatial acceleration and sphere contact foundations for movement.

### Sphere contact foundation (2026-10-03)
Added sphere_contact.hpp: closest triangle point with face, edge and vertex regions, two-sided sphere overlap contacts, surface point, outward separation normal, penetration depth and instance/prism/attribute identity. Tangency yields zero penetration. Coincident center/contact uses the normalized source face normal. Invalid radius and non-finite centers are rejected. This is geometric overlap only, not original KCL thickness behavior or Mario collision response; contacts are not merged across adjacent triangles and queries still scan linearly.

Build/self-tests pass for face/edge/vertex distances, known penetration, tangency/miss, reverse-side normals and metadata. --verify-zone-collision now also tests spheres at sampled transformed triangle centroids in isolation and the whole zone. SoramameZone and DiskGardenZone sphere/segment checks pass; counts and loaded/skipped instances are in build/soramame-sphere.log and build/diskgarden-sphere.log. Build log: build/sphere-build.log.

Next: spatial acceleration, swept movement/depenetration and gravity foundations. These contact functions alone do not prevent tunneling or implement player movement. No gameplay is verified.

### Collision bounds hierarchy (2026-10-03)
Added immutable CollisionIndex with median-split triangle AABB hierarchy and conservative padded bounds. Segment and sphere bounding boxes gather candidates before the existing narrow-phase routines. Candidate order is restored to source order to preserve equal-distance hit selection and contact ordering. The index owns its geometry snapshot; transformed geometry must be reindexed if moved. Candidate geometry is currently copied per query, so this is an initial acceleration path with allocation overhead, not final runtime performance.

--verify-zone-collision now compares indexed segment hits and sphere contact results exactly with full-mesh reference queries at sampled real geometry. SoramameZone and DiskGardenZone pass. Candidate totals and full-scan equivalents are recorded in build/soramame-index.log and build/diskgarden-index.log; these are triangle candidate counts, not measured frame-rate gains. Build/self-tests pass. The hierarchy has not been integrated into player movement because that runtime is still pending.

Next: movement and separation response using sphere contacts, then gravity and actor integration. This index is a native bounds hierarchy, not the original KCL octree traversal. Gameplay remains unverified.

### Bounded sphere overlap separation (2026-10-03)
Added collision_response.hpp. separateSphere iteratively resolves the deepest current sphere overlap using the indexed contact query. It returns corrected position, iterations, residual penetration and a resolved flag; work is capped at 24 iterations and the separation tolerance is 0.001 world units. Narrow gaps or conflicting contacts can remain unresolved and callers must handle that result. This geometric, two-sided correction does not reproduce Mario or original KCL thickness behavior.

Build/self-tests pass for a plane, perpendicular corner, unchanged clear sphere and repeat stability. Real-zone checks compare the final residual against full-mesh contacts. SoramameZone: 33 sampled overlaps resolved, zero unresolved. DiskGardenZone results are in build/diskgarden-response.log. Reports include all previous collision checks and loaded/skipped coverage. No performance/FPS guarantee follows from these tests.

No velocity, sweep, gravity, input or player actor is connected. Static overlap separation alone can tunnel through thin surfaces when used after large movement steps. Next: swept movement and contact velocity handling, followed by runtime player/gravity integration. Gameplay remains unverified.

### Swept sphere collision foundation (2026-10-03)
Added collision_sweep.hpp with bounded conservative advancement over BVH candidates. Each step uses closest surface distance and advances less than the available sphere gap; hits return travel fraction, position and separation normal. Work is capped at 128 iterations; exhaustion returns partial travel with complete=false, never an unobstructed result. Initial overlap blocks at fraction zero and must be separated before movement. Two-sided geometric surfaces and a 0.001 world-unit contact skin are used, not original game collision rules.

Build/self-tests pass for a 200-unit sweep against a thin plane, reverse approach, clear parallel travel, stationary sphere and initial overlap. Real geometry verification passes 33 sampled normal-aligned sweeps in each of SoramameZone and DiskGardenZone. Logs: build/sweep-build.log, build/soramame-sweep.log, build/diskgarden-sweep.log. These sampled tests do not prove all motion configurations or original-game parity; float world coordinates and contact skin may need scale-aware tuning.

Next: sliding/contact velocity handling and a controllable diagnostic sphere, then player/gravity integration. Mario gameplay is still unimplemented and unverified.

### Swept sphere sliding foundation (2026-10-03)
Added collision_slide.hpp. slideSphere separates initial overlaps, sweeps requested displacement, removes motion into each hit normal and retries tangent motion, with an eight-contact budget. Final overlap checks return incomplete status if separation fails. This is a diagnostic movement primitive, not Mario movement, velocity integration or gravity.

Sweeps now reject triangles whose supporting plane stays outside the sphere's reach over the entire segment, avoiding tiny advancement steps for near-floor parallel travel. Synthetic floor/corner sliding, clear movement and near-surface parallel sweeps pass. Real-zone sweep and movement checks pass in SoramameZone and DiskGardenZone: 33 movements complete in each, zero partial results, with final penetration verified by full-mesh contacts. Build/self-tests pass. Logs: build/slide-build.log, build/soramame-slide.log and build/diskgarden-slide.log.

Fixed contact offsets and float world coordinates need further robustness checks. Motion is two-sided and geometric; no slopes, grounding, steps, original KCL thickness or game-specific actor rules are implemented. Next: connect a controllable diagnostic body and gravity foundation to the native scene. Gameplay remains unverified.

### Fixed-step diagnostic body (2026-10-03)
Added diagnostic_body.hpp connecting 60 Hz velocity/gravity integration, bounded swept sliding, contact velocity clipping, ground probing, directional input and jump requests. Sliding now records hit normals for velocity handling. This is a diagnostic sphere with arbitrary tuning (40 units/second directional speed, 60 jump impulse), independent of original Mario actor behavior. Gravity is supplied as a constant vector per test, not read from Galaxy gravity volumes. Jump input is a request event; a future UI must supply key edges.

Build/self-tests pass for falling/grounding, directional ground motion and jumping on a known plane. `--verify-zone-body <game.wbfs> </StageData/archive.arc>` runs 120 fixed updates at sampled placed triangle locations with synthetic normal-aligned gravity. SoramameZone and DiskGardenZone each pass nine runs: no partial movement steps and no residual penetration above 0.001. Logs: build/body-build.log, build/soramame-body.log and build/diskgarden-body.log.

The body is not yet wired to keyboard input or rendering. Fixed gravity, direct directional speed and ground probing do not reproduce game slopes, moving surfaces, steps, radial gravity, animation or Mario controls. Collision coverage remains limited to previously supported common-layer instances. Next: a controllable diagnostic scene window and gravity data integration. No playable game is verified.

### Keyboard-controlled diagnostic scene body (2026-10-03)
Added --preview-zone-body and root Preview diagnostic body.cmd. The native window renders common-layer models and an orange octahedral sphere marker, running fixed-step diagnostic gravity/collision with WASD input and edge-triggered Space jump. Escape closes; focus loss clears input. Spawn uses the first supported collision triangle and constant normal-aligned gravity, with a fixed local orthographic camera. This is not Mario gameplay or a selected game scenario.

Build/self-tests and hidden SoramameZone window checks pass for eight fixed body updates, rendering, key press, focus loss and close. Visible play and performance have not been manually evaluated. Existing GPU readback/display upload limitations remain. Logs: build/body-preview-build.log and build/body-preview.log. Next: improve diagnostic camera/input validation, implement game gravity volumes and connect original player actor logic. No playable port is verified.

### Point gravity data foundation (2026-10-03)
Added point_gravity.hpp and --zone-point-gravity. GlobalPointGravity placements now supply local center, range, distant offset and inverse direction. Defaults follow PlanetGravity/PointGravityCreator: unlimited range, distant=500*scale.x; nonnegative Range/Distant/Inverse overrides follow GravityUtil's argument handling. Range boundary is exclusive. Normalized inward/outward direction and near-center zero direction are implemented. This exposes direction only, not the full game's gravity magnitude, priority blending or manager activation rules.

Build/self-tests pass for direction, inverse, range boundary, unlimited range and center. SoramameZone common planetobjinfo loads one point source at (250,-687.5,-1125), range 2000, distant 800, inverse false, with nine other gravity records explicitly unsupported. Report: build/soramame-point-gravity.log. Disk/plane/box gravity, scenario layers, parent transforms and manager selection remain pending. The diagnostic body still uses its existing synthetic fixed gravity; loaded point sources are not connected yet. No Mario gameplay is verified.

### Disk gravity data and direction (2026-10-03)
Added disk_gravity.hpp based on Petari DiskGravity and DiskGravityCreator: rotated plane normal, radius=500*maximum placement scale, both-side and edge-gravity flags, angular sector, range/distant override and inverse direction. Within the radius, direction attracts toward the disk plane; outside, enabled edge gravity attracts toward the nearest circular edge. Sector axis is rotated through half the valid degree from the placement side vector. Defaults/arguments follow source Obj_arg0/1/2 (the source comment mentioning arg3 is inconsistent with the function parameter).

Build/self-tests pass for above/below directions, circular edge attraction, disabled back/edge behavior, range boundary and sector exclusion. --zone-disk-gravity loads six real SoramameZone disk sources; four other records are explicitly unsupported by this command. Report: build/soramame-disk-gravity.log. Build log: build/disk-gravity-build.log.

Point and disk implementations provide direction and placement parameters only. Gravity manager selection/blending, source activation, original acceleration magnitude, parent-zone transforms and diagnostic body integration remain pending. The existing body preview still uses synthetic fixed gravity. No Mario gameplay is verified.

### Supported gravity priority selection (2026-10-03)
Added gravity_field.hpp loading common-layer point/disk sources with stored nonnegative Priority. Selection uses the highest in-range active priority, blends equal-priority vectors with PlanetGravity's 4000000/max(1,distance-Distant)^2 weighting, and normalizes the result. Out-of-range high-priority sources permit lower-priority fallback. The source active flag is currently static; game appearance/follower state, host exclusion and gravity type masks are not implemented.

Build/self-tests pass for distance-weighted blending, priority override, out-of-range fallback and inactive sources. --zone-gravity samples stored common player starts. SoramameZone loads seven supported sources and logs three unsupported records; its one start selects three sources at priority 10, direction approximately (-0.144971,-0.989375,0.0110205). Report: build/soramame-gravity-field.log. Build log: build/gravity-field-build.log.

This is selection for the supported subset only; omitted plane/box/etc. sources can change the original game's result. Parent zones, scenarios, source activation and body integration remain pending. The diagnostic preview still uses synthetic fixed gravity. No Mario gameplay is verified.

### Stored spawn and gravity body integration (2026-10-03)
Added gravity_body.hpp, --verify-gravity-body and --preview-gravity-body. The diagnostic sphere starts at the common stored Mario spawn, offset one diagnostic radius along local up, and samples the supported point/disk gravity field every fixed tick. Input tangent axes follow the updated gravity. Acceleration magnitude remains an arbitrary diagnostic 100; this is not Mario actor acceleration. Missing/zero supported gravity stops with an explicit error instead of silently falling back.

SoramameZone's stored start passes 120 body updates: grounded=true, zero partial steps and zero residual overlap. Hidden gravity-body window check also passes. Logs: build/gravity-body-build.log and build/gravity-body.log. Root launcher Preview game gravity body.cmd uses WASD, Space jump and Escape close. Visible controls/performance have not been manually assessed; the existing fixed local camera and readback display path remain.

Only supported common-layer gravity and collision participate. Missing plane/box gravity, scenario activation, parent-zone transforms, game actor logic and collision selection can change behavior. The orange sphere is still diagnostic; Mario gameplay, progression and original game boot remain unimplemented/unverified. Next: improve scene camera/player visualization and port additional actor/runtime dependencies.

### Boxed parallel gravity (2026-10-03)
Added NativeBoxGravity based on ParallelGravity/PlaneInBoxGravityCreator: rotated box centered one scaled half-height above the placement, inclusive projected extents, downward plane direction, inverse flag and base/selected-axis distance for manager weighting. Half-extents use 500*placement scale. Unlike point/disk range, box inclusion uses its box extents directly, following source. Positive scales are required in the supported path.

Build/self-tests pass for boundary inclusion, outside exclusion, distance-axis contribution and inverse direction. SoramameZone now loads all ten common planetobjinfo records using point, disk and box types with no unsupported records in that table. Gravity selection and the 120-update stored-start body regression pass (details: build/soramame-box-gravity.log). This improves that table's format coverage, not full-game gravity parity: source activation/type/host filtering, parent zones and scenario layers remain pending.

Existing Preview game gravity body.cmd now uses the expanded supported field. This is still diagnostic sphere motion and arbitrary acceleration tuning. No Mario gameplay or original game boot is verified.

### Diagnostic body follow camera and display checks (2026-10-03)
The diagnostic body preview now translates its fixed orthographic view with the body while preserving scale and yaw. The scene can be explored without immediately leaving the initial local frame. This is not the original game's camera, and orientation does not rotate with local gravity.

Hidden stored-start gravity-body window verification passes eight simulated/rendered updates, every-pixel display upload/readback comparisons, retained GPU device identity, constant camera extents and keyboard release/focus-loss behavior. Build/self-tests pass. Logs: build/body-camera-build.log and build/body-camera.log. Existing Preview game gravity body.cmd uses this camera. Visible play/performance remains unreviewed. Mario actor behavior, scenario/zone assembly and original game boot remain pending.

### Scenario zone layer masks (2026-10-03)
Added scenario_layers.hpp and --scenario-layers <game.wbfs> <galaxy-name> <scenario-number>. The reader validates scenario/zone tables, finds ScenarioNo, resolves each ZoneName column and decodes its layer bit mask. Common is always included and bits 0..15 map to layera..layerp. Missing/duplicate scenario records and invalid field types fail explicitly.

Build/self-tests and EggStarGalaxy scenario 1/2 inventories pass. Exact masks and enabled layer names are in build/egg-scenario-1.log and build/egg-scenario-2.log. This only reads selection data; scene/collision/gravity loaders still load common data and do not yet assemble scenario-selected parent/child zones. Next: connect masks to recursive zone assembly. No playable game is verified.

Added scenario_assembly.hpp and --scenario-placement-tree. Scenario masks now select common/enabled layer tables before traversing stageobjinfo child zones. Parent and placement matrices compose into world transforms for active object, gravity and start records. Zone instance IDs distinguish repeated instances. Cycles, unknown referenced zones and excessive recursion/placement counts fail explicitly.

Build/self-tests and EggStarGalaxy scenarios 1 and 2 assemble successfully; exact zone/placement/disabled-table counts are in build/egg-scenario-1-assembly.log and egg-scenario-2-assembly.log. World-position inventories: build/egg-scenario-1-placements.tsv and egg-scenario-2-placements.tsv. These retain complete world matrices internally; TSV positions are diagnostic output. Added scenario_scene.hpp and --render-scenario-zone. Materials and texture indices are combined for a native scene render. Unsupported names/model counts are logged. Actor creation and dynamic behavior are still absent.

Build/self-tests and EggStarGalaxy scenario 1 SoramameZone hardware/WARP rendering pass. Exact model/skipped/triangle counts are in build/soramame-scenario-1-render.log; image: build/sample-assets/soramame-scenario-1.bmp. Added scenario_collision.hpp and --verify-scenario-collision. Selected-zone active objinfo layers now resolve supported single-KCL archives and transform triangles/normals with the same assembled parent world matrices as visual geometry. Instance/prism identity is retained. Missing/no/multiple KCL archives remain explicitly logged skips; actor-specific selection and PA behavior are still absent.

Build/self-tests and EggStarGalaxy scenarios 1/2 SoramameZone checks pass for sampled segment hits, sphere contacts and exact indexed/full-mesh query equivalence. Scenario placement records now retain parent matrices. Added scenario_gravity.hpp and --scenario-gravity to decode enabled planetobjinfo sources and apply parent translations/rotations to point centers, disk normals/sector axes and box matrices/up vectors. Supported parent matrices must have unit column lengths; scaled zones fail rather than silently changing gravity semantics. Type/host/activation filtering remains absent.

Added --verify-scenario-body / --preview-scenario-body and root Preview scenario body.cmd. ### Original NameObj native compilation (2026-10-03)
The verified Zig build now compiles untouched Petari NameObj.cpp and NameObjRegister.cpp alongside the existing original Nerve/Spine state infrastructure. Explicit native include adapters provide holder storage, movement notification boundary, minimal Revolution type umbrella and an unused FunctorBase forward declaration. These replace dependency boundaries for this narrow compilation target; they are not implementations of the full Wii SDK, scene movement controller, heap functors or original NameObjHolder layout.

Build/self-tests pass for original constructor registration, virtual initialization, executeMovement, deferred suspend/resume synchronization, notifications and cancellation of pending requests. The original source checkout remains unmodified. Build log: build/name-obj-build.log. CMake source list was updated consistently but CMake is still unverified in this environment.

The compiled NameObj lifecycle is currently exercised in tests, not connected to scenario actor factories or Mario. Native holder pointers are non-owning and scene lifetime/unregistration still needs an explicit runtime design. Next: connect an owned scene actor registry/scheduler and original actor dependencies. No playable game is verified.

### Owned native scene actor scheduling (2026-10-03)
Added native_scene.hpp with unique actor ownership, non-owning registration storage and per-tick original NameObj syncWithFlags/executeMovement dispatch. Construction scopes the original registration singleton to the target holder and restores prior state, including failure cleanup. Scene destruction releases actors and clears registration pointers. Recursive ticking and actor creation during movement fail explicitly; deferred mutation is not implemented.

The diagnostic body preview now advances through a NativeCallbackActor derived from original NameObj rather than directly calling its update in the timer loop. This is a native callback actor, not Mario or a ported game actor factory. Scene category ordering, placement initialization, animation/draw scheduling and original movement-controller notification behavior remain pending.

Build/self-tests pass for separate scene registration, ownership counts, deferred suspension/resume and recovery after an actor movement exception. Scenario body hidden window/render/input regression also passes. Logs: build/native-scene-build.log and build/native-scene-preview.log. No playable Mario game is verified.

### Original JMapInfo native compatibility (2026-10-03)
Added native compilation of original JGadget hashcode.cpp and a local JMapInfo.cpp compatibility copy (src/jmap_port.cpp). Modern C++ rejected two original variable scopes: a case-local string pointer and a goto crossing a pointer initializer. The local copy adds a case scope and moves that initializer before the conditional goto; the original Petari checkout remains untouched. An explicit ASCII string-comparison boundary replaces the broader original StringUtil dependency.

Build/self-tests pass for JMapInfo attachment to a host-endian synthetic table, signed integer/float lookup, row search, missing fields and original field hashing. This proves the lookup path on validated native-layout input only. Raw big-endian disc tables must still be converted into aligned host storage before original actors can consume them; that bridge is not yet implemented. Build log: build/jmap-native-build.log. CMake metadata updated but still unverified. No Mario gameplay is verified.

Bridge follow-up: retail packed rows contain unaligned numeric fields. The bridge now repacks each field into aligned native offsets and a padded row stride, rewriting descriptors/data offset and relocating the string table while preserving relative string offsets. Both real-stage comparisons pass after this correction.

### Original placement lifecycle bridge (2026-10-03)
Added NativePlacedActor adapter and NativeScene::place. Owned actors now receive validated JMapInfoIter input through virtual init, followed by initAfterPlacement, with failed initialization rolled back from registration/ownership. The adapter owns shared native table storage and stable actor names, reads placement fields via original JMapInfo, and computes placement/world matrices. Initialization disallows actor creation/reentrant movement; full deferred actor mutation remains pending.

--verify-placed-actors initializes every objinfo row across stored layers (not scenario filtered), compares names and matrices with the checked parser and dispatches original NameObj movement. SoramameZone and EggStarGalaxy pass; counts in build/soramame-placed-actors.log and build/egg-placed-actors.log. Build/self-tests pass. These are native data adapters whose movement only counts updates, not original enemies, objects or Mario behaviors. Actor factory selection, placement-ID rules, game initialization side effects and original player runtime remain pending. No playable game is verified.

### Active scenario actor lifecycle (2026-10-03)
`--verify-scenario-actors <game.wbfs> <Galaxy> <ScenarioNo>` now constructs an owned NativeScenarioActors runtime from active scenario objinfo records. Shared host-endian original-reader tables stay alive with their actors; repeated zone instances get separate actors and composed world transforms. The adapter initializes through NameObj::init/initAfterPlacement, updates through original executeMovement, and honors original suspend/resume flags.

Verified EggStarGalaxy scenarios 1 and 2: 274 and 285 active adapters across 16 zone instances, respectively; 84 and 104 disabled tables excluded. Every name/world transform matched the checked scenario assembly. Three updates per actor and isolated suspend/resume checks passed. Native build and self-tests passed (scenario-actors-build.log).

These are placement adapters, not original game actor implementations. This verification exercises all active objinfo rows without requiring their model archives; it does not add Mario gameplay, enemy logic, game boot, or progression. Diagnostic body previews are still separate from this scenario actor runtime. Factory selection and original actor dependencies remain pending.

### Scenario runtime integration (2026-10-03)
Scenario body verification and the interactive/hidden scenario preview now construct NativeScenarioActors for the full selected scenario. All active objinfo adapters update once per fixed 60 Hz diagnostic body step, including actors in zones outside the selected rendered/collision zone. The preview retains the actor runtime for its lifetime and verifies update counts against body ticks in smoke mode. Common-zone previews remain unchanged.

Verified EggStarGalaxy scenario 1, SoramameZone: all 274 adapters completed 120 updates alongside the body, which grounded with no partial steps or residual penetration. Hidden window smoke passed eight actor/body updates, frame upload/readback equality, input checks and camera/device retention. Build/self-tests passed (scenario-runtime-build.log).

This connects lifecycle scheduling to the existing diagnostic preview; adapters still only count movement calls, and the orange body remains diagnostic physics rather than Mario. Original actor factory/behavior, game boot and progression remain unimplemented.

### Original NameObjAdaptor dispatch (2026-10-03)
The native build now compiles unchanged Petari NameObjAdaptor.cpp. Diagnostic body callbacks use its original connectToMovement cloning and movement dispatch. Native FunctorBase replaces the previous forward declaration with the original call/clone signatures plus a virtual destructor so native std::function captures are safely released through owned base pointers. This is a native ABI adaptation, not Wii heap compatibility; clone rejects non-null Wii heap requests.

Build/self-tests passed, including original adapter animation/draw dispatch, cloned callback lifetime and capture destruction, movement suspend/resume and exception recovery. Scenario window smoke passed with 274 active adapters and eight body/render updates. Log: adaptor-build.log. CMake source list updated but CMake remains unverified in this environment.

Original gameplay actors, Mario control and boot/progression are still pending; callback integration does not make the game playable.

### Native scene frame phases (2026-10-03)
NativeScene now dispatches calcAnim for all actors, then calcViewAndEntry for all actors, then draw for all actors. Scenario preview runs these original NameObj virtual entry points before every rendered diagnostic image, independently from the fixed movement tick. Placement adapters count each phase for cadence verification; their phase methods do not implement game animations or GPU drawing yet.

Self-tests verify phase ordering across two actors, movement-only suspension without suppression of render phases, recursive-phase rejection and recovery after exceptions. Hidden EggStarGalaxy scenario 1 preview verifies equal animation/view/draw counts for all 274 adapters, at least nine rendered passes including initial display, plus existing eight fixed body updates and pixel checks. Native build and self-tests pass (scene-phases-build.log).

This is a native ordered scheduler without original category priorities, draw-buffer routing or visibility culling. Original Mario/game actor behaviors, game boot and progression remain pending; the port is not playable.

### Original J3D animation frame controller (2026-10-03)
AnimationClock now delegates each tick to Petari J3DFrameCtrl::update. frame_controller.hpp contains the original class declaration and init/checkPass/update subset from pinned J3DAnimation sources; method bodies are preserved with inline linkage to avoid pulling unrelated Wii renderer/material dependencies. The original checkout remains unchanged. This is an extracted native subset rather than compilation of all J3DAnimation.cpp.

The wrapper validates signed-16-bit integral start/end/loop ranges, finite frame/rate and bounded rate/frame excursions before calling the original repeated-wrap implementation. The prior implementation is retained as referenceTick for differential regression tests. Verified 2,500 updates covering all five modes, forward/reverse/fractional rates and wrapping, in addition to existing clock tests. Build/self-tests pass (frame-controller-build.log). Real Mario run.bck hidden preview passes eight skinned poses, retained GPU textures/device, pause, resize, frame upload and close checks.

Original frame timing now drives the animation diagnostic preview. This does not implement Mario control, complete J3D rendering, game boot or progression; the port remains unplayable.

### Mario visual on scenario diagnostic body (2026-10-03)
`--preview-scenario-mario <game.wbfs> <Galaxy> <ScenarioNo> <Zone>` replaces the orange sphere mesh with the supplied disc's Mario BDL and wait/run BCK animations. Model textures are appended to the scene textures; skinned positions/normals use a gravity-aligned local basis. The default model height is fitted to the diagnostic sphere diameter, with feet offset toward the sphere's lower surface. Idle/run selection follows directional input and resets the original frame controller on clip changes. Facing currently uses the diagnostic tangent basis rather than original Mario turning.

Launcher: Preview diagnostic Mario model.cmd. WASD moves, Space jumps, Escape closes. This uses existing diagnostic sphere collision/gravity, not MarioActor physics, abilities or original controls. Other actor adapters still have no game behavior. No boot/progression or playable game is claimed.

Build/self-tests and EggStarGalaxy scenario 1 SoramameZone hidden model smoke pass. Additional real-asset checks verify changing run poses and rotated/translated gravity basis positions within .001 units. Eight skinned frame uploads/readbacks, retained GPU device, 274 scenario adapter updates, camera and input checks pass. Log: body-model-build.log. Preview rendering is simplified; no original TEV, camera, scale calibration or original character collision capsule.

### Diagnostic Mario directional heading (2026-10-03)
The diagnostic Mario visual now turns to tangent movement input, retains its last heading when idle, and projects that heading onto the current gravity plane. If changing gravity makes the old heading parallel to up, the diagnostic input basis supplies a checked fallback. The model basis stays orthogonal; diagonal input is normalized. Turns are instantaneous diagnostic orientation, not original Mario turning or gravity transport behavior.

Self-tests verify diagonal normalization, idle retention with float tolerance, gravity projection, degenerate fallback and orthogonality. Scenario Mario hidden smoke now holds D for eight simulated/rendered frames, checks visual forward aligns with directional input and checks release, alongside run-pose, texture/readback and active actor cadence checks. Final build/self-tests and smoke pass (heading-build.log). The first exact-equality idle assertion was corrected to account for harmless float renormalization, then rerun successfully.

Original Mario control, actions and abilities, game boot/progression remain pending. This is still a diagnostic preview, not a playable port.

### Diagnostic airborne Mario animation (2026-10-03)
DiagnosticMarioModel now selects four supplied MarioAnime clips: wait/run while grounded, jump while airborne with positive velocity along gravity up, and fall while descending. Clip changes reset the original J3D frame controller; remaining in the same state advances its existing frame. The scenario preview supplies post-collision body grounded status and velocity, so Space diagnostic jumps use airborne visuals automatically. This is a native visual state selector, not original Mario action logic, jump sequencing, landing animation or tuning.

Real-asset model smoke verifies run animation, rise/fall transitions, distinct skinned airborne poses, landing to idle/run, and continued-frame behavior without restarting. Full native build/self-tests and the eight-frame scenario model smoke pass (airborne-animation-build.log), including directional input, pixel upload/readback, gravity orientation and 274 active adapters. Existing diagnostic physics jump tests still pass.

The diagnostic Mario launcher uses this behavior. Original Mario gameplay, abilities, game boot and progression remain pending; no playable-port claim.

### Named main collision resources (2026-10-03)
Common/scenario collision loaders now prefer a KCL whose basename matches the placed model name (ASCII case-insensitive), then retain the previous single-resource fallback. Multiple nonmatching resources remain skipped; duplicate matching basenames are rejected. This follows PlanetMap::init calling initCollisionParts with mModelName and LiveActorUtil.cpp formatting that name as %s.kcl. It does not enable special movelimit/water collision behavior or reproduce dynamic Move-joint following.

BroadBeanPlanetC.arc contains broadbeanplanetc.kcl plus movelimit.kcl. Its 1,420-triangle main surface is now included rather than skipping the archive. EggStarGalaxy scenario 1 SoramameZone increases from 5 to 6 collision instances and 5,131 to 6,551 triangles; 49 records remain skipped. Verified 33 segment and 33 sphere checks, 120 body updates with grounding/no partial steps/no residual penetration, and the Mario model window smoke. Build/self-tests pass (main-collision-build.log), including preferred names, ambiguous/empty archives and single-resource compatibility.

This improves diagnostic collision coverage. Original Mario and game actor behavior, special collision semantics, boot and progression are pending; no playable-port claim.

### BTI texture address modes (2026-10-03)
BTI/J3D embedded textures now retain validated GX wrapS/wrapT metadata from the original ResTIMG offsets 6/7. The D3D mesh renderer selects clamp, repeat or mirrored repeat independently per axis for each texture batch instead of always repeating. Scene and animated Mario textures preserve this metadata when combined. Synthetic/raw/TPL textures retain the previous repeat default; TPL sampler metadata, filters, mipmaps and original TEV are not implemented here.

Build/self-tests, a complete disc graphics verification, explicit GPU clamp/repeat/mirror tests with UVs beyond 1, and scenario Mario hidden smoke pass. Mario material rendering covers 4,877 triangles and 137,994 geometry pixels with zero hardware/WARP mismatches; sample mario-wrap-modes.bmp and log texture-wrap-build.log saved in native/build. Samplers are reused within each render call; broader renderer resource retention remains pending.

This improves texture addressing in the diagnostic renderer, not original game playability. Mario actor behavior, game boot and progression remain pending.
