Common/scenario visual loaders now resolve Petari cCreateTable primary archive names before direct-name fallback. generate_factory_aliases.py extracts all 1,183 factory entries, validates complete parsing, and emits 927 non-null primary archive mappings in factory_archives.hpp. Lookup follows original ASCII case-insensitive object-name matching. ### Retained GPU texture samplers (2026-10-03)
MeshRenderCache now retains the nine possible S/T address-mode sampler combinations alongside the device, shaders and texture resources. Animated and scenario previews reuse each sampler after its first use rather than creating it again for every rendered frame. Cache reset releases samplers with the device.

Renderer checks verify identical sampler/device pointers across successive renders and exact pixel equality with uncached rendering. GX wrap/depth/alpha tests, native build/self-tests, Mario material render (zero hardware/WARP mismatches) and the scenario Mario hidden smoke pass. Log: sampler-cache-build.log; sample mario-retained-samplers.bmp. This removes one resource-creation cost but buffers, render targets and some pipeline objects are still per-frame, and CPU readback/display upload remain. No FPS performance claim is made.

MeshRenderCache now retains vertex/pixel shader objects, input layout, depth-stencil state and rasterizer state rather than recreating them every preview render. Shader bytecode and texture/sampler caching remain in place. Cache reset releases all device-dependent objects together.

MeshRenderCache retains color/depth textures, their views and CPU-readback staging texture at the current render dimensions. This optimizes the diagnostic renderer. Original Mario/game actor behavior, boot and progression are pending; the port is not playable.

### Reusable dynamic vertex buffer (2026-10-03)
MeshRenderCache now keeps a dynamic CPU-write vertex buffer, uploading current CPU-skinned/camera-transformed vertices with WRITE_DISCARD each frame. Capacity grows geometrically with checked D3D byte bounds and is retained for smaller draws. Allocation failures leave the previous buffer/capacity intact.

GPU checks verify same-capacity buffer reuse, changed UV uploads without stale vertices, larger-draw capacity growth with exact output, and reuse after reducing draw size. Build/self-tests, Mario material rendering (zero hardware/WARP mismatches), and the scenario Mario animation/input smoke pass. Log vertex-cache-build.log; sample mario-dynamic-vertices.bmp. CPU skinning, full-scene vertex copies, per-frame transfer and readback/display upload remain; no FPS claim.

Original Mario/game actor behavior, game boot and progression remain pending; the diagnostic preview is not a playable port.

### BTI minification/magnification filtering (2026-10-03)
BTI/J3D embedded textures now retain validated GX min/mag filter fields from ResTIMG offsets 20/21. D3D sampler selection maps independent nearest/linear minification and magnification modes, combined with the existing S/T wrap modes. The retained sampler cache now has 36 combinations. For GX mip-filter modes, the base-level nearest/linear component is respected; actual mip chains, LOD and anisotropy remain unimplemented.

Explicit GPU nearest-versus-linear tests, sampler/resource retention and resize checks, native build/self-tests, full-disc graphics metadata verification, Mario material rendering (zero hardware/WARP mismatches), and scenario Mario hidden smoke pass. ### Stored BTI mip chains (2026-10-03)
BTI/J3D textures now decode every stored image level using GX block-rounded byte strides and halved/clamped dimensions. Chains exceeding the legal dimension count or resource bounds are rejected. D3D uploads these levels as immutable subresources; sampler selection honors all six GX min filters and both mag filters, including nearest/linear mip blending. Non-mip filters clamp to the base level. Cache contains 108 wrap/filter combinations. LOD bias/ranges, anisotropy and TPL mip metadata remain pending.

Self-tests verify distinct per-level pixels, dimensions/offsets, truncated data and excessive levels. GPU tests use colored levels to verify minified draws select lower mip data and non-mip filters stay at base level. Complete-disc graphics verification, native build/self-tests, Mario material rendering (zero hardware/WARP mismatches) and scenario Mario hidden smoke pass. Logs mipmap-build.log and mipmap-graphics.log; samples mario-mipmaps.bmp and mario-mipmap-checked.bmp.

This improves diagnostic texture rendering; original Mario/game actor behavior, boot and progression remain pending. The port is not playable.

Added native GlobalSegmentGravity decoding and common/scenario field integration, following SegmentGravityCreator and SegmentGravity math: SRT-derived 1,000-unit Y endpoints, closest segment attraction, independently enabled endpoint gravity, rotated side-degree sectors, exclusive range, distant offset and inverse. Scenario parent rotation/translation transform endpoints and axes; scaled parent zones remain rejected. Shared selection uses segment distance for original inverse-square weighting and priority rules. Hemisphere extensions remain their original creator defaults (zero); follower/host activation and original gravity-manager execution remain pending.

Existing real EggStarGalaxy body and Mario window regressions pass; that tested zone has no segment records, so retail segment placement decoding/coverage is not claimed verified yet. Degenerate SRT axes are explicitly rejected.

Added `--verify-segment-gravity <game.wbfs>` to scan every root stage archive and decode GlobalSegmentGravity rows across all stored layers. ### Authored BTI LOD limits and bias (2026-10-03)
BTI/J3D textures now retain signed min/max LOD and bias fields, matching JUTTexture conversions (LOD /8, bias /100). The mesh sampler uses these values for mip filters; non-mip filters stay on level zero. Samplers are cached by the complete authored wrap/filter/LOD/bias key rather than the previous 108-entry filter-only array. Bias is bounded to D3D's supported range; GX edge-LOD, bias-clamp and anisotropy semantics remain pending.

Full-disc metadata verification passes with validated min<=max. Explicit colored-mip GPU checks verify an authored fixed LOD selects the requested level. Original NerveExecutor first-step timing triggers the native blend; the duration/interpolation are diagnostic choices, not reconstructed Mario action blend rules.

### Original sensor sphere overlap and bounded native ownership (2026-10-03)
Added an unchanged-method SensorHitChecker subset: constructor/destructor, movement, cross/same-group loops and checkAttack sphere overlap. It runs through NativeScene scheduling. Native initialization replaces the missing original connectToScene dependency, and clipping queries use a native message-host interface method. Original category-pair order, same-host exclusion, strict overlap (tangency excluded), validity checks and one-way eye-contact rules are preserved. ATYPE_EYE uses original value 0x7F.

NativeSensorWorld owns checker/sensors/groups, validates geometry, reserves per-category capacity before original unchecked group insertion, and releases original raw buffers. Native categories are supplied explicitly; original type-based initGroup routing, live category changes and automatic actor attack/message behavior remain pending.

Tests verify overlap, exact tangency, clipping, invalidation/revalidation, same-host exclusion, eye contacts, and rejection beyond the original 16-player group limit. Native build/self-tests and scenario Mario hidden smoke pass (sensor-overlap-build.log). The initial unresolved checker init dependency was replaced by the documented native scheduling adapter and the build rerun successfully. CMake sources updated, not locally verified.

This is original overlap logic exercised in a native sensor world; stage actors/Mario do not yet register gameplay sensors. Original gameplay interactions, Mario abilities, boot and progression remain pending.

### Original interpolation ratio adapter (2026-10-03 21:53 UTC)
NativeAnimationInterpolation adapts the scalar behavior of XanimePlayer::changeInterpoleFrame and updateInterpoleRatio. Changing a positive duration performs the original immediate ratio update. Subsequent updates add (1-ratio)/remaining and decrement remaining. Rate zero completes interpolation immediately except attribute 1, which continues the countdown. Zero duration completes directly. Native bounds require durations 0..600 and valid finite playback context.

### Active goal - Original scene coin reward methods

The native build now compiles unchanged ScenePlayingResult coin/purple coin methods and constructor/destructor extracted from pinned source. Native scoped hooks record life increment, one-up and purple-star requests; clamp uses the equivalent native integer operation. Coin counts clamp 0..999; crossing a fifty-coin quotient emits one life request per call even if a batch crosses several thresholds. Purple-star request occurs exactly at count 100. ### Active goal - Wii player-status save chunk container

Added bounded native encodeSaveChunks/decodeSaveChunks adapting BinaryDataChunkHolder file layout: four-byte version/count header followed by big-endian signature/hash/size headers and serialized payloads. Player status uses original metadata and seven-byte native Wii-endian payload. Loader validates complete headers, ranges, known hashes and duplicate known chunks before deserializing; unknown chunks are skipped. It accepts trailing bytes like the original layout. Payload workspace is limited to 4 KiB per chunk. This is portable container code, not unchanged original pointer-casting implementation; disk persistence and other game-data chunks remain pending. Arbitrary chunk deserializer failures after validation are not rolled back.

Original texture-list offsets are relative to the entry-array base, confirmed against the extracted authored layout; a local reference copy is stored in build/wipekoopa-layout-reference.brlyt. This is resource binding, not original nw4r Material execution or TEV shader evaluation.

Validation: build.ps1/self-tests passed (build/wipe-material-texture-binding-build.log). Actual-disc wipe verifier resolves content material WinKoopaC to ImWipeKoopa.tpl (128x204) and frame material WinKoopaLT to Blank.tpl (8x8), each with one authored texture SRT. ### Active goal - Authored wipe animation tracks

Added bounded BRLAN AnimationContent/Info/Target and Hermite/Step key decoding from pinned nw4r resource structures. Native curve sampling preserves ordered duplicate key times for distinct incoming/outgoing tangents and supports finite pre-roll/post-roll keys outside the playback duration. It evaluates Hermite slopes and step values with endpoint clamping. This is a native curve evaluator; original nw4r animation execution and exact parity for all resources are not claimed. Applying targets to pane/material state remains pending.

Validation: corrected build.ps1/self-tests passed (build/wipe-animation-track-build.log). Tests cover Hermite interpolation/clamping, duplicate-time outgoing tangents and step boundaries. Initial range check rejected authored In alpha pre-roll at frame -20; this was corrected based on the resource before verification passed. Extracted In/Out reference files are saved under build. Material/pane application and visual rendering remain pending; the game remains not playable.
### Active goal - Animated wipe pane poses

It is a native adapter rather than compiled original nw4r target application. Scenario runtime now retains full closing-wipe resources/tracks and exposes closingWipePose at the original frame controller's current frame, ready for a renderer. Base resources remain unchanged between samples.

Validation: build.ps1/self-tests passed (build/wipe-pane-animation-build.log), including immutable source state, size/alpha interpolation and missing-pane rejection. Hierarchical screen transforms, window geometry, material shading and visual rendering remain pending; the game remains not playable.
### Active goal - Hierarchical wipe layout geometry

Added native 2D hierarchical pane transforms in authored layout coordinates: parent affine composition, Z rotation, nonuniform scale, base-position anchors, parent visibility and influenced-alpha propagation. Results retain pane corner positions and world matrices for a renderer. Parent order and coordinate finiteness are validated. ### Active goal - Single-frame wipe window mesh

Added native single-frame window quad generation with content inflation, texture-sized borders, authored material/UV/color references, transformed positions and alpha. Content plus four non-overlapping border strips follow the nw4r single-frame topology described by primary implementation https://github.com/doldecomp/ogws/blob/master/src/nw4r/lyt/lyt_window.cpp; this is a native adaptation, not compiled Petari Window execution or proven cross-version visual parity. Multi-frame windows and unsupported texture/UV bindings explicitly reject pending implementation. Zero-area content is omitted when the closing wipe reaches the combined border size.

Validation: build.ps1/self-tests passed (build/wipe-window-mesh-build.log). Independent geometry test checks five quads tile a 30x30 window and content collapses at 16x16 with 8-unit borders. Actual-disc --verify-wipe-window-mesh checks authored animated quad counts and total tiled area at frames 0/45/90: five/five/four quads. ### Authored wipe material shading
Implemented a bounded native floating-point adaptation of the default single-texture material path: authored TEV register interpolation, stage clamping, vertex color modulation and inherited pane alpha. Optional/explicit material configurations are rejected rather than silently approximated. This does not reproduce GX integer rounding.
Validation: native build and self-tests passed (build/wipe-material-shading-build.log). --verify-wipe-material-shading checked all 26,176 decoded pixels from the supplied WipeKoopa archive: content alpha 0..148 and blank frame alpha 148 at pane alpha 148, with black RGB from the authored registers. Texture sampling, viewport mapping and visible wipe rendering remain pending. The game is not playable; original single-player runtime remains the priority.

### Authored wipe texture sampling
Preserved TPL nearest/linear filtering in the layout asset loader and corrected layout texture-map fields to plain wrap modes. Material wrapping overrides the TPL defaults, matching the nw4r material resource path (https://github.com/doldecomp/ogws/blob/master/src/nw4r/lyt/lyt_material.cpp). The bounded loader rejects mipmapped/nonzero-LOD layout textures until supported. Added base-level clamp/repeat/mirror sampling with texel-centered bilinear interpolation, including floating-point alpha passed to material shading without intermediate byte rounding.
Validation: build and native self-tests passed (build/wipe-texture-sampling-build.log). --verify-wipe-texture-sampling passed all 26,176 authored texel centers: content wrap mirror/clamp, border clamp/clamp, both linear min/mag filtering. The authored material shading regression also passed. These remain native adapters; GX fixed-point sampling parity and visible wipe output are not verified. Next: viewport mapping and composition into the native rendered frame. Original single-player gameplay and full campaign are still incomplete.

### Visible animated wipe composition

Next: connect diagnostic fatal-area handling to the original event sequence and staged retry; it currently still retries immediately. Original MarioActor gameplay, miss UI rendering and retail scene retry remain unported. The game is not playable.

### Staged fatal-area retry in the native preview
Connected authored fatal-area detection to NativeScenarioActors::beginFatalSequenceAt instead of immediate diagnostic teleport. The diagnostic body freezes motion, coin collection, checkpoint updates and control/reset/retry requests while original event and layout actors continue ticking. Original frame-120 life loss and authored miss/wipe completion now gate retry. The closed frame must be rendered before retry can advance, including catch-up simulation batches. The existing authored checkpoint restoration then restores position/facing, clears motion and reopens/hides the closing wipe through the original WipeKoopa forceOpen method. NativeGameOver completion remains frozen; a retail game-over screen/scene transition is not implemented.

All 274 actor adapters maintained simulation cadence. --verify-scenario-death-sequence also passed.
This is diagnostic body integration with original event timing, not retail MarioActor death animation, original scene reconstruction or full gameplay. Miss UI rendering, game-over UI and original MarioActor runtime remain incomplete. Full single-player/campaign remains the priority; the game is not playable.

### Original Mario runtime compilation probe
Added probe-mario-runtime.ps1 to compile-check the pinned original Mario.cpp, MarioActor.cpp and MarioJump.cpp against the current native headers. It saves separate diagnostics and machine-readable compiler exit codes under build/mario-runtime-probe, and returns failure when any source fails. This is an integration diagnostic, not a gameplay test. All three currently fail: embedded JGeometry matrices are still opaque, TVec2f is missing, and additional native GX/SDK declarations are needed before original runtime compilation can progress.
Implemented the missing NO_INLINE mapping as the native compiler noinline attribute; rerunning the probe removed those declaration errors. Next gameplay integration work should implement the original matrix/vector operations and required SDK interfaces, then expose the remaining source/link dependencies. Do not replace original Mario behavior with the diagnostic body controller or count probe success alone as playable gameplay.
Validation: the existing native build and complete self-test command passed after the NO_INLINE change (build/mario-runtime-header-build.log). The original Mario compilation probes remain failing as documented; no new gameplay availability is claimed.

### Native JGeometry affine matrix core
Replaced opaque JGeometry matrix declarations with native 3x4 storage and scalar implementations adapted from the pinned source: copy/access, identity, affine concatenation, determinant, point/transpose transforms, rotation basis access, rotation-only transforms, translation and separate full/rotation-only scaling. Concatenation and vector transforms support aliased input/output. Added TVec2 storage and basic set/add/min/max/comparison operations required by Mario.hpp. Matrix constructors preserve source uninitialized semantics; callers initialize explicitly. Full matrix inverse, quaternion/Euler construction and the wider original vector API remain pending. Console ABI and paired-single rounding parity are not claimed.
Validation: native build and all self-tests passed (build/native-matrix-core-build.log), including noncommuting rotation/scale/translation composition, both aliased concat operands, aliased point transforms, transpose round trip, translation preservation during rotation-only scaling and determinant behavior. Scenario Mario smoke passed with 274 actor adapters after the header replacement. Original Mario/MarioActor/MarioJump probes no longer report incomplete matrices, missing TVec2f or missing Vec. They still fail on SDK graphics headers (gx.h/gd.h); Mario also reaches legacy NULL dependencies. These are the next compilation integration steps, followed by remaining math methods and actual runtime/link dependencies. The original Mario runtime and full campaign are still not playable; the full project goal remains active.

### Original SDK header discovery and native vector arithmetic
The graphics headers reported missing by the Mario probe exist in the pinned Petari libs/RVL_SDK/include tree. The probe now includes that tree after native overrides, plus cstddef for legacy NULL declarations; no Wii SDK functions are linked or emulated by this include-path change. Probes progress beyond gx.h/gd.h into original J3D/compiler assumptions: __memcpy/__REGISTER, reciprocal-square-root intrinsics, legacy initialization/linkage and additional geometry APIs.
Expanded native TVec3 with scalar construction, set/zero, arithmetic and component multiplication, scalar scale, dot, negate and interpolation, including in-place overloads. Interpolation follows pinned JMAVECLerp's a + (b-a)*t expression; native scalar rounding is not console paired-single equivalence. Added checks for known vector results and aliased operands to the math regression suite. Quaternion, reciprocal-square-root and the full original vector/matrix surface still require implementation before actual Mario runtime integration.
Validation: native build/all self-tests passed (build/native-vector-core-build.log), including the added vector checks; scenario Mario smoke passed with 274 actor adapters. All three original Mario source probes still return failure, with detailed logs under build/mario-runtime-probe. Original gameplay/full campaign remain unplayable; the goal stays active.

### Native SDK compiler support declarations
Added native ATTRIBUTE_ALIGN and empty __REGISTER mappings, cstddef/cstring prerequisites and an inline __memcpy implementation for the pinned SDK's non-overlapping, nonnegative-size object copies. Alignment retains real host compiler semantics rather than being erased. Added regression checks for 32-byte alignment, returned destination pointers, bounded copy sentinels and zero-length preservation.
Rerunning the original Mario probes removed the __REGISTER, __memcpy and ATTRIBUTE_ALIGN parsing errors. Remaining visible failures include original J3D C/C++ linkage conflicts, reciprocal-square-root intrinsics, missing quaternion types and native LiveActor virtual interface gaps. ### Native quaternion math foundation
Added TVec4/TQuat4 storage, constructors/set/scaling, dot/squared norm, normalization with original near-zero identity fallback, alias-safe quaternion multiplication, Euler construction, basis extraction and 3x3 matrix output. The one-argument multiply retains original pre-multiplication order. Matrix output leaves translation untouched, matching the original routine. Native normalization uses host sqrt instead of console reciprocal-square-root estimate/refinement; console bit-exact rounding and full quaternion API remain unverified/incomplete.
Added checks for identity fallback, quarter-turn basis, noncommuting rotation composition, in-place multiplication/normalization and matrix translation preservation. Original Mario probes now resolve TQuat4f and progress into actual MarioJump.cpp method requirements. Remaining visible work includes reciprocal-square-root/absolute-value intrinsics, additional vector operations/types, J3D linkage issues and the native actor interface. This foundation does not integrate the retail MarioActor runtime or make the game playable.

Scenario Mario smoke passed with 274 actor adapters after quaternion integration. Original source probes remain failing on the documented remaining dependencies; full single-player gameplay is not playable and the goal remains active.

### MarioJump vector length and cross-product dependencies
Added native squared magnitude/distance, length/distance, cross products, near-zero predicate and both setLength forms, plus original signed vector aliases. In-place setLength retains a near-zero source unchanged; the source/destination overload zeroes the destination, following the pinned implementation's distinct behavior. Host square root remains a documented numerical adaptation rather than console rounding parity. Regression checks cover known 3-4-5 length, distance, old-length return, target magnitude, handed cross product and aliased operands, and both near-zero paths.
Original MarioJump probe no longer reports missing length/cross or TVec3s and reaches later function bodies. It exposes an important 64-bit adaptation: startTornadoCentering stores HitSensor* in u32 _A38 and taskOnTornadoCentering reads it back. Do not suppress this diagnostic or truncate pointers; the native task context must retain the actual pointer safely when integrating this runtime. Other remaining errors include compiler intrinsics, native LiveActor fields, SDK vector-pointer conversions and scalar division. Source still does not compile/link as the retail runtime; the port remains unplayable.
Validation: native build and all self-tests passed (build/native-vector-length-build.log), including the added vector length/cross and near-zero assertions. Original Mario source probes remain failing on the documented integration dependencies. The full gameplay/campaign objective remains active and unachieved.

### Scalar division and SDK vector object conversions
Added original TVec3 scalar division/operator-divide assignment semantics (reciprocal followed by scale), and float-vector conversion to SDK Vec pointers. Compile-time checks cover size/alignment/member offsets; regression checks cover quotient values and const/mutable converted pointer reads. These conversions apply to vector objects, not pointers to vector objects. MarioJump's PSMTXRotAxisRad/PSMTXMultVecSR calls pass TVec3f* directly, so their native adapter signatures remain unresolved; do not claim these SDK call errors are fixed by the object conversion.

### Native math compiler intrinsic adaptations
Added absolute-value intrinsics matching pinned declarations, FLT_EPSILON prerequisites and a host reciprocal-square-root implementation. Original TUtil callers retain their refinement expression; the native estimate is 1/sqrt(x), not the PPC estimate table. Floating-point parity remains unverified. Signed absolute value explicitly preserves INT_MIN's PPC wrap result without invoking host overflow; float absolute value clears negative-zero sign. Checks cover finite values, signed zero, INT_MIN, reciprocal-root zero/negative domains and the original refinement expression.
The original MarioJump probe now reports only two compile errors across the full file: its tornado sensor pointer cast to u32 and native LiveActor's missing mName field. Mario/MarioActor still have separate original integration dependencies; neither linking nor playable gameplay is established.

### Native NW4R arithmetic helpers
Replaced console assembly in the native NW4R arithmetic override with scalar absolute value and conditional selection; both signed zeros select the positive branch, and NaN selects the negative branch. The initial standalone test build exposed remaining SDK fast-cast assembly, so integer conversions were adapted too: exact u16-to-float and scale-zero clamped/truncated float-to-u16. NaN integer conversion explicitly throws because retail NaN behavior is not verified. Quantized conversion reference: https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/Interpreter/Interpreter_LoadStorePaired.cpp (ScaleAndClamp); this is a math reference, not an emulator runtime dependency.
Validation: standalone nw4r_arithmetic_tests.cpp built and ran with exit 0, covering conditional-selection finite/zero/NaN/infinity cases, absolute value, all 65,536 u16 round trips and clamp/truncation/NaN rejection. Original Mario probe clears arithmetic.h assembly errors and reaches further NW4R math/SDK dependencies. Full generated MarioJump still compiles to an object. Existing native build/all self-tests passed (build/nw4r-arithmetic-runtime-build.log). Original full runtime/gameplay remains incomplete and unplayable; the project goal stays active.

Other original controller, actor, matrix and SDK dependencies still prevent full runtime compilation/linking and gameplay.
Validation: native build/all self-tests passed (build/sdk-point-transform-build.log), including full point-transform checks. Scenario Mario smoke passed with 274 actor adapters. Original full runtime/campaign remains unplayable; the project goal stays active.

### Guarded native Mario controller source adaptation
Extended native-source generation to Mario.cpp with exact guards on its two invalid bool/nullptr comparisons. normalizeOrZero returns true when it zeroes a near-zero vector and false after normalizing; the original branch behavior is preserved by comparing to false. Added ARRAY_SIZE through a constexpr fixed-array reference helper, avoiding pointer-size counting. Original Petari files remain unchanged.
The generated Mario probe clears the bool/nullptr and array-count errors and reaches controller binder access and a further mixed SDK-vector call. Remaining dependencies include actor/binder state, projection/Euler APIs, 64-bit SDK pointer assumptions and additional native call adapters. Generated MarioJump still produces an object; Mario.cpp itself remains uncompiled and unlinked as the full retail runtime. Full gameplay remains incomplete.
Validation: existing native build/all self-tests passed (build/mario-bool-array-build.log); generated Mario probes confirm the targeted errors are cleared while later integration failures remain visible. No original gameplay availability is claimed; the project goal remains active.

### Native actor gravity/binder storage and mixed SDK call
Added native LiveActor gravity storage initialized to (0,-1,0) and a null Binder reference, following original LiveActor construction defaults. These fields support original controller data access; original Binder allocation, collision solving and lifecycle are still unported and no placeholder binder behavior was added. Added the SDK rotation/scale transform overload for an object source and native vector pointer destination, including in-place coverage.
The original generated Mario probe now clears all gravity/binder field and reached matrix-call errors and reports nine remaining errors in projection/Euler APIs, DVD loader header overloads, NW4R list/compiler syntax and pointer-width SDK assumptions. Full generated MarioJump still compiles to an object. Runtime actor gravity sampling and original binder integration require further work; the new fields alone do not establish gameplay.
Validation: native build/all self-tests passed (build/mario-gravity-binder-storage-build.log), including the mixed transform check. Scenario Mario smoke passed with 274 actor adapters. The original full Mario runtime and campaign remain unplayable; the goal stays active.

### Native SDK/NW4R pointer-width adaptations
Added native NW4R IntPtr/PtrDiff aliases using uintptr_t/ptrdiff_t. The guarded generator now adapts LinkList's invalid friend-class typedef declaration and SDK heap UIntPtr storage to native pointer width. Intrusive-list node/object conversion and heap pointer add/subtract retain full Windows addresses. Original u32 heap offset APIs still have their original bounded offset domain; this does not implement or validate a native allocator. Retail NW4R debug console-address assertions still require native adaptation before enabling that mode.
Validation: standalone sdk_pointer_tests.cpp built and ran with exit 0, checking pointer widths, mutable/const node-object round trips with nonzero node offset, actual heap pointer arithmetic and offset results. Existing native build/all self-tests passed (build/sdk-pointer-width-build.log). Original generated Mario probe now reports five remaining compile errors: TProj3f, matrix getEuler and three DVD loader overload declarations. Full MarioJump still compiles; Mario/MarioActor runtime linking and gameplay/campaign remain incomplete. The project goal stays active.

### Fixed-width native DVD loader declarations
The three JKRDvdRipper wrapper errors were distinct host C++ types: native u32* versus original unsigned-long output pointers. Added a guarded native header adaptation using u32/s32 in loader declarations, retaining the original fixed-width size/entry-number domain. This changes the native API type surface, not loader behavior; a future native backend must implement these signatures. No silent DVD-load success or empty-data stub was added. Pinned Petari files remain unchanged.
Validation: dvd_api_probe.cpp compiled with exact function-pointer types for filename, entry-number and file-object overloads. Native build/all self-tests passed (build/dvd-api-native-build.log). Original generated Mario now reports only TProj3f and getEuler compile errors, and generated MarioJump still produces an object. Loader linking, original Mario runtime and complete campaign gameplay remain unimplemented/unverified; the goal remains active.

### Full native Mario controller object and projection/Euler foundation
Added matrix Euler extraction following the pinned XYZ convention and both singular branches, using host atan2/asin; degree output is available too. Added native 4x4 matrix/projection storage and the original perspective-depth convention, focal-length/power helpers and copy constructor. These are actual math operations, not opaque stand-ins; full projection API and console lookup/rounding parity remain incomplete/unverified.
Generated full Mario.cpp now compiles with exit 0 to build/mario-runtime-probe/Mario.obj (449,535 bytes), alongside MarioJump. MarioActor still fails on native actor lifecycle/API gaps. Neither controller object is linked into galaxy-probe, and their construction, task dispatch, binder interactions and gameplay remain unverified. Added checks for general Euler round trip, both singular rotations and perspective near/far depth/aspect behavior. Original complete gameplay/campaign is still unplayable; the full project goal remains active.
Validation: native build/all self-tests passed (build/mario-projection-euler-build.log), including Euler/projection checks. Scenario Mario smoke passed with 274 actor adapters. Object compilation is a foundation milestone, not evidence of retail Mario gameplay availability.

### Original Mario movement, state/task and collision compilation
Expanded the baseline native runtime probe to fifteen original units: Mario, MarioActor, MarioJump, MarioActorInit, MarioModule, MarioState, MarioTask, MarioMove, MarioMove2D, MarioMove25D, MarioMoveSphere, MarioInit, MarioActorGravity, MarioCollision and MarioFlip. The new optional probe-mario-runtime.ps1 -AllPlayer mode inventories every pinned Mario*.cpp separately in build/mario-runtime-all-player. ### JMath table namespace and full retail SDK declarations
The guarded native generator relocates the original custom std::pair table cell to JMath::TrigTablePair. It preserves a1/b1 storage and all original trig lookup operations, avoiding conflict with the host C++ library. Standalone jmath_pair_tests.cpp passes, checking eight-byte cells, offsets 0/4, 16384-cell table size, zero initialization and coexistence with real std::pair. This change does not generate or validate console lookup-table contents.

Retail runtime probes now use the complete pinned revolution.h umbrella through include-runtime, rather than the diagnostic matrix-only umbrella. Native types are preincluded outside SDK extern-C blocks to avoid giving host C++ templates C linkage. This supplies genuine SDK declarations; it does not implement OS/GX services. The refreshed full scan compiles 69 of 88 original Mario units; 19 still fail. Native build/all self-tests pass (build/jmath-retail-sdk-build.log). Existing diagnostic interfaces remain separate.

Added audit-mario-link.ps1 to parse standard x64 COFF symbol records with bounds checks and case-sensitive symbol names, skip auxiliary records, and inventory only units whose current compilation succeeded. It writes build/mario-runtime-all-player/link-dependencies.json. The report is a raw cross-object dependency inventory, not a link test: SDK/libraries and failed units are excluded, so listed unresolved references are expected integration work. Full runtime linking, retail actor/binder/model ownership and original gameplay are still incomplete. No playability is established.

### Pointer-safe original material-effect hash lookup
Implemented native HashSortTable with owned arrays and pointer-sized payload storage, preserving 32-bit hash keys, original MR::sortSmall selection ordering (including duplicate-key order), and upper-byte bucket lookup. Existing integer add/search APIs remain available for animation indices. Explicit addPointer/searchPointer APIs avoid address truncation and writes through an aliased u32 pointer. The guarded MarioEffect source adaptation uses these APIs at its one insertion and two material-effect searches; original effect tables and branches remain intact.

Safety behavior is explicit: capacity exhaustion returns false, capacities beyond the original u16 bucket domain are rejected, searches require sorting after mutation, and an integer lookup rejects payloads that cannot fit u32. Renaming a key marks buckets dirty and requires re-sort. Arrays are released by the native destructor and copying is disabled. These are host ownership/bounds adaptations; no console heap ownership is claimed. MR::getHashCode retains the existing original implementation; lowercase hashing is still outside this backend's implemented scope.

Registered hash_sort_table_port.cpp in build.ps1 and CMake. Native build/all self-tests pass (build/native-pointer-hash-build.log), covering actual native pointer round trips, misses, capacity, duplicate hash collision/skip behavior, selection-order parity, bucket endpoints, combined-name hashes, integer narrowing rejection and mutation guards. Scenario diagnostic smoke passes with 274 adapters (build/native-pointer-hash-smoke.log). CMake itself remains unverified locally.

Full original MarioEffect now compiles into a native Windows object. Latest complete Mario source inventory is 70/88 successful, with 18 remaining failures; the link-dependency inventory was refreshed. Runtime objects are still not linked into retail gameplay, and original effect emitters, full actor/backend ownership and campaign execution remain incomplete. The game is not playable; the full goal stays active.

The animator clears its missing comparison API and now fails on a separate remaining TVec2 operation.

Latest complete original Mario inventory: 80/88 units compile to Windows objects, with eight remaining failures in drawing allocation, animator vectors, audio allocator size types, swimming math macros and warp template syntax. ### Native display-list buffer ring ownership
Added include-runtime/Game/Player/DLchanger.hpp as an actual native owner for the retail display-list ring. It retains DLholder field types, buffer-count/current-index fields and the original advance-before-return rotation. Buffer arrays use standard 32-byte aligned byte allocation with matching aligned release; the holder array is zero-initialized and freed. Constructor failure releases already allocated buffers, zero-buffer construction is rejected, and copying is disabled to prevent double ownership. The original addDL implementation remains in MarioActorDraw.cpp and is still blocked by other drawing dependencies.

Standalone test-dlchanger.ps1 passes (build/dlchanger-tests-run.log), using the actual runtime header. Tests cover 1/2/3/255 buffers, 32-byte alignment, first/last-byte writes and retained contents over two complete rotations, zero-count rejection, normal destruction with zero live aligned allocations and injected failure on the third allocation with complete cleanup. The test allocator delegates to real host aligned allocation/release while counting and injecting failure; it is not part of production code.

The refreshed original Mario scan remains 83/88 successful. Both drawing probes clear the DLchanger constructor allocation error, while their own aligned arrays, draw functors and other dependencies still fail. This header is isolated to retail runtime probes; the existing diagnostic executable is unaffected. Retail drawing/GX integration and playable gameplay remain incomplete. The full goal remains active.

### Original shadow look-at matrix operation
Implemented the three-vector TPosition3::setPositionFromLookAt operation reached by CollisionShadow::setViewMtx. It retains the original front negation, up/front cross-product order, normalization order, row basis and negative eye translation. Host sqrt replaces console normalization estimates; bit-exact rounding parity and degenerate coincident-eye/parallel-up behavior remain unverified. No alternate fallback camera is introduced.

Regression checks pass for translated axis-aligned and angled views: the eye maps to origin, the target maps along negative camera Z, and the rotation determinant is one. Full Mario compilation remains 83/88; shadow rendering/GX and retail gameplay integration are incomplete. The full goal remains active and no playability is established.

### Native audio pool allocator size declarations
Guarded JASHeapCtrl generation changes both original single/multithreaded pool operator-new and sized-delete parameters from u32 to std::size_t, as required by the native C++ allocation ABI. Allocation checks the native size against u32 maximum before forwarding the unchanged original memPool_.alloc implementation. Sized delete forwards the corresponding validated allocation size through explicit u32 conversion. No pool implementation or heap semantics are replaced.

Standalone audio_pool_size_tests.cpp passes, checking actual template allocator function signatures (including implicitly noexcept sized deletes) and zero, ordinary, u32-maximum and overflowing native size boundaries. The test does not allocate from or validate the unported audio pools. The full MarioSound probe clears both allocator-signature errors and remains failing on audio geometry/templates/iterator headers. Complete Mario compilation remains 87/88. Diagnostic source/build interfaces were unaffected; original audio runtime linking and audible playback remain incomplete. The full project goal stays active and no playability is established.

### Native audio intrusive iterator and pointer-array headers
Guarded JGadget linklist generation removes its conflicting custom declarations in namespace std and uses the host C++17 iterator tags/empty metadata base from <iterator>. Original intrusive node conversion, links and traversal methods remain intact. Guarded JASGadget generation explicitly names JASPtrTable<T> in the dependent-base constructor, retaining pointer-array storage and memset/get/set behavior.

Standalone audio_container_tests.cpp passes with actual generated headers and native std::vector iterators. It verifies nonzero-offset intrusive node/object round trips, forward/backward/post-decrement traversal and assignment, plus audio pointer-array zero initialization, native pointer storage/lookup, backing-array identity and out-of-range get. Full insertion/removal/list lifetime and audio playback are outside this test.

Refreshed MarioSound compilation clears iterator namespace conflicts and dependent-base/table lookup errors. It now reports five errors: audio view matrix overload/normalization and sequence pointer-offset narrowing. Complete Mario compilation remains 87/88. The original audio pool, sound engine and platform backend remain unlinked; full gameplay is not playable and the goal stays active.

Native audio sequence milestone: JASSeqReader now decodes 16/24/32-bit big-endian fields from bytes, including unaligned addresses, and checks pointer-sized cursor differences against the original u32 offset domain. The generated original sequence implementation passes standalone tests for call-stack limits, return, counted loops, MIDI variable-length values, and offset overflow. Sequence reads still rely on caller-provided buffer bounds; this does not implement audio playback. The latest original Mario source scan compiles 87/88 units; MarioSound has three remaining geometry API errors. These runtime objects remain separate from the diagnostic executable and do not establish playable gameplay.

Audio listener geometry milestone: implemented the pinned JGeometry one-matrix setPositionFromLookAt coordinate conversion and vector normalize overloads using native scalar math. Standalone tests cover explicit transform coefficients, translation, aliasing, normalization magnitude and direction. Console paired-single rounding and degenerate-vector parity remain unverified. All 88 original Mario source units now compile to native Windows objects (zero failed units). The COFF inventory reports 2,775 definitions and 723 unresolved symbols; this is a dependency inventory, not a successful runtime link. The diagnostic build and its self-tests pass after the shared math changes. Full original runtime linking, platform backends and gameplay remain incomplete.

Original collision runtime probe: probe-collision-runtime.ps1 compiles nine original subsystem sources with the full runtime actor headers. Eight now compile, including Binder, HitInfo and CollisionParts, after adding tested column-length matrix scale extraction (rotation, signed scale and ignored translation covered). KCollision still fails on goto scope and SDK vector call compatibility. More importantly, its KCLFile pointer/offset unions and raw binary overlay do not match a 64-bit host or big-endian game file. Compile success for the other units does not validate runtime loading or collisions. Next integration work must decode KCL into owned native structures before wiring original collision and Mario objects into gameplay.

KCollision compilation adaptation: all nine collision probe units now compile. A guarded generator scopes the finish-path distance local so existing success jumps are legal C++, and replaces three SDK vector calls with the native typed subtract/dot operations. Original collision branch decisions are retained; generated source remains separate from Petari. This is compilation evidence only: original KCL binary overlays, octree decoding, native ownership and gameplay integration are still pending. No original collision runtime object has been linked into the diagnostic executable.

Native KCL octree traversal: native_kcl_octree.hpp owns big-endian octree bytes and follows the original root-index, child-relative-offset and reserved-halfword leaf conventions without host pointer overlays. Bounded synthetic tests pass for root and nested leaves, a selected child octant, prism range checks, truncated lists and invalid shifts. This is not yet wired to original KCollisionServer and has not been verified against extracted game octrees. Full native prism/vector ownership and loader integration remain pending.

Extracted-game octree verification: native-kcl-octree-tests.exe now accepts a local KCL file and reads its big-endian header, mask/shift fields and octree into the owned native traversal. Both existing extracted BroadBean assets pass 4,096 deterministic in-bounds cell lookups: broadbean.kcl has 1,216 prisms / 8,235 sampled references; broadbean-c.kcl has 1,420 prisms / 8,177 sampled references. All returned prism indices are checked against the file prism count. This validates traversal extents and decoding on those assets, not collision hit equivalence, exhaustive coverage, original-server integration or full gameplay.

Owned native KCL storage: native_kcl_storage.hpp decodes big-endian vectors, original KC_PrismData records, thickness, minimum, masks and shifts into native allocations. Reserved prism zero is maintained; triangle count is explicit rather than subtracting pointers into unrelated allocations. Both BroadBean assets pass finite-value/vector-index validation, per-prism attribute comparisons against disk bytes, independent-copy allocation checks and the existing 4,096-cell octree checks. The storage uses original TVec3f/KC_PrismData types, but is not yet bound into KCollisionServer; raw original setData and searchBlock must be replaced before that integration can be used.

Stable native octree leaves: nativeLookup caches native-endian reserved-halfword/prism/zero-terminator lists by original byte offset, providing stable pointer identity for original collision traversal and shared-leaf skip comparisons. Synthetic tests verify shared leaf identity and independently owned copies. Each of the 4,096 sampled cells on both BroadBean assets compares the returned native list against byte-decoded prism indices and checks its terminator. Original KCollisionServer wiring remains pending; this does not yet validate collision hit behavior.

Original collision-server binding: generated runtime KCollisionServer now owns decoded storage through setNativeData(bytes), constructs a native pointer view for original vector/prism access, uses an explicit triangle count and delegates searchBlock to stable native-endian octree leaves. Copy operations are disabled because its mFile points at its own native view. All nine collision units compile after this change. The legacy lengthless setData rejects use explicitly; original asset-loading callers must migrate to bounded setNativeData. Server construction/linking and original collision hit tests are not yet verified, and these objects are not linked to the diagnostic executable. This remains an incomplete runtime integration milestone.

Original collision link test in progress: added bounded-data server construction accepting a caller-owned attribute map, and a test that calls the actual original getPos/KCHitArrow implementations on every positive-height prism. Native SDK declaration copies remove header-defined MMIO globals for this test via explicit preincludes. The executable has not linked: remaining original math/map/metadata dependencies are unresolved. No collision-hit test has passed yet. test-original-kcl-server.ps1 captures the current linker diagnostics; test-only scalar SDK dot math is provided in the test translation unit. Native constructor compile succeeds; full runtime integration remains incomplete.

Collision link dependency progress: extracted three exact original MathUtil definitions (vector/scalar isNearZero and isSameDirection) into generated CollisionMath.cpp; native object compilation passes. These preserve original boundary comparisons rather than substituting vector magnitude tolerance. Linked server tests still fail on additional original scene/math dependencies. Per-game-object LTO avoids the bundled runtime LTO symbol failure, but has not completed the link. No original collision hit result is verified yet.

Original collision kernel verification: test-original-kcl-server.ps1 now extracts exact named method bodies from the generated runtime source for a focused linked test, avoiding unrelated scene dependencies without stubbing those services. Actual getPos/calXvec/KCHitArrow methods pass forward prism-center line hits at t approximately 0.5 and reverse/backface misses for all 1,216 BroadBean and 1,420 BroadBean-C prisms. Native bounded constructor, data binding and count/access methods are included. This is a subset-method link test, not a full KCollision runtime link; metadata, scene collision registration, broadphase hit behavior and Mario gameplay remain unverified. Full generated runtime source remains maintained separately.

Original octree line-query verification: linked exact checkArrow/V3u conversion method bodies plus original KCollisionPlus coordinate routines and original tolerance/scalar-direction math. Native vector normalization replaces PSVECNormalize with host scalar normalization; console rounding parity remains unverified. For all 2,636 prism-center forward segments across both extracted assets, octree-backed original checkArrow agrees with an all-prism original KCHitArrow scan on hit presence and nearest hit fraction within 0.002. Forward/backface tests continue passing. This validates these local queries on sampled segments, not all rays, sphere binding, stage transforms, scene metadata, full subsystem linking or Mario gameplay.

Original sphere kernel verification: exact KCHitSphere now links into the focused original-method test with a host scalar MR::fastSqrtf backend. Across all 2,636 prisms in both BroadBean assets, radius-10 spheres at centroid plus normal*5 report face contacts with penetration approximately 5 (tolerance 0.01); centroid plus normal*20 reports no contact. Original line and broadphase/reference comparisons still pass. This covers face-interior contact/separation only; edge/corner regions, thickness variant, sphere broadphase, Binder response and gameplay are pending. Host sqrt is not console estimate/rounding parity.

Original sphere broadphase verification: focused linked test now executes exact checkSphere against owned native octree lists. For radius-10 spheres above each of the 2,636 prism centroids, every prism's contact presence is compared with an all-prism KCHitSphere scan; returned penetration depths agree within 0.01 and contact flags match. All comparisons pass on both BroadBean assets, with the existing face/separation and line tests. This verifies sampled local sphere queries, not arbitrary contacts, thickness variant, transformed stage geometry, Binder collision response, full runtime linking or playable gameplay.

Bounded CollisionParts initialization: generated initNative accepts owned KCL bytes and calls setNativeData while retaining the original matrix, zone, camera-code, bounding-range and keeper setup. Its metadata pointer still uses original JMapInfo::attach and requires native metadata adaptation. All nine collision units compile and focused original collision tests pass after this addition. This entry point has not run in a native stage; asset creation callers, metadata services, CollisionDirector and Binder integration remain incomplete.

Original JMapInfo compilation milestone: generated source scopes its string-pointer switch local and moves the signed-value address initialization before its failure branch; original metadata operations otherwise retained. Native object compilation passes. This is not native binary decoding: attach still overlays raw data and native numeric accesses need endian/alignment/bounds adaptation. Existing StageTable provides bounded big-endian diagnostic reads but is not yet connected to original JMapInfo API.

Native JMap storage conversion: native_jmap_storage.hpp validates bounded header/field/row extents and strings, converts descriptors and numeric/string-offset fields to host endian, and owns the resulting image for original JMapInfo::attach. Original accessors link with original JGadget hash code. A three-cell integer/float/string-pointer fixture passes exact comparisons; the extracted beach-objinfo table has zero rows and validates only empty-table/header handling. Nonempty game-table verification, unaligned original access adaptation, lifetime binding to JMapInfo and stage metadata integration remain pending. This does not establish collision metadata or playable gameplay.

Verification correction: optimized Zig builds defined NDEBUG, removing assert-based checks and the metadata attach call placed inside assert. Earlier optimized PASS lines were insufficient evidence. The collision test driver now explicitly uses -UNDEBUG; rerunning both complete 1,216/1,420-prism tests with assertions enabled passes all line, sphere and reference comparisons. Rebuilt metadata test with -UNDEBUG now executes attach and checks all three fixture cells successfully. Future optimized assertion tests must explicitly retain assertions; empty extracted metadata remains unverified for nonempty game values.

Alignment-safe original JMap numeric reads: guarded generate-jmap-runtime.ps1 regenerates original scope fixes and replaces seven source reads plus two inline float/bool reads with memcpy-based host access. test-native-jmap.ps1 explicitly retains assertions. Both aligned and deliberately unaligned integer/float/string-pointer fixtures pass three exact cell comparisons. This addresses row numeric alignment; native storage lifetime, additional malformed/overlapping-field cases, nonempty extracted metadata and stage integration still require work.

Owned original metadata binding: generated JMapInfo::attachNative decodes bounded bytes and retains shared native storage. Copies retain backing storage; reload constructs/validates the new table before replacing ownership. Aligned/unaligned fixtures pass after attachment from temporary input data and reload, with a copied object's integer access still valid. Original collision kernel regressions pass; all nine full collision source units compile with the expanded native metadata layout. Legacy raw attach remains for native views; same-owner reattachment retains storage. Full stage metadata loading and nonempty game-table verification are still pending.

Real collision metadata verification and bounded stage binding: extracted BroadBeanPlanet.pa from the user WBFS with cluster H0 verification. Original JMapInfo accessors with owned native storage pass exact comparisons on all 6,000 cells in this nonempty game table. CollisionParts::initNative now takes bounded KCL and metadata byte vectors and uses attachNative instead of a raw metadata pointer. All nine collision source units compile. The full initializer still depends on original scene/zone/camera services and has not executed in a native stage; gameplay remains incomplete.

Original CollisionCode metadata verification: focused generated metadata methods retain original code-table creation, hashes, numeric attribute selection and camera ID access. Linked alongside original JMapInfo and original hash operations; assertion-enabled fixture and 6,000-cell BroadBean metadata tests pass. Nonempty game test explicitly requires numeric collision-code fields to be exercised. Test-owned code tables are released manually; production table lifecycle and full Triangle/CollisionParts scene linkage remain pending. No native stage or Mario gameplay is established.

Verified prism/attribute asset pairing: the old broadbean.kcl sample is 106,188 bytes and is not the 104,666-byte BroadBeanPlanet archive mesh; pairing it with newly extracted BroadBeanPlanet.pa failed metadata-index validation. Old geometric query tests remain scoped to those standalone samples. The server test now uses kcl/pa pairs extracted together from BroadBeanPlanet.arc and BroadBeanPlanetC.arc, retaining separate names. All 1,200 and 1,420 prism attributes select valid original JMapInfo rows via actual getAttributes; line, sphere and full-scan query comparisons pass on both matching pairs. This establishes local original collision plus metadata binding, not stage/zone services or playable gameplay.

Native stage inverse backend: sdk_matrix_port.cpp now implements PSMTXInverse for affine 3x4 transforms, including inverse translation and alias-safe output. Assertion-enabled tests pass for a rotated/sheared/nonuniform reflected transform, inverse composition, point roundtrip, in-place operation and exact-singular rejection without output mutation. Host division replaces console reciprocal estimates; bit-exact rounding is unverified. This supplies the inverse operation used by original CollisionParts matrix setup, but full transformed CollisionParts queries and scene initialization remain pending.

Original transformed collision-part query milestone: focused test links exact CollisionParts constructor/checkStrikeLine, Triangle constructor/fillData, HitInfo constructor and required server accessors with native SDK inverse/vector operations. For all 2,620 prisms in verified asset pairs, translated/rotated/uniform-scale world-space queries return original HitInfo/Triangle records belonging to the collision part; nearest world hit fractions agree with local full-prism reference within 0.002. These run actual original part query methods, not the diagnostic mesh query. Scene/zone registration and Binder response remain absent; full original runtime linking and gameplay are still incomplete.

Original transformed sphere-part milestone: focused linked test includes original checkStrikeBall/checkStrikeBallCore/calcCollidePosition/projectToPlane and normal accessors. On all 2,620 prisms in verified pairs, radius-20 world spheres under the rotated/translated/uniform-scale-2 part produce matching original Triangle identities, face classifications and approximately 10 world-space penetration (tolerance 0.02). Existing local/reference and world-line tests still pass. Moving-part reaction, nonuniform scale behavior, scene registration and Binder response are not yet verified; gameplay remains incomplete.

Original Binder reaction milestone: focused test links actual Binder constructor/clear/obtainMomentFixReaction and Triangle normal access. Each of the 2,620 transformed face contacts is passed through original Binder reaction processing; reaction agrees with the original contact normal times penetration, and applying it reduces target-prism residual penetration below 0.02 or clears contact. This tests single-contact static reaction only. Full Binder::bind/findBindedPos loops, multiple-plane sliding, moving-part reaction, collision director registration and Mario gameplay remain pending.

Original gravity-relative Binder classification: focused test links exact storeContactPlane and four original MapUtil floor/wall classification definitions. For each of 2,620 original transformed contacts, opposite-normal gravity marks ground, matching-normal gravity marks roof, and normalized perpendicular gravity marks wall, with other states clear. All reaction/query regressions pass. Binder movement traversal still depends on original Collision namespace/scene category keepers; no replacement scene service has been substituted, and full bind loops remain pending.

Original collision zone verification (2026-10-04): the collision harness now extracts the unchanged CollisionZone constructor, addParts, eraseParts, calcMinMaxAndRadius and addAndUpdateMinMax methods plus CollisionParts::getTrans from CollisionCategorizedKeeper.cpp. Assertion-enabled optimized native checks verify translated-part AABB/sphere bounds, multiple-part containment, removal and explicit bound recomputation, absent-part removal, empty-zone bounds, and the original special global-zone registration behavior. Both paired BroadBean collision assets pass this check along with the existing 1,200 + 1,420 prism line/sphere/Binder contact checks. This verifies original zone mechanics only: scene director registration, full Binder movement, and original Mario gameplay remain unlinked and unverified.

Original scene registration compile milestone (2026-10-04): include-runtime now selects the original NameObjHolder declaration instead of the diagnostic std::vector adapter. The collision compile probe includes original NameObj, NameObjRegister and NameObjHolder; the latter is generated with the removed C++17 std::mem_fun call replaced by an equivalent lambda invoking the same member pointer. All twelve scene-registration/collision units compile. This is compilation evidence only; original scene creation and full collision director/Binder linkage still require implementation and runtime verification. The diagnostic build continues to use its separate holder adapter.

Linked original scene holder verification (2026-10-04): test-original-scene-registry.ps1 links original NameObj and NameObjRegister with the generated C++17-compatible original NameObjHolder and exact original NameObj movement wrappers from ObjUtil.cpp. Assertion-enabled optimized execution passes 24 actor registrations, repeated reverse-order lookup exceeding the 16-entry cache, missing-name lookup, virtual member dispatch, holder-wide suspend/resume after flag synchronization, and clearing both arrays. The scene notification dependency is an explicit test-only counter; this does not implement the production scene movement controller or prove full scene/director linkage. No diagnostic holder participates in this harness.

Original moving collision transform verification (2026-10-04): the existing collision harness now extracts original CollisionParts::updateMtx, makeEqualScale and updateBoundingSphereRangePrivate, with exact MR::isSameMtx from MtxUtil.cpp. Both asset runs pass assertion-enabled checks for translated previous/current matrices, inverse-coordinate round trips, stationary-frame counters, disabled updates, forced updates, and uniform-scale bounding changes. The 2,620 prism line/sphere/contact checks still pass after restoring the transformed part. This exercises the transform update component used by CollisionCategorizedKeeper::movement; complete keeper/director movement and Binder::bind remain unlinked. Nonuniform auto-scaling and full moving-platform ride behavior are not established by these tests.

Native original collision scene integration (2026-10-04): native_collision_scene.hpp/.cpp supplies an initial native host with original NameObjHolder and SceneObjHolder storage, the original CollisionDirector constructor/movement/getCollisionDirector, original CollisionCode tables, and original category keeper construction, terrain registration, movement and sphere queries. The native factory supports CollisionDirector only and throws for other scene objects; scheduling accepts only its actual movement category. Scene zone count is supplied explicitly (1..32). Original SceneObjHolder constructor/create/getObj/isExist and selected keeper methods are generated unchanged from Petari. Host teardown releases director/keeper/zone/contact/table allocations and clears registration. Constructor allocation-failure cleanup is not yet comprehensive, and this component is not integrated into galaxy-probe.

Run test-original-collision-scene.ps1 to regenerate, link and execute the component. With assertions enabled, both matched BroadBean asset pairs pass original director lookup/reused creation, zone registration, actual scheduled movement, per-prism original category sphere queries matching direct part hits (2,620 positive prisms), and removal leaving no query hits. Isolated collision and scene registry checks also pass. This establishes a real original collision query route in a separate native runtime harness; it does not establish full Binder::bind, original Mario movement, playable stages, or campaign completion. Bounding extent in this harness is computed from loaded vertices; the original camera-code registration path in calcFarthestVertexDistance still needs integration.

Original Binder loop integration (2026-10-04): the collision scene harness now links unchanged Binder::bind, findBindedPos, moveAlongHittedPlanes, storeCurrentHitInfo and moveWithCollisionParts plus original Triangle/CollisionParts movement-force methods, MR::normalizeOrZero, and Collision namespace sphere/hit-buffer wrappers. These route through the actual original director/category keeper hosted natively. Assertion-enabled checks pass 32 inward movement/contact-resolution samples on each asset (64 total), finite adjusted movement, recorded contact planes and nonzero push-out, separation from each source triangle after applying the returned displacement, and unchanged free movement far from terrain. The full 2,620 existing category/direct collision comparisons remain green. Moving-ground following and moving-reaction flags are disabled in these movement samples; sliding, offsets, stored plane arrays and full player integration remain unverified. The original Binder algorithm now executes in this component, but it is not yet wired into Mario or galaxy-probe and no playable gameplay is claimed.

Original terrain extent initialization (2026-10-04): collision scene generation now links original KCollisionServer::calcFarthestVertexDistance and isNearParallelNormal. The harness uses that initialization instead of assigning a manually computed maximum extent; independent vertex extent calculations agree within 0.01 for both assets, with all scene/Binder checks still passing. Native camera code collection preserves unique valid code IDs (0..254), host name and zone as pending camera groups for later original camera chunk creation. Tests compare the preserved set against original CollisionCode::getCameraID for every loaded prism and explicitly verify duplicate suppression and invalid code 255 handling. This is metadata retention, not working camera chunk creation or camera gameplay. Full CollisionParts::initNative still requires original actor/sensor and placement context integration; the renderer/game executable remains separate.

Owned native terrain loader (2026-10-04): NativeCollisionScene::loadTerrain now owns original CollisionParts/KCollisionServer/JMapInfo allocations, loads bounded native KCL/metadata storage, validates finite invertible transforms, invokes original resetAllMtx/resetAllMtxPrivate, terrain extent calculation and scaled bounding initialization, collects camera metadata, and registers enabled terrain in the original category/zone. Zone capacity is checked before registration. Failed initialization rolls back camera metadata and frees unregistered assets. The scene releases owned terrain before its director/zones during teardown. This loader takes an explicit host name and zone and currently targets terrain category 0; original actor/sensor placement and CameraParamChunk creation are still pending.

The integration harness now loads both matching game asset pairs through this reusable loader rather than assigning a stack collision server and manually registering terrain. Assertions pass valid ownership/registration, rejection of truncated KCL and metadata, zero-row metadata incompatible with the loaded prisms, and singular transforms, with scene part counts and pending metadata unchanged after each rejection. Existing 2,620 collision checks and 64 original Binder contact-separation samples still pass. This runtime is still separate from galaxy-probe and original Mario gameplay.

Native runtime DLL boundary (2026-10-04): build-runtime.ps1 now builds galaxy-runtime.dll from the original collision scene/Binder component and verifies dynamic loading with a separate client. native_runtime_api.h exposes version/error, scene creation/destruction, bounded terrain load, movement tick and Binder displacement/contact queries using a C ABI, opaque handles, bytes and plain float arrays. Native C++ actors, allocation ownership and exceptions remain inside the DLL. This provides the intended boundary for connecting original runtime code to the existing PC renderer without mixing diagnostic/original actor layouts. Only one collision scene may be active; use these calls serially on one thread. The current bind entry point creates a zero-offset Binder for each request; persistent original Mario actor state is not implemented.

DLL tests use LoadLibrary/GetProcAddress to verify every export and API version, then run two scene create/load/tick/bind/destroy cycles per asset. Both actual game collision pairs pass invalid-load recovery, free movement, contact against the first stored prism, invalid-gravity error propagation, stale-handle rejection, and library unload. The renderer has not yet loaded this DLL, and a playable Mario controller/campaign remains unimplemented. The DLL is an original collision runtime component, not a completed PC game.

PC executable runtime connection (2026-10-04): native_runtime_client.hpp dynamically loads galaxy-runtime.dll beside galaxy-probe.exe, validates ABI version and all exports, owns/destroys its opaque scene, and routes terrain load/tick/Binder queries using byte buffers and numeric arrays. No original runtime C++ object crosses into the diagnostic executable. galaxy-probe now supports --native-collision WBFS OBJECT_ARCHIVE KCL_PATH METADATA_PATH. This command reads resources directly through the existing authenticated WBFS/archive reader, loads them into the original runtime DLL, and checks 32 terrain contact displacements plus free movement.

The normal native/build.ps1 build and existing self-tests pass. Both commands below pass with the user WBFS (64 executable-to-DLL contact samples total):

    galaxy-probe.exe --native-collision "D:\Desktop\Super Mario Galaxy (USA, Canada) (En,Fr,Es).wbfs" /ObjectData/BroadBeanPlanet.arc /broadbeanplanet.kcl /broadbeanplanet.pa
    galaxy-probe.exe --native-collision "D:\Desktop\Super Mario Galaxy (USA, Canada) (En,Fr,Es).wbfs" /ObjectData/BroadBeanPlanetC.arc /broadbeanplanetc.kcl /broadbeanplanetc.pa

The executable now calls the original collision runtime, but the rendered diagnostic body still uses its prior movement path. Original Mario controller/input, full actor scene integration, stage cameras, campaign gameplay and enhancements remain unfinished. Build the runtime with build-runtime.ps1 before using this command; ordinary self-tests do not require the DLL.

Rendered original collision route (2026-10-04): --preview-runtime-body and --preview-runtime-body-smoke now load the selected common-layer placed KCL resources with their matching .pa metadata and placement transforms into galaxy-runtime.dll. DiagnosticBody's optional native route calls original Binder for displacement and uses its contact classifications; it bypasses the prior diagnostic sphere slide/sweep route. Its input, gravity integration and jump tuning remain diagnostic. Other existing preview commands retain their prior path. Stage resource capture is opt-in and fails if a selected KCL has no matching metadata; unsupported/skipped stage objects remain outside this preview's scope.

The normal executable build and self-tests pass. verify-runtime-body.ps1 passes against /StageData/EggStarGalaxy.arc from the user WBFS: eight placed terrain instances, 129 original Binder movement updates (every scheduled body update), actual terrain contact during a fall, finite final position, final independently checked overlap below 0.05, and native D3D frame readback matching generated pixels after contact. Existing input/focus loss, reset, pause/single-step, following-camera extent and window lifecycle checks pass. The smoke window is hidden and exits. For an interactive diagnostic sphere, use --preview-runtime-body WBFS /StageData/EggStarGalaxy.arc. This is rendered original collision execution, not original Mario movement or completed game gameplay; the full player/controller, scene coverage, cameras and campaign remain incomplete.

Original Mario actor prerequisite layer (2026-10-04): probe-actor-runtime.ps1 compiles fourteen real original sources with full runtime headers: LiveActor, AllLiveActorGroup, LiveActorGroup, NameObjGroup, ClippingDirector/ActorHolder/ActorInfo/GroupHolder/Judge, ViewGroupCtrl, ActorLightCtrl, ActorPadAndCameraCtrl, ModelManager and ActorAnimKeeper. All compile.
