The isolated-source COFF inventory records 338 definitions and 198 external symbols, excluding collision/runtime library providers; it is not link success.

probe-actor-link.ps1 now attempts an actual separate DLL link of those actor objects, an exported original LiveActor construction entry, and the current native collision runtime providers. The link still fails on missing original animation/model/clipping/utility providers, recorded in build/actor-runtime-link-probe.log and actor-runtime-link-status.json. The probe entry is never executed and does not bypass scene registration. This identifies the actual integration work needed before original MarioActor construction; galaxy-runtime.dll and the verified rendered collision path are unchanged. Original Mario gameplay is still unavailable.

Actor utility native adaptations (2026-10-04): actor-runtime probe now compiles nineteen optimized original/adapted actor and utility units, including LiveActorFlag, ObjUtil, JMapUtil, LiveActorUtil and ActorSensorUtil. All compile. Generated native sources replace the legacy va_list.h include with cstdarg, select explicit float atan2 calls for placement rotations, select the u32 resource-index overload, and preserve joint local-matrix pointers with uintptr_t instead of truncating them to u32. The original XanimeCore declaration has a native uintptr_t pointer-storage field. Native geometry adds the original projection/killElement and quaternion/translation matrix formulas, preserving non-normalized inputs. Source generation validates anchors and leaves pinned Petari files unchanged.

Assertion-enabled actor-native-adaptation-tests pass original LiveActorFlag initialization, vector projection including aliasing and non-unit direction semantics, quaternion identity/quarter-turn/non-unit matrix semantics, native joint pointer field width and original XjointTransform initialization. The full joint setter/model lookup path remains unexecuted. Actual actor link probing now uses these real utility providers and omits the duplicated native scene-scheduling hook from generated ObjUtil; the native scheduler remains its provider. Prior undefined actor flag, CSV-u8, clipping-group metadata and debug sanitizer diagnostics are resolved; the full link still fails on remaining scene/model/animation/GX/library providers. The expanded provider set also exposes their deeper dependencies; this is not an overall dependency-count completion measure. Current collision DLL/rendered diagnostic functionality remains separate and original Mario gameplay is not available.
Original actor provider expansion (2026-10-04): the optimized actor probe now compiles 23 providers. Added unchanged NameObjFinder, MovementOnOffGroupHolder and BvaPlayer, plus generated DisplayListMaker with its removed std::mem_fun helper replaced by an equivalent pointer lambda calling MaterialCtrl::update. Pinned originals remain unchanged. An actual DLL link confirms the former object lookup, movement group, visibility player constructor and display-list constructor missing-symbol diagnostics are absent, with no duplicate-symbol or sanitizer diagnostics. The complete link still fails on deeper original scene/model/material/GX providers; these components have not been executed as gameplay. ResourceHolder compilation exposed its original heap placement-new interface and is not yet included in the passing provider list. Full original Mario and playable campaign remain unavailable.
Resource provider integration (2026-10-04): the optimized probe now compiles 25 providers, including ResourceHolder and ResourceInfo. Generated ResourceInfo uses cstddef instead of the unavailable legacy size_t.h. ResourceHolder includes Native/HeapPlacement.hpp: host-sized heap placement new rejects size overflow, forwards the original heap/alignment to JKRHeap::alloc, throws on allocation failure, and provides matching constructor-failure cleanup through JKRHeap::free. Assertion-enabled heap-placement-tests pass forwarding, allocation failure, overflow rejection and throwing-constructor cleanup using an allocator double; these checks do not prove a working original heap backend. Actual actor DLL linking resolves ResourceHolder/ResTable providers but remains incomplete on original heap, archive, J3D loader, material and scene providers. MaterialCtrl currently needs the original affine inversion method and has not been added to the passing probe. No original Mario gameplay or full campaign is verified.
Original material controller prerequisite (2026-10-04): native TMatrix34 now implements affine invert using the pinned cofactor formula and 32*float-epsilon singularity threshold, with temporary storage preserving source/destination aliasing and no destination mutation on rejected determinants. Assertion-enabled actor adaptation checks pass a translated/sheared/reflected affine transform, identity composition, point round-trip, aliasing, singular rejection and both signed threshold boundaries. The original MaterialCtrl source now compiles, bringing the actor provider probe to 26 passing units. Actual link probing resolves its material update/matrix setter providers and exposes remaining SDK matrix, player, camera, heap and J3D services. Full material rendering and original Mario gameplay remain unverified; the candidate actor DLL still does not link.
Native SDK affine providers (2026-10-04): sdk_matrix_port.cpp now supplies PSMTXTrans and PSMTXConcat for original material/player callers. Concatenation composes affine transforms in a*b order and uses temporary storage when either or both input matrices alias the destination. test-sdk-affine.ps1 passes assertion-enabled noncommuting composition against sequential vector transforms, left/right/both alias cases, translation and rotation-only vector behavior. Actual actor DLL probing no longer reports either SDK symbol or duplicate definitions. Remaining original camera, player, heap, archive and J3D services still prevent the full actor runtime from linking; scalar checks do not prove paired-single rounding parity or working material/gameplay rendering.
Original animation provider expansion (2026-10-04): 33 actor/utility/animation providers now compile, adding original MaterialAnmBuffer, BckCtrl, BtpPlayer, BtkPlayer, BrkPlayer, BpkPlayer and the original AnmPlayer runtime methods. The generated AnmPlayer copy omits only AnmPlayer_FORCE_EMIT, a binary-matching helper with no callers in the pinned repository; its runtime constructors, update/start/stop/reflect and attach/detach dispatch are retained. Actual DLL probing resolves the added player/buffer/control definitions and now exposes underlying J3D material animator registration/removal services, in addition to the existing model loader, scene, heap and archive dependencies. This is compile/link integration evidence; these material animation providers have not been executed on a loaded original model. Corrected inventory: original J3DModelLoader/J3DAnmLoader/JKRHeap implementations exist under Petari/src/JSystem; the initial search inspected only libs/JSystem. These original sources require native adaptation and platform providers. No playable game or campaign is claimed.
Original resource lookup execution (2026-10-04): native hash provider now supplies getHashCodeLower with the original multiply-by-31 recurrence and signed-character handling, using host tolower in place of the console locale table. test-resource-lookup.ps1 links and executes the real generated ResourceInfo/ResTable methods with assertions enabled. ASCII case-insensitive names, last-extension stripping, retained extensions, forward/index/file-info/reverse lookup and absent-resource behavior pass. The test releases the names/table it allocates. Non-ASCII console locale parity remains unverified. Actual actor DLL probing no longer reports getHashCodeLower or duplicate definitions, but the full link still fails on original J3D, heap, archive, camera and player dependencies. This proves resource-table lookup behavior, not model loading or playable gameplay.

Original JSystem integration (2026-10-04): actor source probing now supports Petari/src/JSystem as well as src/Game. The real J3DAnimation.cpp and J3DModelData.cpp compile unchanged, bringing the passing provider set to 35. Actual actor linking resolves J3DFrameCtrl init/update/checkPass and J3DModelData::newSharedDisplayList without duplicate symbols, but remains incomplete on underlying graphics display-list/OS services and other original dependencies. Their diagnostic logs remain in actor-runtime-probe, but failed units are not in the passing provider list. Loading original files also requires correct bounded big-endian conversion and native ownership; compilation alone will not validate this. Model animation/gameplay execution remains unverified.
Original model loader allocation integration (2026-10-04): generated J3DModelLoader.cpp now includes the host-sized heap placement interface while retaining the original loader methods. HeapPlacement supplies scalar/array overloads for explicit heap+alignment and current-heap alignment selection; the latter forwards a null heap to the original JKRHeap allocator, matching the original overload's selection. Matching placement-delete overloads release through JKRHeap on constructor failure. test-heap-placement.ps1 passes assertion-enabled allocation/failure/overflow forwarding plus array constructor-failure cleanup using an allocator double; the original heap backend remains unlinked. All 36 actor/animation/model providers compile. Actual actor DLL probing resolves J3DModelLoaderDataBase::load but still fails on deeper original material/joint/graphics/heap methods and globals. No loaded original J3D model is validated; bounded big-endian adaptation and runtime services are required before this can be executed safely as model loading. No gameplay is claimed.
Original J3D material/joint provider integration (2026-10-04): 42 actor/animation/model providers now compile. Added original J3DMaterial, J3DMaterialAttach, J3DMaterialAnm, J3DJointTree and J3DJointFactory unchanged, plus J3DJoint with its missing JMath header explicitly included in the generated copy. Actual actor DLL linking resolves the material animator registration/removal methods, J3DMaterial vtable and J3DMtxCalc::mJoint definitions, without duplicate symbols. The full candidate runtime remains unlinked on original graphics state/draw buffers, SDK/platform and other scene/player services; model loading, animation rendering and gameplay have not yet been executed. Original draw-buffer/graphics-state providers (2026-10-04): 45 integration providers compile. Added original J3DSys and J3DStruct unchanged and J3DDrawBuffer with the host heap placement header in its generated copy. Actual DLL probing resolves current/parent matrix state and draw-buffer entry globals without duplicate symbols. Default transform and draw-matrix data definitions remain unresolved. Graphics methods are not executed: their console GX FIFO/SDK operations still require a native graphics backend. Original J3DMtxBuffer compilation identified weighted skinning PowerPC assembly plus aligned allocation calls; its log is retained and the source is not yet in the passing provider list. The skinning calculation needs a scalar native implementation preserving joint/inverse-bind composition and weight accumulation. Full actor/model linkage, loaded model rendering and original gameplay remain incomplete.

Native weighted skinning calculation (2026-10-04): generated J3DMtxBuffer retains the original methods except calcWeightEnvelopeMtx's paired-single assembly, replaced by scalar world-joint * inverse-bind composition accumulated in influence order through Native::envelopeMatrix. The original scale-flag AND and envelope index/weight traversal are retained; weights are not normalized. Its heap allocation calls use the existing native placement header. All 46 integration providers compile. test-envelope-matrix.ps1 passes assertion-enabled weighted matrix output against independently transformed/weighted points, reversed joint indices, non-unit weights, scale-flag propagation and empty helper output. Console instruction rounding parity and execution on loaded original joint/envelope data remain unverified. Actual actor DLL linking still fails on underlying matrix-array, normal-matrix, billboard, graphics and game services; there is no loaded-model skinning or gameplay claim.
Native normal/billboard transform providers (2026-10-04): generated J3DTransformPort retains original default transform/scale/matrix constants and billboard routines. Y-billboard fast square-root and vector normalization use scalar host arithmetic. Normal-matrix inverse-transpose delegates to the tested host inverse then transposes its 3x3 portion, leaving the destination untouched on rejected inverse. test-j3d-transform.ps1 passes assertion-enabled normal/tangent perpendicularity under shear/reflection, transpose consistency, singular preservation, billboard column scales and preserved translations/Y column. All 47 integration providers compile. Actual actor DLL probing resolves normal/billboard/default-transform symbols with no duplicate definitions; matrix-array, shape, graphics and other game services remain unresolved. Instruction rounding parity, degenerate Y-billboard inputs and loaded-model rendering remain unverified. No playable gameplay is claimed.
Native draw-matrix array concatenation (2026-10-04): J3DTransformPort now supplies J3DPSMtxArrayConcat using the native scalar affine composition provider, preserving the fixed left matrix and processing contiguous 3x4 matrices. Expanded assertion-enabled transform tests pass two distinct matrices against individual compositions, exact in-place input/output aliasing and zero-count output preservation. All 47 provider units still compile; actual actor DLL linking no longer reports the array-concatenation symbol or duplicates. Partially overlapping arrays are not supported/verified. Shape, graphics and game services still prevent complete linkage, and loaded-model rendering/gameplay remain unverified.
Original shape provider integration (2026-10-04): all 52 integration units compile, adding J3DShapeMtx/J3DShapeTable unchanged and generated J3DShape/J3DShapeDraw/J3DShapeFactory. Generated compatibility changes select numeric zero for the raw GD array-address argument and supply heap placement plus the SDK alignment rounding macro where required. Actual actor DLL probing resolves J3DShape::countBumpMtxNum and other added shape definitions without duplicate symbols, while FIFO matrix loading, normal scaling, matrix-index pipeline and platform/game services remain unresolved. These providers have not been executed on a loaded game shape; bounded file conversion and a native graphics backend remain required. No playable gameplay is claimed.
Native shape normal scaling (2026-10-04): J3DTransformPort now supplies scalar J3DScaleNrmMtx and J3DScaleNrmMtx33 following the original assembly's independent column multiplication. The affine version preserves translation. Expanded assertion-enabled transform checks pass signed/nonuniform column scales, agreement between 3x3 and 3x4 variants, and preserved translations. All 52 integration units compile and actual actor DLL probing resolves both scaling methods without duplicates. FIFO matrix-upload and matrix-index pipeline services remain unimplemented, so original shape rendering/full gameplay is not verified.
Original material factory integration (2026-10-04): all 54 integration providers compile. Generated J3DMaterialFactory and J3DMaterialFactory_v21 preserve original factory methods and replace unavailable TRUE/FALSE conditional constants with their SDK numeric BOOL values 1/0. Actual actor DLL probing resolves the factory create methods without duplicate definitions and now exposes underlying material-block/TEV implementations and default tables. Material parsing/construction has not been executed on a converted game asset; full linkage, native graphics execution and gameplay remain incomplete. Pinned sources are unchanged.
Original material-block/TEV/packet integration (2026-10-04): all 57 integration units compile. Added J3DMatBlock and J3DTevs unchanged, plus generated J3DPacket with the native heap placement header and SDK alignment macro. Actual actor DLL probing resolves patched TEV initialization/vtable and default swap-mode table definitions without duplicates; underlying GD texture commands, vertex-buffer methods, packet draw and other graphics/game services remain unresolved. These original command-emitting routines have not been executed through a native graphics backend, so compilation/link-provider progress does not establish material rendering or gameplay. An attempted J3DTexture.cpp entry was removed after confirming that source path does not exist.
Original vertex/model runtime providers (2026-10-04): all 59 integration units compile. Added original J3DVertex unchanged and generated J3DModel with the unavailable TRUE constant in its NBT scale comparison replaced by SDK BOOL value 1. Actual actor DLL probing resolves J3DVertexBuffer::setArray and J3DModel::calc without duplicates, revealing original deformation/vertex-color/skin-deform and SDK cache-service dependencies. Model calculation/drawing is not yet executable: full linkage, converted asset ownership and native graphics execution remain required. No original Mario gameplay is verified.
Original model deformation providers (2026-10-04): all 61 integration units compile, adding original J3DSkinDeform and J3DCluster unchanged. Actual actor DLL probing resolves the model-level deformation/vertex-color/skin-deform dispatch methods without duplicates. Cluster deformation now exposes original vector normalization/trigonometric-table and SDK cache/synchronization dependencies; lower-level CPU skin and vertex-color implementations also remain to be linked. No deformation has been executed on a loaded game model; full runtime and native graphics integration remain incomplete. No playable gameplay is claimed.
Native SDK vector normalization (2026-10-04): sdk_matrix_port.cpp now supplies PSVECNormalize using scalar reciprocal square-root scaling with source/destination alias protection. Assertion-enabled SDK affine tests also verify a non-axis signed vector, exact in-place agreement and the host zero-vector NaN result. Console estimate/rounding and zero-input parity remain unverified. Actual actor DLL probing resolves the original cluster deformation normalization dependency without duplicate symbols. Cache/synchronization, trigonometric table, graphics and other game services remain unresolved; loaded-model deformation and playable gameplay are not established.
Original trigonometric provider integration (2026-10-04): original JMATrigonometric.cpp now participates in the 62-unit passing compile probe. The native header declares its atan2 specialization before first use, fixing modern C++ specialization ordering. Assertion-enabled trig-table-tests pass all 1,024 asin and atan initialization entries, asin/acos endpoints and atan2 quadrants against host reference math. Actual actor linkage resolves the original table providers but remains incomplete on cache/synchronization, graphics and game services. Host math rounding parity and loaded-model gameplay remain unverified.
Native JMath providers (2026-10-04): generated JMath retains original Euler-to-quaternion conversion and supplies scalar quaternion hemisphere interpolation, vector scale-add/interpolation and affine column scaling in place of console-only assembly. All 63 integration units compile. test-jmath.ps1 passes assertion-enabled quaternion opposite-hemisphere and non-normalized interpolation, vector source/destination aliasing, signed scale/translation preservation and identity Euler conversion. Full actor DLL linkage remains incomplete on platform/graphics/game dependencies. Console instruction rounding parity and loaded-model gameplay remain unverified.
Original model name-table provider (2026-10-04): original JUTNameTab joins the 64-unit passing compile probe. test-name-table.ps1 executes the original methods with assertions on a host-order fixture, verifying named/indexed lookup, missing names, out-of-range name index, hash-match string confirmation and resource reset. The actual actor DLL still fails on graphics/platform/game services. Wii name-table files require bounded BE conversion before this raw original API; the fixture does not validate game-file loading or gameplay.
Native GD command provider boundary (2026-10-04): generated J3DGD now compiles as the 65th integration unit. It supplies a scalar count-leading-zero intrinsic and uses SDK BOOL numeric values. Texture/TLUT host pointer casts are replaced with the required Native::gpuResourceAddress backend interface; this interface remains intentionally unimplemented pending real emulated-address/resource ownership and command decoding. No host pointer is truncated as a substitute for graphics integration. SDK assertion tests pass count-leading-zero across zero, all 32 one-bit positions and all bits set. Actual actor linkage now reaches the explicit resource-address service plus remaining platform/graphics/game dependencies; no graphics execution or gameplay is claimed.
Native GPU resource-address storage (2026-10-04): GpuResources owns registered byte snapshots, assigns monotonically increasing 32-byte-aligned emulated addresses within the BP address range, rejects host overlap/invalid extents, and bounds command-address resolution. Removal invalidates old addresses; addresses are not reused within the registry lifetime. Serial access only; changed resources require removal/re-registration. The Native::gpuResourceAddress provider requires prior registration and never truncates pointers. test-gpu-resources.ps1 passes snapshot ownership, offset addressing/alignment, overlapping registration rejection, extent checks and stale-address rejection. Actor DLL probing resolves the service without duplicates. Asset registration and command decoder/render-backend integration are not yet connected, and the complete runtime still does not link. No texture rendering or gameplay is claimed.

Native GD command storage milestone: `src/gd_storage.cpp` provides the original GD display-list initialization, current-list storage, 32-byte zero padding, coherent host-memory flush and fail-fast overflow. The host override `include-runtime/revolution/gd/GDBase.h` preserves SDK byte writers but validates integer address bounds before writing, avoiding out-of-range pointer arithmetic in the original overflow check. Serial current-list state is required. `test-gd-storage.ps1` passed with assertions enabled: actual SDK BP/CP big-endian encoding, padding, exact-capacity overflow, rejected padding with insufficient capacity, null storage and missing current list. The actor link probe now includes this provider; it still fails on other unresolved original graphics/platform dependencies. No candidate actor DLL was executed. Commands are not yet decoded or rendered, and this does not establish gameplay.

Original buffered J3D texture-command integration verified by `test-j3d-texture-commands.ps1`: executes the generated original J3DGD buffered functions, captures actual BP command bytes for all eight texture slots, checks dimensions/format/address encoding and resolves encoded addresses into owned native resource snapshots. Removed registrations reject pointer emission. This runner excludes original direct-MMIO FIFO functions, which have no native backend yet, and does not execute a renderer. Low-level `__GDWrite` now checks bounds because J3D inline writers bypass the SDK checked wrappers; overflow is fatal to the current command build and any partially emitted command must be discarded. Both texture-command and GD-storage tests passed with assertions enabled. This advances original graphics integration but does not establish original actor gameplay or texture rendering.

`include/Native/GxCommandState.hpp` now consumes bounded big-endian BP/CP/direct-XF command streams into CPU register state. Texture slot state resolves the original emitted native resource addresses. BP masking persists across lists and resets after the following non-mask BP write; behavior checked against https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Source/Core/VideoCommon/BPStructs.cpp (LoadBPReg), used as hardware behavior reference only, not an emulator runtime. Test executes original buffered J3D texture methods and verifies all eight decoded slots plus CP/XF writes, masked attribute preservation/reset, cross-list mask persistence, rejected XF range, truncation and unsupported draw command with state rollback. No GPU side effects, TLUT transfer, draw/indexed/call commands or actual rendering are implemented by this state decoder. Actor/campaign integration remains incomplete.

Texture-command image bridge: `src/gx_command_texture.hpp` resolves the base image's native registered address with its required encoded extent, then invokes the existing GX tiled texture decoder. Original J3D buffered RGBA8 commands now produce decoded image pixels consumed by the existing Direct3D texture renderer in the integration test. Hardware and WARP offscreen readback both passed for a deterministic 4x4 RGBA8 tile, alongside rejection of an 8x8 image requiring more registered bytes. The bridge rejects indexed images until command-side TLUT transfers exist. Base-image decoding does not yet implement command-side mip/sampler state or original geometry draw execution; the test does not establish game rendering/playability. The game executable has not been changed by this test-only integration.

Command-side TLUT transfer: GxCommandState consumes original J3DGDLoadTlut register writes with a required resource registry, copies palette bytes into bounded owned upper-bank TMEM storage and tracks transferred byte validity. Slot palette registers select offset/format for indexed base-image decoding. Malformed transfer or subsequent unsupported commands roll back the entire list state. Original C4/RGB565 palette-load and slot-7 binding commands now pass an alternating red/green image pixel check and both D3D hardware/WARP readback, including palette ownership after source deregistration and rejected out-of-range transfer preserving previous contents. Mip/sampler state, geometry draw execution, original actor runtime and playable campaign remain incomplete. This is integration-test coverage, not the game executable.

Real game-asset texture-command verification: `test-j3d-texture-commands.ps1 'D:\Desktop\Super Mario Galaxy (USA, Canada) (En,Fr,Es).wbfs'` reads the supplied WBFS, extracts /ObjectData/Mario.arc and /mario.bdl, bounds TEX1 headers/image/palette extents and emits actual original J3D buffered commands for each base image. All 22 Mario textures passed register/resource decoding, exact pixel agreement with independent BTI-header dispatch and D3D hardware/WARP offscreen readback. Indexed resources use original palette-transfer/bind commands. This test exercises actual game texture assets; it does not execute original J3D model draw calls, original Mario actor/controller or campaign gameplay. Mipmap/sampler/geometry work remains pending. Run without the WBFS argument for deterministic fixture-only checks.

Original J3D FIFO matrix capture: generated runtime header overrides route J3DFifo BP/CP/XF/indexed writes and J3DGX float writes into bounded GD command storage, preserving big-endian encoding and replacing host MMIO writes. Float-pointer emission now reads the float directly rather than aliasing it as u32. `generate-gd-fifo.ps1` is called by actor source generation and the command test. The test now compiles the complete generated J3DGD source rather than excluding its FIFO functions; explicit adapter preincludes prevent stale include-resolution cache from selecting the old SDK path. Original position/normal matrix emitters passed XF register address/value checks and the 22 real Mario texture hardware/WARP checks still pass. This captures commands only; indexed commands are emitted but their execution and original model draws remain pending. A current GDLObj is required for these native FIFO adapters. Full actor linkage/playable gameplay remain unverified.

Indexed XF execution milestone: GxCommandState handles indexed A/B/C/D opcodes (0x20/0x28/0x30/0x38), CP array base/stride state, descriptor word count/destination and bounded big-endian resource reads. Original J3DFifoLoadIndx and J3DFifoLoadNrmMtxIndx3x3 now pass position/normal register checks at a nonzero source index using explicitly encoded BE matrix storage. An invalid matrix index rejects the list and preserves previous XF contents. Assertions remain enabled; 22 real Mario texture hardware/WARP checks pass after this change. Indexed matrix source buffers must be packed in command byte order and registered before execution; actual original model buffers are not yet wired into this path. Geometry draw execution, remaining runtime dependencies and playable campaign are still unfinished.

Actor integration revalidated after graphics adapters: all 65 current source units compile; full actor candidate DLL still fails on remaining graphics and game services and was not executed. Native SDK cache-writeback providers now refresh only the intersection of requested live host ranges with registered resource snapshots, preserving addresses, and synchronized variants issue a host sequentially-consistent fence. Nonregistered host ranges need no CPU cache operation. Test verifies a changed matrix byte remains stale until original DCStoreRangeNoSync, refresh preserves its address, and DCFlushRange restores updated bytes before original indexed matrix execution. The actual actor link probe resolves DCStoreRangeNoSync/PPCSync dependencies and remains incomplete on other services. Serial registry access and removal before host-storage destruction are required; this is not support for concurrent GX execution or a claim of gameplay.

Native J3DMtxProjConcat now replaces the original paired-single assembly with a scalar 3x4-by-4x4 product. The original SDK Mtx argument spelling decays to a pointer; original assembly explicitly accesses the right-hand fourth row and actual J3DTexMtx callers pass effect projection matrices. Native code preserves that full projection behavior and computes into temporary storage before writing outputs. Assertion-enabled transform tests pass composition against separately transformed homogeneous points, destination aliasing with either input, and preservation of the right-hand fourth row. Console fused-operation rounding parity remains unverified. All 65 actor integration units compile and the full actor link probe now resolves this projection provider, but remains incomplete on other graphics/game services; no candidate DLL was run or gameplay claimed.

Original scalar transform providers restored unchanged from pinned J3DTransform.cpp: both translate/rotate overloads and standard/old/Maya/Maya-old texture matrix functions now participate in generated J3DTransformPort, using the original initialized trig tables. Assertion-enabled tests pass signed scale, centered quarter-turn texture point mapping, old/new translation-column placement, Maya quarter-turn columns, and quarter-turn object rotation/translation alongside existing normal/projection tests. All 65 actor integration sources compile; linkage resolves the texture-matrix providers and still fails on GX array/draw and other original runtime services. Loaded original model drawing and campaign gameplay remain unverified.

GX/GD array binding: gx_array_port.cpp implements GXSetArray, GDSetArray and GDSetArrayRaw as bounded CP base/stride command emission, preserving the original RVL GXAttr.c/GDGeometry.c NBT-to-normal mapping. Native registered host pointers use byte addresses rather than texture-only 32-byte alignment; texture-address alignment remains enforced separately. Complete 12-byte emission is capacity-checked before either register is written. Original indexed position/normal matrix tests now use GXSetArray rather than handcrafted CP writes; NBT alias, nonaligned interior base and invalid attribute rejection pass. GPU registration/alignment tests still pass. Full actor linkage resolves these array providers but remains incomplete on vertex descriptor setup, display-list/draw and other game services. Original host matrix buffers still require BE packing before indexed execution; no gameplay is claimed.

Original GDSetVtxDescv now participates in native integration, generated unchanged from the pinned RVL GDGeometry.c prefix before GDSetArray. The original writer emits bounded CP VCD low/high and XF input-spec commands into native storage. Assertions pass matrix-index/direct/index8/index16 declarations, color/normal/texture input counts and decoded register bit fields. The actor candidate includes this provider; full linkage still fails on display-list/draw and other game services. Descriptor lists must be valid and terminated as required by the original API; this is not vertex payload decoding or playable gameplay.

Native GXCallDisplayList now emits validated registered address/length commands and GxCommandState executes nested lists with bounded resource reads, maximum nesting 32 and cumulative 64 MiB command-byte budget. State changes commit only when the whole parent list succeeds. Tests pass a called CP-state list, stale-resource rejection preserving existing state, and self-recursion rejection. Geometry draw payloads remain unsupported; this enables original state/matrix list calls but not original shape drawing or playable gameplay.

Original vertex-format command path verified: J3DGDSetVtxAttrFmtv emits its real VAT register commands into native storage; GxCommandState now exposes bounded position-format decoding with component count/type/fraction and VCD addressing mode. Assertion-enabled test selects VAT slot 3 and verifies XYZ signed-16 position components with 7 fractional bits and INDEX16 addressing, emitted by the original function. This supplies the input format required by a future draw payload reader; actual geometry execution remains unfinished and no gameplay is claimed.

Native indexed-position reader now combines original VCD/VAT addressing/type/fraction state with registered CP array base/stride, checks index width/address overflow/resource extent and decodes BE components with finite-value validation. Original descriptor/format/array command tests verify a nonzero indexed XYZ signed-16 record with 7 fractional bits yielding (-1,2,-0.5), plus rejection of an out-of-resource index. Other numeric paths still need broader verification and byte-dequantization flag handling; no draw primitive execution or game rendering is established by this reader. Gameplay remains unfinished.

Real Mario vertex command verification: all 2,587 VTX1 positions from the supplied WBFS /ObjectData/Mario.arc /mario.bdl pass exact comparison through original GDSetVtxDescv/J3DGDSetVtxAttrFmtv/GXSetArray commands and native bounded indexed-position reads. Native GXSetIndTexOrder emits masked BP register 0x27 updates, preserving other indirect stage fields and original null-map/coord normalization from RVL GXBump.c. GXSetTevDirect now emits the original zero indirect-fields BP stage command with stage/capacity validation. Native TEV register services: GXSetTevColor/GXSetTevColorS10 dispatch through the original J3D signed-color writer (field-based host-safe conversion for RGBA8), and konst color/alpha selectors use bounded masked BP updates preserving adjacent stage/swap fields. Tests pass distinct RGBA channel values and different selections for paired stages. These providers join the actor link candidate; TEV shader execution and full gameplay remain unfinished.

TEV color bank decoding: native command state now retains both regular signed-color and constant-color banks for shared BP addresses E0-E7, selected by the original command's bank bit. RGBA lookup sign-extends 11-bit regular components and preserves constant components. Tests verify original J3D constant writes do not overwrite regular colors at the same address and signed negative color values survive. Real asset command verification still passes all 2,587 Mario positions and 22 texture hardware/WARP readbacks. TEV shader execution and primitive drawing remain unfinished; no gameplay is claimed.

TEV combiner field decoding: GxCommandState::tevStage reads all 16 color/alpha stage registers into A/B/C/D selectors, raw bias/scale/destination, subtract/clamp flags, and raster/texture swap selectors. Bias 3 remains raw for later comparison-operation shader handling. Assertion-enabled tests use pinned SDK TEV packing macros across all stages and reject stage 16. Full command fixture checks and supplied-WBFS verification pass: 2,587 Mario positions and 22 texture hardware/WARP readbacks. This prepares material state only; TEV shader execution, primitive rendering, and original gameplay remain unfinished. Project goal remains active; the separate continuation timer remains stopped by user request.

Native GXSetTevColorIn and GXSetTevAlphaIn now emit bounded masked stage-register updates with A/B/C/D selectors in SDK field order. Tests verify operation/bias/scale/destination/clamp and swap fields survive input changes, invalid arguments emit no bytes, and undersized buffers fail before emission. Assertion-enabled WBFS verification retains all 2,587 Mario position and 22 texture hardware/WARP passes. Native GXSetTevColorOp/AlphaOp emit bounded masked operation updates preserving inputs and swap selectors. Original SDK GDTev.c defines comparison encoding: bias 3, width in scale bits, equality in subtract bit. Assertion-enabled tests cover add/subtract and all eight comparison encodings, both color and alpha destinations and clamp settings. The actor-link candidate resolves these functions but remains incomplete on other services; supplied WBFS checks still pass all 2,587 positions and 22 texture hardware/WARP readbacks. Native GXSetTevOrder now emits masked per-stage texture/coordinate/channel selection preserving its paired stage. Tests cover distinct paired stages, texture-disable flags, null map/coordinate/channel, and preserving neighbor order. Original SDK dirty-state derived texture updates remain pending. Refreshed actor-link probe resolves GXSetTevOrder but still fails on other renderer/game dependencies. Native GXSetTevIndirect packs stage/format/bias/alpha/matrix/wrap/LOD/previous-stage fields using the pinned SDK IND_CMD macro, with argument and capacity validation before emission. GxCommandState decodes these fields for future shader use. Tests check exact command bytes, decoded fields, rejection of reserved matrix IDs without emission, and direct-mode reset with neighbor preservation. Actor-link probing resolves GXSetTevIndirect but other dependencies remain. This implements configuration only; indirect shader sampling and original gameplay are unfinished.

Native GXSetTevSwapMode and GXSetTevSwapModeTable emit bounded masked updates preserving alpha-operation/input fields and paired konst selectors. Assertion-enabled tests cover all tables, distinct stage swap choices, konst field preservation, and invalid table rejection without emission. GXSetAlphaCompare now emits bounded original-format BP alpha-test commands; GxCommandState exposes both reference values, comparisons and logical operation. GXSetTevKColor now delegates to the original field-based J3D constant writer with register/capacity validation. GXSetNumTevStages now emits an immediate masked native general-mode update using the original SDK four-bit count-minus-one field. Native state exposes 1-16 active stages. Assertion-enabled checks cover every valid count, preservation of unrelated general-mode fields, and invalid count rejection before emission. SDK dirty-state timing is replaced by immediate command emission; derived SDK state still requires integration. This adapter replaces binary pointer-overlay loading; raw ResFont::Rebuild is intentionally not provided.
Disc archive font loading milestone (2026-10-04): NativeArchiveFonts now resolves all five original Font.arc resources using case-insensitive basename matching, registers their exact owned-archive sizes, loads them through native ResFont and unregisters temporary source registrations. Actual user WBFS test passes for MessageFont26, PictureFont, MenuFont64, NumberFont and CinemaFont26, including glyph access after disc archive unmount and archive-owner release. Source data remain owned by each font's native record bridge. Font.arc uses lowercase resource names. This archive-to-font path is verified independently; GameSystemFontHolder has not yet been redirected to it and original HUD/text GPU rendering and gameplay remain unverified.
GameSystemFontHolder native loading entry point (2026-10-04): generated createFontFromFile now delegates to an explicitly bound NativeGameFontBackend rather than the unsafe legacy archive overlay path. WBFS fixture binds a disc/archive-backed implementation, invokes the holder entry point, verifies all five published pointers and original getMessageFont after archive unmount; an unbound backend rejects startup. Tests pass and all 147 runtime providers compile. Added backend dispatch to actor link providers. The disc-backed backend currently lives in the fixture; production runtime backend ownership/binding and embedded error-font handoff are not yet integrated. Holder font consumers require backend-owned fonts to outlive them. Original text/HUD drawing and gameplay remain unverified.
Reusable disc font backend ownership (2026-10-04): moved the WBFS-backed font loading implementation out of the fixture into NativeDiscFontBackend. It owns font collections per holder, rejects populated/duplicate holder loading, supports explicit release/reload, clears only owned pointers during detach and conditionally unbinds itself during teardown. Archive font registration now has scoped unregister cleanup for exceptional loading paths. Actual WBFS lifecycle tests pass for duplicate rejection, explicit/idempotent release, reload, backend teardown clearing all five holder pointers and rejection after auto-unbind. Holders must outlive the backend or be explicitly released first; consumers must be quiescent during mutation. Reusable runtime code exists, but top-level GameSystem startup binding and embedded error-font handoff are not integrated. Original text GPU rendering and gameplay remain incomplete.
Embedded error font resource validation (2026-10-04): source cErrorArchive is 117460 bytes while its RARC header declares 117440; bounded parsing of the declared range validates the archive and exposes language-specific embeddedfont.arc entries. Fixture loads the US-English nested archive and its MessageFont26 through native ResFont with exact decompressed resource size, then verifies metrics/glyph access and detach. Existing five WBFS campaign font and holder lifecycle tests also pass. This confirms the real embedded resource path; generated createFontFromEmbeddedData still uses the legacy path, and error-to-campaign ownership handoff remains pending. No gameplay or HUD drawing verification is implied.
Native embedded-to-campaign font startup handoff (2026-10-04): generated GameSystemFontHolder::createFontFromEmbeddedData now delegates to NativeGameFontBackend. NativeDiscFontBackend loads language-specific embedded font bytes from bounded cErrorArchive/nested RARC resources, owns the font independently, and keeps original getMessageFont precedence. Campaign loading validates existing embedded ownership, builds all five fonts before publishing, clears the embedded pointer and releases its owned font. WBFS tests pass for repeated embedded-to-campaign transitions, unknown-language failure leaving holder empty, error-only backend teardown, campaign detach/reload and auto-unbind. All 147 providers compile. Default backend language is explicitly UsEnglish; top-level game language/startup binding, original HUD text drawing and playable gameplay remain incomplete.
Original MessageHolder provider integration (2026-10-04): added the complete pinned MessageHolder source to the original runtime compile/link provider set. All 148 providers compile. Link rerun resolves original MessageHolder scene initialization/destruction methods; font-holder methods also remain resolved. Full runtime link still fails on GameSystem services, threading, display, audio and other dependencies. MessageData's legacy BMG/RARC/JMap pointer overlay and native-endian casts remain unsafe for Wii resources and have not executed; existing bounded NativeBmgText/NativeMessageCatalog need a record/ownership bridge before original message parsing can be claimed. No gameplay, message presentation or HUD draw validation occurred.
Native message information record bridge in progress (2026-10-04): added NativeMessageRecords with stable host-layout INF1 headers/records, native message offsets/camera IDs and owned encoded DAT1 storage. Added regeneration-preserved empty MessageData constructor for native record attachment and extracted unchanged original getMessage/getMessageInfoTool kernel. Uses actual /UsEnglish/MessageData/Message.arc from WBFS. Retail BMG core size is 242464 while full resource is 255248 because flow sections follow the declared text core; this bridge follows bounded INF1/DAT1 parsing and leaves FLW/FLI unsupported. Test checks original getter metadata and owned text offsets for all messages. Text bytes remain encoded big-endian UTF16/tag data; original text presentation and dialogue flow are not yet bridged. Full original constructor remains legacy and unexecuted.
Native dialogue flow record milestone (2026-10-04): NativeMessageRecords now owns host-layout FLW1 headers, TalkNode records and branch tables. Node message/branch fields are converted as u16 pairs; event arguments remain host u32. Actual US-English WBFS tests compare all 1316 node fields and 632 branch entries directly against big-endian source bytes, execute unchanged original getNode/getBranchNode/isValidBranchNode methods, and retain the 2464 message-record getter checks; all pass. The trailing FLI1 header declares an extent ending 16 bytes beyond the available resource; it remains unsupported and is not read. Node navigation/controller behavior, branch predicate evaluation, event execution, text/tag presentation and full campaign gameplay remain unverified.
Original message ID lookup bridge (2026-10-04): NativeMessageRecords attaches a native-owned JMapInfo message ID table and exposes it through MessageData. Executed unchanged original findMessageIndex/getMessageDirect using the existing original JMapInfo implementation against every US-English MessageId.tbl row, comparing indices and resulting text offsets to independently decoded table/BMG records; missing-ID lookup rejects correctly. Existing 2464 message, 1316 node and 632 branch checks pass. Test linkage uses generated original JMapInfo and real hash/string providers. Complete MessageData constructor/MessageHolder source binding, text/tag presentation, dialogue navigation/events and full gameplay remain incomplete.
Native MessageHolder archive startup integration (2026-10-04): generated initSystemData/initGameData delegate to an explicitly bound native message backend. Reusable NativeDiscMessageBackend owns bounded system records from embedded US-English System.arc and game records from /UsEnglish/MessageData/Message.arc, publishes their MessageData views and clears owned scene/system/game pointers during explicit release or teardown. Actual WBFS tests execute original holder initialization, scene alias/destroy/reinit and release alongside all record/ID/node/branch comparisons; pass. All 148 runtime providers compile. Backend currently explicitly US-English; other language selection, top-level binding, native UTF16/tag presentation, dialogue-controller/event execution and playable gameplay remain incomplete. The legacy MessageData archive constructor remains unexecuted; native backend uses owned record attachment instead.

Original TalkNodeCtrl navigation now executes against native-owned scene message records through MessageSystem::getSceneMessageData. Generated MessageHolder scene initialization binds the native service; destruction and backend release unbind it. An unavailable scene raises an explicit initialization error. Binding is serial and requires quiescent consumers during scene transitions; top-level GameSystem startup still needs integration. Generator preserves this adaptation and extracts unchanged original navigation methods for verification.

Assertion-enabled WBFS checks pass all 1,316 scene nodes: message/event successors, branch successors, current-node classification, and null-node behavior. Existing 632 branch-entry and 2,464 message-record checks pass. All 148 actor provider compilation probes pass. These checks cover navigation and ownership, not event execution, dialogue rendering, NPC interaction, or playable single-player gameplay.

Dialogue entry lookup verification now runs original MessageData::findNode against every message-ID row from the supplied WBFS and compares the first matching message node to the raw FLW1 data. Missing IDs return no node. Native message loading now rejects out-of-range message records, node successors, event branch indices, branch pairs, unsupported node types and ID-table indices before exposing the message view. Assertion-enabled tests pass the retail data and deliberate corruptions of each dialogue-edge type. This supports original NPC dialogue initialization; camera setup, talk events, native text/tag processing and integrated NPC interaction remain pending.

Original NW4R layout, group and bounding-pane implementations are now compiled and included in the full actor-runtime link probe. All 151 providers compile. The refreshed linker diagnostics no longer report Layout::mspAllocator as missing, resolving the allocator storage used by original layout startup. The runtime DLL still fails to link on remaining layout, audio, controller, game-system, archive and other dependencies. No layout resource is executed or rendered by this change: Wii binary layout resources still require bounded host decoding and native pointer adaptation before use. This is runtime linkage progress, not a playable game or verified original UI.

All 15 original NW4R layout implementation files are now runtime providers (163 total providers compile). Generated host adaptations replace MSL-only C headers with host headers, restore material rounding/TRUE macros, and explicitly reject the TPLPalette raw-Wii overlay entry point. The latter requires a bounded native TPL decoder rather than a 32-bit pointer-address heuristic. This boundary is incomplete functionality, not a texture replacement implementation. Original material arrays, binary layout/animation/ARC resources, text encoding and draw execution still need native validation/adaptation before running their resource paths.

The full link probe confirms previously missing Pane::AppendChild, TextBox::typeInfo, TexMap::Set and AnimResource::Set providers are resolved. It still fails on other SDK/game dependencies; no original layout is rendered and no gameplay claim follows from compilation. Original dialogue-record tests remain separate from this layout compilation milestone.

Original MEMAllocFromAllocator/MEMFreeToAllocator callback dispatch is restored through a source-extracted kernel and included in the full runtime link probe. Original Layout::AllocMemory/FreeMemory inline entry points pass 256 allocate/write/free cycles alternating between two independent host callback contexts, verifying allocator identity, parameters, size and free routing. The fixture supplies allocator storage and host malloc/free callbacks; production retains Layout's original allocator storage and still needs top-level native heap binding. Link diagnostics resolve both dispatch symbols; the full DLL remains incomplete. No material alignment defect was established by the source audit, so no speculative material-layout rewrite was made.

Original JKRHeapAllocator layout callbacks now request alignof(std::max_align_t) in the runtime header instead of Wii/default alignment zero. This keeps original heap allocation/free routing while ensuring host layout object alignment. The generator preserves the override. The live native heap fixture executes original MEMAllocator dispatch into original JKRHeapAllocator callbacks: 128 simultaneous varied-size blocks retain payloads, identify the owning expandable heap, satisfy host alignment and restore the full initial free-space budget. Existing full heap lifecycle/concurrency/worker checks pass. All 163 runtime providers compile with the new header. Production top-level layout allocator selection and UI resource execution remain pending; no playable game claim.

Default MR::NewDeleteAllocator now allocates from the current native JKR game heap with host alignment and frees via owning-heap lookup. Desktop global new/delete is no longer used for this original UI allocator; both full MemoryUtil provider and extracted test kernel retain the adaptation through generation. Live tests verify allocation ownership and payload, switch the current heap before freeing, and confirm capacity recovery in both heaps. Missing current heap returns null, and null free is harmless. The complete heap suite and all 163 provider compilations pass. Original setLayoutDefaultAllocator selects this same allocator, but integrated startup/resource/render verification remains unfinished.

Original NW4R ut_LinkList implementation is now included in the actor-runtime providers (164 compile). Assertion-enabled native execution verifies insertion and traversal order, single/range erase, node clearing and reuse, and destructor unlinking without deleting externally owned nodes. Full link diagnostics no longer contain unresolved LinkListImpl methods, supplying the container operations used by panes, resource accessors and animation lists. The runtime DLL still fails on remaining SDK/game dependencies; no original UI or campaign execution is claimed.

Original NW4R sine/cosine lookup table and interpolation are now native runtime providers (165 providers compile). Generated adaptation uses finite modulo reduction before the original interpolation, preventing repeated-subtraction nontermination for extremely large floats, and rejects non-finite angles explicitly. Tests compare all 8,193 quarter-step signed angles to the original table interpolation and analytic sine/cosine with 0.00008 tolerance, plus huge-angle/wrap/non-finite cases. Full link diagnostics resolve SinFIdx/CosFIdx used by original UI texture transforms. Full runtime linkage and original UI/gameplay execution remain unfinished; Wii instruction rounding parity is not claimed.

Native scalar SDK matrix providers now supply 3x4 identity/copy/scale/translation apply and 4x4 identity/copy. Translation apply follows the pinned SDK assembly's addition to the translation column and preserves the linear matrix. Assertion-enabled tests cover matrix values, transformed vectors, distinct and identical destinations; existing affine composition/alias checks pass. Original nw4r math_types.cpp is also a runtime provider, resolving VEC3TransformNormal (compiled, not separately executed in this fixture). All 166 providers compile, and refreshed full link diagnostics resolve these seven missing matrix symbols. Console paired-single rounding parity and full runtime/UI/gameplay remain unverified.

GXSetNumIndStages now emits the pinned SDK's three-bit NBMP general-mode field via a bounded masked native BP update. All valid counts 0-4 are checked against exact command bytes and decoded state, unrelated general-mode bits remain preserved, and invalid counts fail before emission. Existing original J3D texture state/pixel checks and hardware/WARP readbacks pass. Refreshed full actor link resolves this setter but remains incomplete. Native immediate emission replaces SDK dirty-state scheduling; indirect shader execution and SDK derived-state integration still need verification. Original gameplay remains unfinished.

GXSetNumTexGens and GXSetNumChans now emit masked general-mode updates plus XF count registers 0x103F/0x1009, matching the original SDK dirty-state flush's register values. Native immediate setters preflight the complete 19-byte sequence. Assertion-enabled tests cover every legal texture-generator count (0-8) and color-channel count (0-2), BP/XF agreement, preservation of unrelated general-mode fields and invalid-input rejection before emission. Existing indirect count tests and texture state/pixel hardware/WARP readbacks pass. Full link diagnostics resolve both providers; original draw execution, complete shader behavior and integrated game runtime remain unfinished. Immediate native emission does not reproduce SDK scheduling timing.

GXSetChanCtrl now emits native XF channel-control registers following pinned GXLight.c: material/ambient sources, enable, all eight light bits, diffuse attenuation, and specular/spot/none rules. Combined color/alpha channel IDs update both associated registers, while individual IDs leave other channels untouched. The complete 9/18-byte update is preflighted. Assertion-enabled tests cover 13,824 channel/light/diffuse/attenuation configurations, ignored high light-mask bits, combined/individual routing and invalid-channel rejection before emission. Existing count/state/pixel and hardware/WARP readback checks pass. The full link resolves GXSetChanCtrl but remains incomplete; this verifies command state, not executed lighting shaders or original gameplay.

Native GXSetBlendMode now emits the original blend-enable/subtract/logic/factor fields while preserving dithering, write masks and unrelated BP bits. GXSetColorUpdate/GXSetAlphaUpdate independently update their original write-mask fields. Complete command sequences are preflighted. Assertion-enabled tests pass all 4,096 blend combinations (all modes, factors and logic ops), unrelated state preservation, both write-mask values and invalid-mode rejection before emission. Existing graphics command checks and hardware/WARP texture readbacks pass. Refreshed full link resolves these setters but remains incomplete. These are register-state checks; actual D3D blend/logic behavior and full original UI/gameplay rendering still require integration and verification.

GXLoadTexMtxImm now emits bounded native XF uploads using the pinned SDK primary/post texture-matrix bank addressing and 2x4/3x4 counts. Full command size is preflighted and float bits are preserved. Tests cover both shapes across primary/post bank slots, unchanged adjacent registers and invalid address rejection before emission; existing texture GPU readbacks pass. Full actor linkage resolves this setter but remains incomplete. Renderer audit confirms GxCommandState still rejects draw/indexed commands; the existing mesh renderer is diagnostic and does not consume the original GX draw stream. Original texture transforms, TEV/lighting/blending and draw execution need integrated rendering verification before any playability claim.

Original NW4R detail::DrawQuad now executes in a native fixture through a real GXBegin command-header provider and existing native GX vertex emitters. Native GxUiVertices decoder reads the exact direct XY/F32, optional RGBA8, ST/F32 stream selected by original SetVertexFormat. Tests cover all 18 combinations of colors/no colors and 0-8 UV sets, quad corner ordering (0,1,3,2), positions, colors, UV values and truncated streams. GXBegin validates primitive/header fields and preflights storage; full runtime link resolves it but remains incomplete. Decoder metadata currently comes explicitly from the layout format; CP/VAT dispatch integration, GPU drawing and general game vertex formats remain pending. GxCommandState still rejects draws until a complete transactional draw backend is connected. This milestone decodes original emitted geometry; it is not a displayed original UI or gameplay.

Original layout geometry now reaches a real native GPU draw path. NativeUiColorRenderer consumes decoded original XY/RGBA8 quad vertices, triangulates their emitted order, projects screen coordinates and renders vertex colors to an RGBA8 target. Test executes original detail::DrawQuad, decodes its emitted GX stream, renders on both hardware and WARP, and verifies every pixel of an 8x8 target: exact solid RGBA including alpha and untouched border coverage. All 18 original color/UV emission formats still decode correctly.

This renderer covers untextured vertex-color primitives only. It does not yet load actual layouts, apply texture/TEV/lighting/blend state, or connect GxCommandState to draw dispatch. Original UI and full game startup/campaign remain unfinished. Pixel verification proves the original emitted primitive-to-GPU path, not playable gameplay.

Original emitted layout quads now support native GPU base-level texture sampling with the first emitted UV set and vertex-color modulation. Renderer validates texture extents and handles point/linear min/mag filters and clamp/repeat/mirror wrapping; mipmapped sampling and full TEV remain pending. The test executes original DrawQuad with UVs and tint, decodes the command bytes, draws a four-quadrant texture, and verifies orientation, channel modulation and alpha on both hardware and WARP. Existing untextured exact-pixel checks and all 18 emission/decode formats pass. Actual layout/texture resource binding, CP/VAT dispatch, multi-texture TEV and integrated gameplay remain unfinished. The supported explicit rendering path is not yet wired into full GX command consumption.

Actual supplied-game font glyphs now reach the original-quad native GPU path. NativeFontGlyphRegion selects atlas/sheet coordinates and character width from the bounded RFNT decoder and validates cell/texture extents. The fixture mounts the WBFS Font.arc, decodes menufont64.brfnt, releases the archive, executes original DrawQuad for A and ?, decodes its emitted stream and draws the selected atlas regions on hardware and WARP. Every glyph pixel matches the decoded sheet exactly with point sampling. Existing color/texture quad checks pass. This verifies actual font-resource-to-GPU pixels, not original text-writer layout, strings, message tags, blending or integrated dialogue/HUD. Those and full campaign gameplay remain unfinished.

Original NW4R CharWriter, TagProcessorBase and TextWriterBase sources are now included unchanged in the native actor/runtime provider set. All 169 providers compile successfully on x86_64 Windows. Refreshed full runtime link still fails on remaining dependencies. This is provider integration only: original text layout, glyph emission, message tags and integrated dialogue/HUD have not been executed or verified by this change. Existing actual-font quad GPU tests do not establish original text-writer execution or playable gameplay.

Original CustomTagProcessor, ReplaceTagProcessor and MessageTagSkipTagProcessor are now included unchanged in the native runtime provider set. All 172 providers compile; refreshed full link still fails. TextWriterBase::PrintImpl for char and wchar_t has only a declaration in the pinned source/header, so adding ut_TextWriterBase.cpp does not restore its missing body. A faithful native implementation is still needed. Game-specific tag providers now supply their available original methods, but actual message execution remains unverified; Wii BMG UTF16/tag bytes must be converted into correctly bounded host strings before these original processors consume them. No dialogue, HUD or playable gameplay claim follows from compilation.

Original character stream compatibility checks now execute unchanged CharStrmReader with Windows UTF16 wchar_t, UTF8 multibyte characters, SJIS encoded glyph identifiers and CP1252 byte codes. Assertion-enabled test verifies returned codes and cursor advancement, including terminators; all pass. Windows wchar_t is confirmed two bytes for this target. This validates valid host strings only; reader has no input length parameter and is not a bounded decoder for malformed/truncated external strings. Bounded BMG-to-host conversion and missing PrintImpl reconstruction remain necessary before original dialogue printing. No integrated text rendering or gameplay is verified.

NativeBmgText now exposes bounded message tokens, decoding big-endian UTF16 text while retaining inline tag payloads as original bytes. Tag sizes must be even and at least six bytes, fit DAT1 bounds and reach a message terminator. Actual supplied WBFS checks parse all 2464 messages: 94479 tokens including 3454 inline tags. Invalid zero/short/odd tag sizes are rejected; existing original message/node/lifecycle tests pass. Typed tag endianness conversion and host strings are not yet implemented: original tag accessors combine byte fields and native multi-byte fields, so blindly swapping every UTF16 word would corrupt tags. Original dialogue drawing and gameplay remain unfinished.

Native BMG tokens now provide validated group/id and bounded 8/16/32-bit parameter access with explicit big-endian decoding. Tests verify mixed-byte known vectors, out-of-range and maximum-size_t index rejection, plus every group/id/byte parameter in all 3454 actual game tags. Existing 2464-message and dialogue navigation checks pass. Original CustomTagProcessor also writes host pointers into inline string-argument payloads; its Wii-sized tag allocation cannot safely hold 64-bit pointers without a separate native argument representation. Typed access is implemented, but original processor adaptation, host message strings, PrintImpl and integrated rendering remain pending. The port is not playable.

NativeMessageArguments owns per-message numeric and string replacements in host storage keyed by the original group-6/group-7 argument index. Strings are copied into owned std::wstring values; no 64-bit pointer is written into Wii tag bytes. Assertion-enabled checks pass source-string lifetime, replacement, negative values, clearing/missing values and unchanged encoded tags, alongside all actual BMG/flow checks. This component is not yet bound to CustomTagProcessor; its unsafe legacy writes remain in the compiled original provider and must be replaced before execution. Returned string references require the argument entry to remain alive/unmodified. Full message printing and gameplay remain incomplete.

Generated CustomTagProcessor now stores replacement numbers and copied strings in per-processor native maps keyed by original tag identity, retaining original setter tag selection/occurrence limits. Setters no longer write values or 64-bit pointers into encoded tag payloads; handlers read owned entries and unassigned string tags emit no replacement. Added explicit clearNativeArguments for message-buffer invalidation. All 172 providers compile; full link remains incomplete. Existing owned-argument/BMG tests pass, but the adapted processor itself has not executed; these tests do not establish its runtime integration. Maps must be cleared before replacing/reusing text buffers and tag fields still require correct host representation. Also bounded number formatting to the actual local buffer capacity; original format switch fallthrough remains to be reconciled. Missing PrintImpl, dialogue drawing and original gameplay remain unfinished.

Correction to earlier PrintImpl notes: the original wchar_t specialization is implemented in Game/Screen/CustomTagProcessor.cpp and has resolved since that provider was added. Only the char specialization was missing. Generated NarrowTextWriter now reuses the original wide specialization's alignment, wrapping, spacing, baseline and tag-operation control flow, removing game-specific wide-only alpha/controller hooks. It is included in the full link probe; refreshed diagnostics no longer report either PrintImpl specialization as missing. Full link still fails on other providers. This is source-derived native adaptation, not proven console parity; neither original wide printing nor the new narrow variant has executed in a rendering test. Narrow encoded-stream bounds, custom tag processor type checks, message buffer invalidation and full dialogue/gameplay integration remain pending.

Narrow PrintImpl now executes against real MenuFont64 metrics with a recording CharWriter sink. Assertion-enabled tests verify emitted character sequence/positions, character spacing, baseline-relative newline movement, final cursor and width-limit wrapping. Test-only original template sources omit unused wchar_t instantiations without altering method bodies. A test exposed that the source-derived print method returns the final line width for the left-aligned multiline fixture, because original newline processing resets cursor before prior-line width collection; this observed behavior is retained pending console comparison. The recording sink does not execute original CharWriter glyph emission, GX texture binding or GPU drawing. Actual text rendering, full tag/lifecycle integration and playable original campaign remain unfinished.

Original CharWriter::PrintGlyph vertex emission now executes through native GXBegin/GX vertex writes into captured command storage. Native decodeFontQuad supports its exact 83-byte XYZ/F32, RGBA8, ST/U16 format with 15 fractional UV bits; non-finite positions, wrong headers/extents and nonzero depth are rejected until projected glyph integration. Actual MenuFont64 A glyph emission is decoded and rendered on hardware/WARP; every output pixel matches the bounded decoded font sheet. Existing quad/font GPU tests pass. Fixture uses a texture-binding recorder and a minimal test CharWriter constructor; production GX texture-object loading, complete original CharWriter lifecycle, TEV/blending and command dispatch are not integrated. This verifies original glyph geometry-to-GPU pixels, not whole strings/dialogue/HUD or playable gameplay.

Native GXInitTexObjLOD now adapts the original SDK implementation with a fixed 32-byte texture-object representation, copying fields rather than overlaying a host struct containing 64-bit userData. Corrected native FAST_GPFLAGSET: the original header expands this macro to nothing outside its console compiler. Tests pass all six minification filters, magnification, LOD clamps, image/metadata preservation and invalid/non-finite input rejection before mutation. Full link probe resolves GXInitTexObjLOD; it still fails on other dependencies. GXInitTexObj/GXLoadTexObj, texture address registration and original font texture binding remain unfinished. LOD object field verification does not prove GPU mipmap sampling or playable gameplay.

Native GXInitTexObj now reuses original SDK texture-object initialization with fixed-width storage and registered native GPU addresses instead of truncated host addresses. Validates dimensions/wrap/mipmap, supported formats and complete encoded base/mip storage in the registry before mutating the object. Assertion-enabled tests pass dimensions/format/wrap bits, stable resource address, mip LOD defaults, insufficient-resource rejection and unchanged destination on rejection; existing LOD tests pass. Full link resolves GXInitTexObj but still fails elsewhere. Caller must register encoded texture storage before initialization and maintain registry lifetime through draws. GXLoadTexObj, native font sheet registration and production draw dispatch remain unimplemented; no texture binding or gameplay completion is claimed.

Native GXLoadTexObj now emits complete BP mask plus sampler mode0/mode1, image dimensions/format and registered resource-address commands for all eight texture slots. It validates live complete encoded image/mip storage before emission. Tests consume each emitted command sequence with GxCommandState and verify slot/dimensions/format/address; removed resources reject loads with zero emitted bytes. Existing object initialization and LOD tests pass. Full link resolves GXLoadTexObj but remains incomplete. Native backend omits Wii TMEM-region scheduling and currently rejects indexed textures until TLUT integration; font-owned encoded sheets still need registration and lifetime wiring. This validates texture binding commands, not full draw dispatch, original dialogue or playable gameplay.

ResFont attachment now registers the owned encoded font-sheet range in the native GPU registry; removing the font unregisters it before releasing sheet storage. GPU resource addresses remain usable after source archive/input release and are invalid after font detach. Font-owner storage initializes after the registry so shutdown unregisters fonts before registry destruction. Tests pass actual MenuFont64 registration/removal, all five WBFS campaign fonts and archive-owner release, embedded/campaign font handoff/lifecycle and narrow text layout. Full runtime link still fails elsewhere. Registry access remains serial and consumers must be quiescent before font mutation. Original CharWriter still needs execution through production texture binding and full draw dispatch; dialogue/HUD/gameplay remain unfinished.

Original CharWriter::LoadTexture now executes in the glyph GPU fixture, replacing the test texture-binding recorder. Actual WBFS MenuFont64 loads through native ResFont with registered encoded sheets; unchanged LoadTexture calls native GXInitTexObj/GXInitTexObjLOD/GXLoadTexObj. The 25-byte texture command prefix is consumed into GxCommandState and its bound sheet is decoded from the GPU registry; original PrintGlyph's 83-byte geometry follows and renders with exact expected glyph pixels on hardware/WARP. Existing quad/font GPU checks pass. Minimal fixture constructor remains; full original CharWriter lifecycle/setup, texture-cache invalidation during font teardown, blending/TEV and transactional draw dispatch are not yet integrated. This verifies original glyph loading/emission to native GPU pixels, not strings/dialogue/HUD or playable campaign.

Original CharWriter texture caching now observes the native GPU registry generation. Successful resource registration/removal advances the generation; LoadTexture resets its original pointer/filter cache before reuse when that generation changes. The full CharWriter provider and glyph test kernel share this generated adaptation. Tests prove unchanged-resource cache hits emit only 83-byte glyph geometry, while registration and removal each force the 25-byte texture rebind prefix; hardware/WARP glyph pixels and GPU registry tests pass. All 172 runtime providers compile. Invalidation is conservative for any resource lifetime change, serial registry access is unchanged, and cache coherence with arbitrary external texture-state changes still depends on original SetupGX/ResetTextureCache. Full draw dispatch, text/HUD and gameplay remain incomplete.

Glyph GPU fixture now executes original CharWriter constructor/destructor, filtering, UpdateVertexColor and Print alongside original LoadTexture/PrintGlyph. Tests cover alpha modulation, actual glyph left bearing and cursor advance; existing font cache and hardware/WARP pixel tests pass. Testing exposed native-endian Color aliasing: original packed-u32/channel overlay applies alpha to the wrong channel on little-endian Windows. Generated native Color now explicitly packs/unpacks RGBA channels and copies GXColor fields. ToU32ref returns a packed value rather than an aliasing reference; repository search found no external users of that reference API. Fixture and full actor probe force-include the override for consistent translation-unit behavior. Full original GX setup, dialogue strings, draw dispatch and playable gameplay remain unfinished.

Completed color-adapted provider rebuild: all 172 providers compile; refreshed full runtime link still fails on unresolved dependencies. GxCommandState now decodes sampler wrap and min/mag filter settings, converting hardware minification codes into GX filter enums and rejecting reserved fields. decodeCommandTexture carries these settings into TextureImage rather than returning default sampler values. Tests cover all six minification filters, magnification and all eight loaded texture slots; original J3D texture commands and hardware/WARP readback, glyph GPU/lifecycle/cache checks and texture-object tests pass. The UI renderer still rejects mipmapped filters until native mip-chain sampling is implemented. Full TEV/blending, transactional draw dispatch, dialogue/HUD and gameplay remain unfinished.

Native font command-stream parser now accepts original BP texture state, zero-padding and exact CharWriter direct-format glyph quads, producing owned draw records with decoded bound textures. Graphics state commits only after the complete stream validates; malformed opcodes/truncated draws roll back prior state. Repeated texture states share decoded sheet ownership, with explicit 64MiB input, 4096-draw and 16-million decoded-pixel budgets. Actual original bound/cached glyph streams and malformed-stream rollback tests pass alongside hardware/WARP glyph pixels/lifecycle/cache tests. Vertex format is supplied by the original CharWriter contract; general CP/VAT draw decoding, full command dispatch, projected depth, TEV/blending and integrated dialogue/gameplay remain unfinished.

NativeUiColorRenderer now renders a bounded batch of up to 4096 quads into one D3D target, clearing only once and preserving draw order; the single-quad API delegates to this path. Original CharWriter emits two actual font characters with original left bearings/advances, the transactional font stream shares their decoded texture, and both draws render together. Every target pixel, including overlapping glyph rectangles and untouched background, matches the expected painter-order atlas pixels on hardware/WARP. Existing single-quad/glyph/cache/lifecycle checks pass. This currently uses overwrite rendering without GX alpha blending/TEV; complete original text strings, dialogue/HUD layout and playable gameplay remain unfinished.

Native UI batches now optionally use GX's standard source-alpha/inverse-source-alpha equation for color and alpha through a D3D blend state, preserving prior draw contents.
