### Requested audio output menu
Provide selectable Stereo (2.0) and 5.1 Surround output in the eventual settings menu. Stereo is the default and fallback for unsupported devices. The 5.1 option must select a six-channel host output when available; if the game's source is stereo/Pro Logic II encoded, decode or upmix it explicitly and do not describe that as original discrete surround. Show the effective mode after fallback. Verify channel routing and device/backend capability with actual output tests. This is a saved requirement; the game audio runtime and menu are not implemented yet.

Audio implementation plan and current DSP dependencies are tracked in [`AUDIO-DSP-ROADMAP.md`](AUDIO-DSP-ROADMAP.md). The included `jdsp` image hashes to `0xD643001F`, which matches Dolphin's SMG Zelda-UCode entry; `test-dsp-ucode-hash.ps1` verifies the source image and reference table. This identifies an HLE target but does not execute the microcode or produce audible PCM.

### Native DSP address bridge foundation (2026-10-11)
Added `Native::DspAddressSpace` to map host memory spans to stable, aligned 32-bit guest addresses for Wii DSP command payloads. It validates range bounds, prevents overlapping mappings, reserves a guard gap so one-past-end frame pointers cannot alias the next range, supports interior pointer translation and checked resolution, and synchronizes registration for audio/render worker use. Host-generated copies of the original JASAiCtrl and JASDSPInterface providers now translate frame-buffer and DSP table pointers through this shared registry, and both providers compile. This is still only producer-side address assignment: DSP mailbox/firmware execution is not implemented, the audio thread has no real PCM output backend, and there is no audible playback or game boot verification.


### Native original display configuration
DisplayConfig provides original SC aspect/progressive/EuRGB60 and VI TV-format/digital-output queries. Defaults NTSC, widescreen, progressive digital presentation. Configure at startup while original render-mode selection is idle; individual queries are synchronized, but a multi-query selection is not an atomic configuration snapshot. These compatibility fields do not select host window resolution or implement VI scanout. Original unchanged RenderMode.cpp passes all 64 supported combinations; invalid TV-format updates reject before changing state. Runtime link includes the provider; complete boot remains unfinished.


### Multiplayer menu and browser Download Play
The requested Host/Join menu, P2P/LAN modes, browser launch, shareable join URL, Luigi's independent view, player-1 event teleport, and transfer-progress loading screen are recorded in MULTIPLAYER-REQUIREMENTS.md. All are pending requirements; original single-player runtime remains the priority. Browser download transfer is limited to explicitly redistributable assets: the host must not transmit the commercial game's proprietary files to a guest without them. Internet join URLs also require reachable signaling/NAT traversal or a relay; a private LAN IP alone cannot serve an internet guest.

### Native host OS alarm adapter (2026-10-10)
Added a host scheduler for original OSCreateAlarm, OSSetAlarm, OSSetPeriodicAlarm, OSCancelAlarm, and alarm tag/user-data APIs. Callbacks run asynchronously on a host scheduler thread with a null OSContext; this is a host service approximation, not Wii decrementer-interrupt behavior. Cancellation synchronizes with in-flight callbacks; periodic alarms skip missed periods while retaining their original phase. One-shot, periodic, reschedule/cancel, user-data/tag, and in-flight cancellation tests pass in test-os-alarm-port.ps1. The original runtime link probe resolves all three OS alarm symbols. Including the existing adapted DrawSyncManager implementation also resolved its class methods but surfaced four still-missing GX GPU-status/breakpoint/abort services.

### Expanded game-system provider probe (2026-10-10)
Added original game-system, scene-initialization, language/audio-wrapper, message, and scenario-layout translation units to the provider probe. Corrected its missing RVLFaceLib include path; the original NameObjFactory.cpp now compiles successfully and the full probe records 191 compiled units. The refreshed original-runtime link still fails with 1,411 undefined symbols, including GX GPU-status services, layout/message helpers, and Wii audio/DSP and WPAD services. The higher count follows linking additional original implementations and their dependencies; it is not a successfully linked game. This library is never executed: normal game startup and full campaign verification remain absent, so the project is not playable.

# Runtime link checkpoint — 2026-10-11

The latest native link probe now generates the original `StoryEventBCSV` and `GalaxyIDBCSV` data symbols from Petari's 480-byte and 3,360-byte BCSV files through the existing host-endian JMap reader. It also includes native language and GX depth-peek providers, WBFS-backed DVD init/status functions, and a host-adapted GX initializer generated from Petari's original default-state setup. The initializer directs commands into the host GDL buffer and avoids Wii MMIO FIFO access; the original GX default command body is retained. Native support now includes host FIFO state queries, vector vertex-format setup, TLUT-region encoding, texture-object format/mipmap reads, texture-region callbacks, progressive field state, bounding-box clear, PE poke-state commands, and D3D-backed `GXPeekARGB`; focused GX/EFB tests pass.

The fresh link-only audit reports 1,359 providers and zero duplicate definitions. It resolves `GXInit`, `GXGetCPUFifo`, `GXGetGPFifo`, `GXGetFifoPtrs`, `GXSetMisc`, `GXGetGPStatus`, `GXAbortFrame`, `__GXSetGenMode`, GX metric selectors/read fallback, texture-object format/mipmap reads, TLUT-region setup, vector vertex-format setup, default texture callbacks, progressive field state, common PE poke operations and `GXPeekARGB`. Host FIFO-idle reporting follows the native finish fence, and EFB pixel readback samples the active D3D target with Wii alpha-read behavior. Native FIFO-breakpoint hints are inert; draw-sync completes at decoded token positions. The alarm abort path signals cancellation without using D3D on the alarm thread, serializes recovery submission, and waits for the recovery fence/reset acknowledgement. Host XF/RAS performance-counter reads intentionally return zero because D3D11 has no direct fixed-function equivalent. The audit has 553 undefined references / 551 unique symbols, one fewer of each than the previous run. Audio/DSP and broad runtime dependencies still prevent linking; no game executable is produced. All nine D3D scripts, GX submission/service tests, and OS alarm tests pass; the D3D abort/recovery test also passed three repeated runs. ### ARAM-to-decompressor worker integration (2026-10-11)

## Native DVD read cancellation (2026-10-11)

Implemented host `DVDCancel` for asynchronous file reads. Reads now stage bytes until the worker completes; cancellation reports the Wii SDK canceled result (`-3`), leaves the caller buffer unchanged, and waits for callback/job cleanup before returning. Normal async completion still copies the requested bytes and restores `DVDCommandBlock::addr` to the caller's destination. `test-native-dvd-cancel.ps1` passes normal asynchronous read/callback/close and active cancellation against the supplied WBFS, including single callback, canceled state and unchanged destination checks. The refreshed full link-only audit resolves `DVDCancel` and reports 1,490 providers, 308 unique unresolved symbols, and zero duplicates; it still exits 1 without producing a game executable. No boot or gameplay is verified; end-to-end completion remains 0%.

## Host DVD cancel and Wii power/reset services (2026-10-11)

Added a cancellable native async DVD read path: data is staged until the worker's completion boundary, an active `DVDCancel` reports the original `-3` result once, leaves the caller buffer unchanged, and synchronizes with the callback before close/cancel returns. Normal async reads retain exact byte output and caller buffer identity. `test-native-dvd-cancel.ps1` passes both completion and active-cancel paths against the supplied WBFS.

Added explicit PC-side implementations for `OSSetPowerCallback`, `OSGetResetButtonState`, `OSRestart`, `OSReturnToMenu`, `OSRebootSystem`, and `OSShutdownSystem`. `test-native-os-power.ps1` passes callback lifecycle and each deferred action request. The port currently has no native game-window/launcher consumer for those deferred actions, so restart, returning to a menu, reboot, and shutdown are not yet end-to-end behaviors. Also repaired the speaker-provider build cache/warning handling, generated JUTException header isolation, and the Mario side-step/warp UTF-8 source transform so the complete audit runs reproducibly.

The newest full link-only audit reports 1,503 providers, 253 undefined occurrences / 252 unique unresolved symbols, and zero duplicate definitions. End-to-end project completion remains 0%.

FaceLib formatting and audible game audio are not runtime-tested; end-to-end project completion remains 0%.

The DSP ROM boot HLE validates the original task-upload mail ordering, bounded guest-memory range, exact Petari uCode hash, and Zelda startup mails. `native\test-dsp-rom-mailbox.ps1` exercises it through the SDK mailbox functions, and `native\test-dsp-boot-hle.ps1` verifies the host `DspBoot` provider sets DSP-running state only after the exact handshake. Replaying the saved actor-runtime link response with the new HLE, `DspBoot`, and generated uCode sources produces a link-only DLL. Zelda audio command processing remains unimplemented, and the DLL is not the game executable; normal boot and gameplay remain unverified.

## Native runtime probe progress (2026-10-12)

The instrumented executable probe using the supplied WBFS now passes embedded `ErrorMessageWindow` BRLYT parsing/initialization and reaches the original game frame loop. The work added a strong host `PSMTXRotRad` implementation (X/Y/Z, case-insensitive axes, safe invalid-axis handling), `JKRHeap` allocation/free for cloned heap-bound callbacks, and an audio-thread startup handshake that waits for DSP buffer initialization. Focused affine and callback-functor tests pass when compiled and run from a temporary output directory; the normal scripts cannot write their logs under `native/build` in this environment. The diagnostic wrapper then stops at `GXCopyDisp requires active native render state`: it has only a no-op GX command sink and lacks the real renderer/display-copy binding. It also reports one rejected Wii AI DMA guest range. This is not a successful game-rendering or playable-game test. See [AUDIO-DSP-ROADMAP.md](AUDIO-DSP-ROADMAP.md) for the full probe boundary and DSP limitations. Mouse-to-Wii-pointer and keyboard/controller shake binding plans remain in [INPUT-ROADMAP.md](INPUT-ROADMAP.md); multiplayer behavior remains as previously specified in this README. Overall verified end-to-end project completion remains **0%**; the native game is not yet playable.

Renderer integration follow-up (2026-10-12): `test-display-copy-emit` passes from a temporary output path, covering copy-register emission, destination address, clear-state restoration, and atomic buffer-overflow rejection. Source tracing shows `MainLoopFramework::endGX` flushes and resets the GX command list before its XFB copy, so a real launch must retain GX register state across submissions, own/register the video XFB buffers, and keep the D3D EFB/command submitter alive through each game frame. The probe's no-op command sink supplies none of those services. The next integration step is a real native video/render bootstrap, not suppressing the `GXCopyDisp` guard. This component test does not change the unplayable status or the 0% verified end-to-end completion.

Progressive XFB copy milestone (2026-10-12): Added `NativeD3dXfbCopy` for progressive, unscaled, gamma-1.0 GX display copies. It reads the D3D EFB, applies the configured seven-tap vertical filter, encodes packed YUYV 4:2:2, writes the registered XFB range while preserving 32-byte row padding, and applies requested EFB clear state. `NativeD3dCommandSubmitter` now selects this path for display copies by default and keeps the RGB565 helper for supported texture copies. The new `test-d3d-xfb-copy.ps1` passes both direct pixel/stride checks and a full GX display-copy submission through a D3D WARP context; `d3d-render-stream-tests` also passes. Scaling, interlace, and non-1.0 gamma are explicitly rejected pending implementation. The native game bootstrap still does not create/bind this submitter, own/register its XFB buffers, or present to a real window, so this is a renderer component milestone, not game boot/playability. Overall verified end-to-end project completion remains 0%.

Renderer/XFB frame bridge checks (2026-10-12): `test-xfb-resource-registry.ps1` passes registration, refresh, buffer replacement, and preservation of externally owned XFB memory. `test-d3d-xfb-copy.ps1` passes direct progressive YUYV output and full GX display-copy submission through the WARP D3D context. Regenerated `GameSystem.cpp` compiles with the production frame-loop XFB registry and display-copy observer binding. These checks validate the pieces at the GX-to-XFB boundary; the engine still has no verified host window/presentation bootstrap and the retail game has not completed a native boot or rendered gameplay. End-to-end completion remains 0%.

Native XFB presentation milestone (2026-10-12): Added `NativeD3dXfbPresenter`, which decodes the progressive GX YUYV 4:2:2 buffer to RGB, uploads it to D3D11, aspect-fits it into a resizable Win32 swap chain, and presents with DXGI. `test-d3d-xfb-present.ps1` verifies known YUYV colors in the D3D backbuffer and a WARP `Present`. `NativeD3dCommandSubmitter` now offers an optional callback carrying each completed XFB copy and its registered bytes, allowing a host to connect this presenter. GX submission plus the callback is tested through WARP. The engine launcher still does not create the game window/device or install this callback/submitter, and no native boot or playable frame from the retail game has been verified; overall end-to-end completion remains 0%.
